# Changelog

All notable changes and decisions for **Antigravity** are recorded here, per session.

The format is loosely based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/).

## [Unreleased]

> 🏁 **Milestone — 2026-08-04, stable development checkpoint.** Commit `14338ca` on `develop` (pushed, in sync with `origin/develop`). Everything through **Delegation** is shipped, tested, and live-verified: Core Workspace Modules Phase 3 in full (Calendar & Scheduling, Attendance, Roles & Permissions Management, Delegation), plus the Jitsi local-dev protocol fix and the Docker/npm/pnpm storage relocation off `C:`. Full monorepo `typecheck`/`test` (398 backend + 9 frontend)/`build` all clean, working tree clean. See `CONTEXT.md`'s status table for the same marker. The reference point before starting **Leave Management** next.

### Session 1 — 2026-07-14 — Discovery & Architecture

**Phase:** Requirements gathering + architecture design (no application code yet).

#### Decisions locked
- **UI paradigm:** Full server-driven UI over a **controlled, versioned primitive set** (not unbounded UI).
- **Multi-tenancy:** Shared Postgres database + `tenant_id` + **Row-Level Security** (defense in depth with app-layer scoping).
- **Build sequencing:** Thin vertical slice first (one industry, auth → workspace → 2 modules end to end).
- **Context:** Commercial startup, solo developer, no hard deadline.
- **Stack:** TypeScript everywhere — Next.js (FE) · NestJS (API) · Supabase Postgres + Prisma · shared Zod schemas.
- **Auth:** Supabase Auth (JWT feeds RLS); RBAC/permissions owned in our DB.
- **AI (MVP):** RAG Q&A over workspace data + report/content generation (no autonomous actions); pgvector + latest Claude models.
- **Onboarding:** Self-serve signup → create company → pick industry template → workspace auto-generated.
- **Billing:** Entitlement/feature-flag system now; Stripe integration deferred (seam in place).
- **Compliance:** SOC 2 / GDPR-ready design now; certification deferred.
- **Notifications:** In-app + email, poll-based; Supabase Realtime deferred.

#### Core engine design approved (ARCHITECTURE.md §6–7)
- **Configuration Engine:** four-layer model (Blueprint → Tenant Config → Entitlements → Identity/Permissions) compiling a per-user **Workspace Manifest**.
- **Override semantics:** **stable-id structured overrides** (add/remove/patch), not deep merge.
- **Permission model:** server-side **Permission Resolver**; manifest is **pruned, not hidden**.
- **SDUI Renderer:** closed versioned primitive registry; **composite widgets** as the escape hatch for rich module screens; Zod validation + per-node error boundaries + graceful unknown-node fallback.
- **Security invariants:** bindings/actions are named, server-authorized references (no raw queries client-side); tenant + data-scope enforced on every data source.

#### Artifacts created
- `ARCHITECTURE.md` — full v1 (16 sections). **Approved in full.**
- `CHANGELOG.md` — this file.
- `CONTEXT.md` — living project-state file (read first each session).

#### Approvals
- **ARCHITECTURE.md v1 — fully approved** (2026-07-14).
- **CHANGELOG.md — approved/active.**

#### Next up
- Break down **Phase 1** into an ordered, buildable task list.
- Begin repo scaffolding once the Phase 1 plan is approved.
- Confirm open items in `CONTEXT.md` §7 (org hierarchy model; first vertical-slice industry = IT).

### Session 2 — 2026-07-14 — Phase 1 Planning & Stage 0 Scaffold

**Phase:** Phase 1 execution begins.

#### Decisions locked
- **Org hierarchy:** confirmed hierarchical `departments` (`parent_id`) + `teams` nested under departments.
- **First vertical-slice industry:** confirmed **IT**.
- **Phase 1 implementation plan approved** (`C:\Users\deepm\.claude\plans\abundant-dancing-stardust.md`): monorepo layout (pnpm workspaces, no Turborepo yet), 12 ordered build stages, Prisma schema sketch, first IT blueprint JSON fixture, and 8 concrete resolutions for gaps ARCHITECTURE.md left abstract (notably: RLS must be keyed on a `current_setting('app.tenant_id')` session GUC set per-request by `TenantPrismaService`, not `auth.jwt()`, since Prisma bypasses Supabase's Data API).

#### Stage 0 — Repo scaffold (complete)
- Monorepo created at `d:\Purnit`: `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, root `package.json`/`tsconfig.base.json`, `.env.example`, `.gitignore`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.
- `apps/web` — Next.js 15 / React 19 (App Router, TS), minimal home page.
- `apps/api` — NestJS 11 (hand-scaffolded, not via interactive CLI), `GET /health` verified live.
- `packages/manifest-schema` — placeholder package (`@antigravity/manifest-schema`), wired as a `workspace:*` dependency of both apps; real Zod contracts land in Stage 5.
- Tooling note: pnpm was not preinstalled and Corepack lacked permission to write to `C:\Program Files`; installed pnpm via `npm install -g pnpm` into the user-writable npm prefix instead — no elevation required.
- Verified: `pnpm -r run build` and `pnpm run typecheck` pass clean across all 3 packages; both dev servers manually smoke-tested (API `/health` → `200 {"status":"ok"}`, web homepage → 200, renders "Antigravity") then stopped.

#### Environment setup issues found and fixed
- Real secrets were pasted into `.env.example` (a file meant to be committed) instead of only `.env`. Restored `.env.example` to placeholders; real values live only in the gitignored `.env`.
- `DATABASE_URL` initially pointed at Prisma Postgres (`pooled.db.prisma.io`), a different hosted database from the Supabase project — would have broken the RLS/Auth-Hook design, which requires the app's tables and Supabase Auth to share one Postgres instance. User confirmed intent to use Supabase's own Postgres; corrected.
- DB password contained an unencoded `#`, the URL-fragment delimiter — silently truncates in connection-string parsers. Percent-encoded to `%23`.
- User rotated the exposed Supabase service-role key as a precaution (it had briefly appeared in a template file and this conversation).

#### Stage 1 — DB schema + RLS (complete)
- Prisma 7.8.0 installed. Prisma 7 restructured config significantly from prior versions: connection URLs now live in `prisma.config.ts` (via `defineConfig`), not the schema's `datasource` block, and `directUrl` was removed entirely from both — the CLI (migrate/studio) reads one `datasource.url` from config, while the *running app* is expected to instantiate its own `PrismaClient` with an explicit driver adapter (`@prisma/adapter-pg`) rather than a `datasourceUrl` shorthand (which no longer exists). `prisma.config.ts` is wired to `DIRECT_URL` (elevated CLI role); the app will use `DATABASE_URL` (restricted role) via `PrismaPg` in Stage 2's `TenantPrismaService`.
- Multi-file Prisma schema (`apps/api/prisma/schema/*.prisma`, no preview flag needed — folder-based schemas are stable in v7) for all 11 Phase 1 tables: `Tenant`, `User`, `Department`, `Team`, `Role`, `RoleAssignment`, `Blueprint`, `TenantConfig`, `Plan`, `FeatureFlag`, `Notification`, `AuditLog`, `Project`, `Task`.
- Created a restricted `app_runtime` Postgres role (`NOBYPASSRLS`) distinct from the elevated migration role — RLS is decorative if the app connects as a role that can bypass it. Password generated securely and set out-of-band via `prisma db execute`, never written to a committed file.
- Enabled + forced RLS on every tenant-scoped table, policy keyed on session GUC `current_setting('app.tenant_id', true)`, **not** `auth.jwt()`, since Prisma's direct Postgres connection bypasses Supabase's Data API (per the plan's gap-resolution #1).
- Found and fixed a real edge case during verification: on a pooled connection that has previously set the GUC, reading it unset later returns `''` (empty string) rather than `NULL`, and casting `''::uuid` throws instead of failing closed. Fixed with `NULLIF(current_setting(...), '')::uuid` so the policy always degrades to "0 rows," never a hard error.
- Also discovered Supabase's Supavisor pooler requires the connection username suffixed with the project ref (`app_runtime.<ref>`) even for non-default roles, or it rejects the connection with `ENOIDENTIFIER`.
- Partial unique index added via raw SQL (`tenant_configs_one_active_per_tenant`) — Prisma's schema DSL has no filtered/partial unique constraint.
- **Verified live against Supabase** with a temporary script (written, run, then deleted — not part of the permanent codebase): same-tenant reads visible, cross-tenant reads return 0 rows, cross-tenant inserts rejected by `WITH CHECK`, and no-session reads fail closed. All passed.
- One destructive step (`prisma migrate reset --force`, needed to cleanly reapply the corrected migration) was blocked by Prisma's own AI-agent safety guard and required explicit user confirmation before running — obtained and executed correctly.
- `apps/api/generated/` (Prisma Client output) added to `.gitignore`; `postinstall: prisma generate` wired into `apps/api/package.json` so a fresh install always regenerates it.
- Full workspace `typecheck` + `build` still pass across all packages.

#### Stage 2 — Auth & tenancy (complete)
- **`TenantContextService`** (`apps/api/src/tenancy/tenant-context.service.ts`) — `AsyncLocalStorage`-based request context (`tenantId`, `authUserId`, `permissionsHash`).
- **`TenantPrismaService`** — instantiates `PrismaClient` via `@prisma/adapter-pg` + `DATABASE_URL` (restricted `app_runtime` role); `run(tenantId, fn)` opens a short transaction per call and sets `app.tenant_id` before executing, matching Stage 1's RLS design; `.root` exposes the same client unscoped for platform-root tables (tenants, blueprints, plans).
- **`AuthContextMiddleware`** — verifies the bearer token (if present) via JWKS and soft-populates the ALS context for the rest of the request; **`JwtAuthGuard`** hard-enforces it on protected routes.
- **`JwtVerifierService`** — JWT verification via `jose`'s `createRemoteJWKSet` against `${SUPABASE_URL}/auth/v1/.well-known/jwks.json`, not a shared HS256 secret — confirmed necessary: this project's tokens are signed **ES256** (Supabase's current default for new projects), so a legacy-shared-secret approach would not have worked.
- **`POST /auth/signup`** — Zod-validated input; creates the Supabase auth user via the Admin API; in one Postgres transaction creates `Tenant`, materializes `Role` rows from the seeded IT blueprint, creates `User`, links an admin `RoleAssignment`, and seeds an empty active `TenantConfig` (L2). Rolls back the Supabase auth user if the transaction fails — signup is all-or-nothing across both systems.
- **`GET /me`** (guarded) — the Stage 2 verification target; returns the current session's user, scoped through `TenantPrismaService`/RLS.
- **Custom Access Token Hook** (`custom_access_token_hook` migration) — a `SECURITY DEFINER` Postgres function stamping `tenant_id` and a placeholder `permissions_hash` (role-assignment-based; Stage 3's resolver defines the canonical version) onto every Supabase JWT. `SECURITY DEFINER` is required: `users`/`role_assignments` have `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, and Supabase invokes the hook as `supabase_auth_admin`, which has no `app.tenant_id` session var and no `BYPASSRLS` — without it the lookup would silently return zero rows and no user would ever get a `tenant_id` claim. **Required a one-time manual step**: enabling the hook in Supabase Dashboard → Authentication → Hooks (not reachable via SQL or the Data API; only the Dashboard or a Management API personal-access-token we don't hold) — user completed this.
- **Blueprint seed** (`apps/api/prisma/seed.ts`, wired into `prisma.config.ts`'s `migrations.seed`) — the IT blueprint fixture from the Phase 1 plan, upserted idempotently.
- **Verified fully end-to-end against the live Supabase project**, through the real HTTP API (not just SQL): signup creates all expected rows (Tenant, User, 2 materialized Roles, RoleAssignment, TenantConfig); login through Supabase's own Auth REST API returns a JWT that — once the hook was enabled — carried correct `tenant_id`/`permissions_hash` claims; `/me` returned the right user for two independently signed-up tenants with zero cross-tenant leakage through the full stack (not just raw SQL as in Stage 1); no-token and pre-hook (claim-less) requests both correctly 401. Test tenants and their Supabase auth users were cleaned up afterward.
- One real debugging detour, documented for future reference: a test harness mistake (a stray `dotenv` stdout banner leaking into a shell variable) produced a malformed `Authorization` header that Node's HTTP parser rejected at the protocol level (400, zero app-level logs) — not an application bug. Root-caused by testing with `curl -v` and fixed by extracting the token cleanly.
- All packages still typecheck and build clean.

#### Stage 3 — RBAC + Permission Resolver (complete)
- **`apps/api/src/rbac/`** — new module, `@Global()` (consistent with `TenancyModule`'s pattern, since `PermissionResolverService` will be needed broadly from Stage 4 onward):
  - `scope.ts` — scope ordering (`own < team < department < tenant`) and `broaderScope()`.
  - `permission-triple.ts` — parses/validates `"resource:action:scope"` strings.
  - `grant-resolution.ts` — `applyGrantDeltas(base, deltas)`: resolves `+`/`-`-prefixed grant deltas against a base set (the `role.charge-nurse extends role.nurse` pattern from ARCHITECTURE §5.2/§6.3). This is **materialization-time** logic for Stage 4's override-applier to reuse when tenant-custom roles extend others — not used at permission-check time.
  - `permission-collapse.ts` — `collapsePermissions(grants)`: unions already-flat grants, collapses to broadest scope per resource:action, returns `{ permissionsHash, has(resource, action), toArray() }`. Sorted before hashing so the hash is stable regardless of input order.
  - `permission-resolver.service.ts` — `PermissionResolverService.resolveEffectivePermissions(tenantId, userId)`: fetches a user's `RoleAssignment`s (with their materialized `Role.permissions`) via `TenantPrismaService`, unions, and collapses. Deliberately a plain join, no live extends-walk — consistent with Stage 1/2's "roles are materialized, not live-looked-up" decision (CONTEXT.md §9/§10).
- **Known Phase 1 simplification** (documented in code + here, not silently assumed): a `RoleAssignment`'s own `departmentId`/`teamId` (which scope a grant applies *to*) isn't yet factored into resolution — every Phase 1 assignment is tenant-wide. Revisit once department/team-scoped assignments actually get created (no UI for that yet in Phase 1).
- **`GET /me/permissions`** (guarded) — added as a verification convenience so Stage 3 has something callable/visible, not just unit tests; not meant as a permanent API surface (Stage 4+ calls the service directly).
- **Jest set up for the first time** (`apps/api/jest.config.js`, `ts-jest`) — wired into `apps/api/package.json`'s `test` script, the root `test` script (`pnpm -r run test --if-present`, so packages without tests don't fail the run), and CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml` now runs tests between typecheck and build).
- **16 unit tests**, all passing — covering exactly the plan's Stage 3 verification asks: grant-delta resolution (add/remove/mixed, no-op removal of a non-existent grant, base-array immutability) and permission collapse (broadest-scope-wins, order-independent hashing, hash changes on add/broaden/remove, malformed-input rejection).
- **Verified live** against the real Supabase project (not just unit tests): signup → login → `GET /me/permissions` returned exactly the seeded Admin role's 10 permissions, correctly sorted, with a stable hash — proving the resolver works through the full stack (JWT → middleware → guard → resolver → RLS-scoped query), not just in isolation. Test tenant cleaned up afterward.
- Full workspace typecheck, test, and build all pass.

#### Stage 4 — Configuration Engine core (complete)
- **`apps/api/src/config-engine/`** — the four-layer pipeline from ARCHITECTURE §6.4:
  - `types.ts` — loose internal types (`BlueprintDefinition`, `NavItem`, `UINode`, `ActionSpec`, `DataBinding`, `TenantConfigOverrides`, `WorkspaceManifest`, `KeyedListPatch<T>`). Formalized as shared Zod contracts in Stage 5.
  - `override-applier.ts` — `applyKeyedListPatch`/`applyOverrides`: the stable-id add/remove/patch mechanism from §6.3, applied uniformly to navigation *and* page children (one mechanism, not two).
  - `entitlement-filter.ts` — `filterByEntitlements`: drops modules/nav for plans that don't unlock them; `null` (no plan assigned — every Phase 1 tenant today) means unrestricted, matching "no billing wall yet."
  - `permission-pruner.ts` — `pruneByPermissions`: the **prune, not hide** invariant — disallowed nav/actions are absent from the returned tree, never present-but-disabled.
  - `compiler.service.ts` — `ConfigEngineService.compileWorkspace(tenantId, user)`: orchestrates all four layers + role labels + feature flags into a finalized `WorkspaceManifest` with an `etag`.
- **Real gap resolved, not silently assumed:** ARCHITECTURE.md describes permission-driven pruning ("removes any nav item, page, widget, action... gated by a permission the user lacks") but never specifies how a blueprint element *declares* which permission gates it — neither `NavItem` nor `UINode` had such a field. Resolved with a minimal `requiredPermission?: "resource:action"` field on both, plus `moduleKey?: string` on `NavItem` for entitlement-filtering. Documented in `types.ts` and `CONTEXT.md`.
- **`attachDataSourceRefs`** from the architecture's pipeline is deliberately elided — blueprint bindings already reference data-source names directly (`bind: { source: "projects.list" }`), so there's nothing to "attach" at compile time; resolving/authorizing what a source name means happens at fetch time (Stage 6/8), not here.
- **IT blueprint re-seeded** (`prisma/seed.ts`) — `pages` was left empty in Stage 2 ("populated in Stage 4"); now filled in with the plan's full fixture (dashboard/projects/tasks pages), plus `requiredPermission`/`moduleKey` tags added so pruning and entitlement-filtering are actually demonstrable (e.g. a `project.delete` mutation gated at `project:delete`, present for Admin, absent for Member).
- **`GET /me/workspace`** (guarded) — Stage 4's verification convenience, same pattern as Stage 3's `/me/permissions`; returns the full compiled manifest. Temporary — Stage 6 replaces it with the real `GET /api/workspace/bootstrap` (etag/304 caching, lazy per-page fetch).
- **17 new unit tests** (33 total), covering the plan's exact verification wording: a nav-label patch changes only that item and leaves the rest of the blueprint untouched; unknown patch/override targets (page id, list anchor) degrade safely instead of crashing; Admin sees a `project:delete`-gated action that Member does not (asserted as *absent from the array*, not present-but-disabled); an unset permission prunes its nav item entirely.
- **Verified live** against the real Supabase project: signed up, fetched the full compiled `WorkspaceManifest` via `GET /me/workspace` (correct nav, correct dashboard tree, correct `permissionsHash` matching Stage 3's), then applied a tenant-config nav-label override directly to the DB and re-fetched — confirmed only `nav.projects`'s label changed, everything else identical.
- **Known constraint documented, not enforced yet:** the `etag` is `hash(blueprintVersion, tenantConfigVersion, permissionsHash)` per ARCHITECTURE §6.7 — it depends on `TenantConfig.version` bumping, not on hashing override *content*. This means "updating" a tenant config must always mean *inserting a new versioned row* (marking the old one inactive), never mutating `overrides` in place — no code path does the latter today (there's no tenant-config write API at all yet; that's Phase 3's admin-authoring UI), but a raw-SQL/script mutation would silently produce a stale etag. Noted here so it isn't rediscovered as a mystery when that UI gets built.
- Fixed a real bug surfaced while re-verifying: the root `test` script had `--if-present` positioned after `run test`, so pnpm forwarded it to Jest itself (`Unrecognized option`) instead of applying it to the recursive-run. Corrected to `pnpm -r --if-present run test`.
- Full workspace typecheck, test, and build all pass.

#### Stage 5 — Shared manifest-schema package (complete)
- **`packages/manifest-schema/src/`** — the full manifest contract as Zod schemas, all exported with inferred TS types: `permission.ts` (`Scope`, `PermissionTriple`, `parsePermissionString`), `ui-node.ts` (`UINode`, `DataBinding`, `ActionSpec` — recursive via `z.lazy()`), `nav-item.ts` (`NavItem` — also recursive), `keyed-list-patch.ts` (generic `keyedListPatchSchema<T>()` factory — the one stable-id add/remove/patch mechanism, shared rather than duplicated per use site), `blueprint.ts` (`BlueprintDefinition`, `BlueprintRoleDef`), `tenant-config.ts` (`TenantConfigOverrides`, `PagePatch`), `manifest.ts` (`WorkspaceManifest`, `MANIFEST_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1`).
- **Refactored `apps/api` to consume it, zero duplication:** deleted `apps/api/src/config-engine/types.ts` and `apps/api/src/rbac/permission-triple.ts` entirely; every former consumer now imports from `@antigravity/manifest-schema`. `apps/api/src/rbac/scope.ts` keeps only the resolver-specific `broaderScope` algorithm, re-exporting `Scope`/`SCOPES` from the shared package rather than redefining them.
- **`ConfigEngineService.compileWorkspace` now calls `WorkspaceManifestSchema.parse()`** on its own output as the final step before returning — a malformed manifest now fails loudly inside the compiler, not silently at a client with no way to know what shape to expect. `prisma/seed.ts` similarly validates the IT blueprint fixture against `BlueprintDefinitionSchema` before upserting.
- **Real ESM/CJS interop bug hit and fixed, not glossed over:** the package initially built as ESM (`"type": "module"`, `export *` syntax). This broke two ways: (1) Jest's CommonJS `require()` couldn't parse the compiled output at all (`Unexpected token 'export'`); (2) far more seriously, **the actual compiled API server failed to boot** — `node dist/main.js` crashed immediately with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`, because Node's ESM resolver additionally requires explicit `.js` extensions on relative specifiers, which `tsc` doesn't emit by default. Since `apps/api` compiles to CommonJS (NestJS's standard mode) and Next.js consumes either module format transparently, the fix was to make the shared package build as **CommonJS** (`tsconfig.json`: `module: "CommonJS"`; dropped `"type": "module"` from `package.json`) rather than attempt a dual ESM+CJS build — avoids the whole dual-package-hazard class of bugs for a solo-maintained package with exactly two consumers.
- **Verified for real, not assumed:** typecheck/build succeeding was insufficient proof (nothing in `apps/web` referenced the package yet, so a broken import path wouldn't have surfaced). Added a real import (`MANIFEST_SCHEMA_VERSION`) to `apps/web`'s homepage, rebuilt, ran the dev server, and confirmed the rendered HTML actually contains the value — proving genuine cross-package resolution through Next's bundler, not just a clean `tsc` pass. Separately, the compiled API server was stopped and restarted from a clean build specifically to catch the runtime (not just typecheck-time) failure described above.
- **Live end-to-end proof of the plan's literal verification criteria:** signed up, logged in, called `GET /me/workspace` — 200 response with `schemaVersion: 1`, meaning the compiler's `WorkspaceManifestSchema.parse()` call succeeded on real compiled output, not just the test fixture.
- Full workspace typecheck, test (33/33), and build all pass — including the real dual-mode check (Jest CJS test run + actual `node dist/main.js` boot) this stage specifically required.

#### Stage 6 — Bootstrap/page API endpoints (complete)
- **`GET /api/workspace/bootstrap`** and **`GET /api/workspace/pages/:pageId`** (`apps/api/src/workspace/`) — the real delivery API from ARCHITECTURE §6.7, replacing Stage 4's temporary `GET /me/workspace` (removed).
- **`ConfigEngineService` split into a cheap/expensive half** (`compiler.service.ts`): `resolveIdentity()` fetches only tenant + active tenant-config row + resolved permissions — everything the `etag` depends on — without touching blueprint JSON or pruning. `resolvePrunedBlueprint()` is the expensive half (load blueprint, apply overrides, filter entitlements, prune), only called once a 304 is ruled out. `getEtag()` exposes the cheap half alone for the pre-check; `compilePage(tenantId, user, pageId)` is new, returning a single page's tree (or `null` if the id doesn't exist *or* was pruned away — same response either way, preserving prune-not-hide for direct page fetches too).
- **`etagMatches()`** (`config-engine/etag.ts`) — pure, unit-tested (6 tests) `If-None-Match` comparator: handles the quoted form we emit, a bare-value fallback, and comma-separated candidate lists.
- **`POST /api/data/:source`** and **`POST /api/mutations/:mutation`** stubs (`apps/api/src/data-sources/`, `apps/api/src/mutations/`) — both guarded, both 404 with a clear "not registered yet" message for any name. Deliberately structured as their own modules now (matching the plan's intended Stage 8 file layout) rather than inline in the workspace controller, so Stage 8 fills in real registries without restructuring.
- **Extracted `CurrentUserService`** (`apps/api/src/tenancy/current-user.service.ts`) — resolving the internal `User` row from the auth context was duplicated in `MeController` and would have been a third time in `WorkspaceController`; factored out once genuinely reused, not preemptively.
- **Verified live against all three of the plan's literal criteria**, in sequence against the real Supabase project: (1) `GET .../bootstrap` returns a valid, schema-validated manifest for a real JWT; (2) refetching with `If-None-Match` set to the returned `ETag` returns `304` with a confirmed zero-byte body; (3) a permission was added directly to the tenant's Admin role via script, and refetching — even while still sending the *old* `If-None-Match` value — correctly returned `200` with a new, different `ETag`, proving cache invalidation on permission change. Also verified `GET .../pages/page.projects` (200, correct tree), `GET .../pages/page.nonexistent` (404), and both stub endpoints (404, clear message). Test tenant cleaned up afterward.
- Full workspace typecheck, test (39/39), and build all pass.

#### Stage 7 — SDUI renderer + core primitives, frontend (complete)
- **`apps/web` is now a real application**, not a placeholder. Scope extended beyond the plan's literal "renderer + primitives" wording to include what testing it in a browser actually requires: login/signup pages and a workspace shell — the original Stage 0–11 plan never allocated a stage for auth UI.
- **`Renderer`** (`apps/web/src/sdui/renderer.tsx`) — registry lookup, Zod validation at the boundary, per-node error boundary, graceful unknown-node fallback, exactly per ARCHITECTURE §7.5. **Found and fixed a real isolation bug before it shipped**: an initial version parsed props *before* constructing the node's own error boundary, so a validation failure would bubble to the *parent's* boundary and take out sibling nodes too — violating "rest of page intact." Fixed by restructuring so validation happens *inside* a child component that the boundary wraps, giving every node (including its own prop-parsing step) an isolated failure domain.
- **17 primitives registered** (`apps/web/src/sdui/primitives/`): Layout (Page, Section, Stack, Grid, Card), Display (Heading, Text, KpiCard, Badge, EmptyState), Data (Table, List), Input (SearchBar, FilterBar), Action (Button, ActionMenu, Link). `Form`/`Field` deliberately deferred — nothing in the current blueprint renders a form yet (Stage 8 will, for project creation). `SearchBar`/`FilterBar` are visually present but not yet wired to actually filter data (no page-level query-state plumbing yet; documented in-code).
- **Composite placeholders** (`apps/web/src/modules/{projects,tasks}/`) for `ProjectBoard`/`TaskList` — not static mocks: they genuinely call `useDataBinding` and show the real loading/error/empty states against Stage 6's still-stubbed data endpoints, so the dashboard isn't dead until Stage 8/9 land.
- **Data binding** (`use-data-binding.ts`) resolves `{const}`/`{ref}` synchronously and `{source, params}` via `POST /api/data/:source` — which 404s for every name today (Stage 6 stub), so every source-bound widget's error state is exercised by simply loading the dashboard, not a contrived test.
- **Action dispatch** (`use-action-dispatch.ts`) implements `navigate` and `mutation` per the plan's literal scope; `openModal`/`openDrawer`/`aiPrompt`/`download` log a warning and no-op.
- **Real bug caught and fixed while writing the blueprint's Heading node**: the fixture used `{{user.firstName}}`, but `WorkspaceManifest.user` only has `displayName` — would have silently rendered a blank greeting in the browser. Fixed in `prisma/seed.ts`, re-seeded, re-verified live.
- **Auth**: Supabase browser client signs in directly (client never brokers login through our API, matching ARCHITECTURE §5.1); access token stored in `localStorage` — an explicitly documented Phase 1 simplification (no SSR-protected routes, no httpOnly cookie; revisit before Phase 2 hardening).
- **Frontend testing set up for the first time**: Vitest + React Testing Library + jsdom. One focused test (`renderer.test.tsx`) directly proves the plan's literal Stage 7 verification: a manifest with a valid node, a node with props that fail Zod validation, and a node with an unregistered `type@version`, all as siblings — asserts the valid siblings render, the broken node shows its own error fallback (not the page), and the unknown node shows the graceful fallback. Passes.
- **Verified live**: both servers built/started for real (not just `next build`/`nest build` succeeding); every page route (`/`, `/login`, `/signup`, `/workspace`) confirmed to return 200 with no server-side crash; a full signup → Supabase login → `GET /api/workspace/bootstrap` round trip confirmed the `displayName` fix resolves correctly end to end. Both servers left running for hands-on browser testing.
- Full workspace typecheck, test (40/40 — 39 backend + 1 frontend), and build all pass.

#### Stage 8 — Projects module end-to-end (complete)
- **The `Project` Prisma model already existed** (Stage 1) — no schema change needed. What Stage 8 actually built was the machinery to make it *reachable*: real data sources, real mutations, and the generic registry pattern both this module and Stage 9's Tasks module depend on.
- **`DataSourceRegistry`/`MutationRegistry`** (`apps/api/src/data-sources/`, `mutations/`) — in-process, name-keyed registries (NestJS providers, `@Global()`), populated at boot by each feature module's own registrar. `DataSourcesController`/`MutationsController` (Stage 6 stubs) now dispatch to whatever's registered, still 404ing any unregistered name. Deliberately *not* the full `ModuleDefinition` abstraction ARCHITECTURE §9 describes (blueprintFragments/entitlementKey/etc.) — built the shared piece once two modules (Projects now, Tasks next) genuinely need it, not the whole plugin system for hypothetical future modules.
- **`isRowInScope()`** (`rbac/scope-check.ts`, unit-tested) — the second half of scope-based authorization: `PermissionResolverService.has()` tells you the broadest granted scope for `resource:action`; this decides whether that scope actually covers *this specific row* (`own` → ownerId match, `department`/`team` → departmentId match — no `teamId` column on `Project` yet, documented simplification).
- **Projects module** (`apps/api/src/modules/projects/`): `projects.list`/`projects.count` data sources (scope-filtered, capped at 50 rows — no real pagination yet), `project.create`/`update`/`delete` mutations (presence-gated by the controller, scope-gated by the resolver itself via `isRowInScope`). List results join in the owner's `displayName` (not stored on `Project`) so the Table's "owner" column shows a name, not a raw UUID.
- **Real `ProjectBoard`** (`apps/web/src/modules/projects/ProjectBoard.tsx`) replaces Stage 7's placeholder: groups fetched projects into columns by `status`, and shows a "+ New Project" quick-create control **only when the `project.create` action survived pruning** — extending prune-not-hide from primitives/nav to a composite's own controls. Creating a project calls the mutation directly (bypassing the generic `ActionSpec.input` resolution, since a typed-in name is dynamic user input, not something derivable from render context) and calls the new `useDataBinding` `refetch()` afterward.
- **`Table.tsx`** gained per-row mutation-action buttons (e.g. a Delete button using `project.delete`), calling `refetch()` on success, alongside its existing row-click-navigate behavior.
- **Blueprint fixture fixed, not just extended:** removed a dead `navigate: page.project-detail` action (that page never existed — Phase 1's page model has no per-record detail-view routing at all, an honest scope cut rather than a shipped broken link) from both the dashboard board and the projects table; moved `project.create` off the table (creation now lives on the board) onto... nowhere else needed, since it's now board-only; fixed `project.delete`'s `input: { ref: "row" }` to `{ ref: "row.id" }` (the mutation expects just an id, not the whole row object — would have sent the wrong shape).
- **Two real bugs caught during live verification, both fixed with regression tests, not just patched:**
  1. A discriminated-union narrowing bug in `Table.tsx` (`"mutation" in action` didn't narrow as cleanly as switching on the literal `kind` field — TypeScript compile error, caught before runtime).
  2. **A genuine 500.** Testing the Member role's `project:read:team` scope with no department set hit a real crash: the scope filter used a non-UUID sentinel string (`"__no_department__"`) against `departmentId`, a Postgres `uuid` column — Postgres rejected the query outright instead of matching zero rows. Fixed by skipping the query entirely (returning `null`, same as "no read grant") rather than relying on a sentinel value; added `projects.data-sources.spec.ts` with an explicit regression test so this can't silently return.
  3. Also fixed: `POST /api/data/:source` was returning NestJS's default `201 Created` for what's always a read operation — added `@HttpCode(200)`.
- **6 new unit tests** (`scope-check.spec.ts`, `projects.data-sources.spec.ts`), 50 backend + 1 frontend total, all passing.
- **Verified live, end-to-end, against the real Supabase project** — every one of the plan's literal criteria, plus more found along the way: bootstrap shows Projects nav + board with the create action present for Admin; created a project via the mutation endpoint and confirmed it appeared in the next `projects.list` fetch; swapped the same user to Member-only and confirmed the create action was **pruned from the manifest** (empty `actions` array, not present-but-disabled) *and* that calling the mutation directly still 403'd server-side (defense in depth, not just UI hiding); confirmed Member's `project:delete` is rejected entirely (no grant at any scope); confirmed the department-scope 500 is fixed; deleted the project as Admin and confirmed it disappeared from the list. Test tenant cleaned up afterward.
- Full workspace typecheck, test (51/51), and build all pass.

#### Post-Stage-8 troubleshooting — stale `.next` build cache
- User hit a runtime error opening the app in the browser: `Cannot find module './617.js'`, traced to `apps/web/.next/server/...`. Root cause: `next build` (production) and `next dev` had both been run against the same `.next` output directory earlier in the session (during Stage 7/8 verification) — their chunk manifests aren't cross-compatible, so Next.js ended up referencing a chunk from the wrong mode.
- **Fix:** stopped the dev server, deleted `apps/web/.next` entirely (a fully regenerable build-cache directory — nothing of substance lives there, same category as `node_modules`/`dist`), restarted `next dev` fresh. Verified `/`, `/login`, `/signup` render real content with no trace of the error before handing back.
- **Note for future sessions:** avoid running `next build` and `next dev` back-to-back against the same `apps/web/.next` without clearing it in between (or use separate output dirs if this recurs) — this is a known Next.js footgun, not an application bug, but it's cheap to avoid.
- User confirmed the app works correctly after the fix.

#### Session 2 resumed — Stage 9 (Tasks module end-to-end) (complete)
- **Reused Stage 8's patterns exactly, as intended** — no new abstractions needed. `tasks.data-sources.ts`/`tasks.mutations.ts` mirror `projects.*` structurally; `TasksRegistrar` mirrors `ProjectsRegistrar`; `isRowInScope` reused unmodified.
- **One real adaptation, not a limitation:** `Task` has no `ownerId`/`departmentId` columns (unlike `Project`). "Own" scope maps to `assigneeId` instead; "department"/"team" scope reaches the department by joining through the owning `Project` (`where: { project: { departmentId } }`) for reads, and via `include: { project: { select: { departmentId } } }` for the row-level scope check in `task.updateStatus`. Documented inline in `tasksWhere` and the mutation.
- **Fixed the blueprint bug flagged at the end of Stage 8** — `task.updateStatus`'s `input: { ref: "row" }` → `{ ref: "row.id" }`. Also added a `task.create` action to the `page.tasks` blueprint node (mirroring `project.create` on the dashboard board) so the real `TaskList` composite has something to presence-gate its create UI against.
- **Real `TaskList`** (`apps/web/src/modules/tasks/TaskList.tsx`) replaces the placeholder: fetches "my tasks," shows a per-task status dropdown (gated on `task.updateStatus` surviving pruning) that calls the mutation directly and refetches, and a "+ New Task" control (gated on `task.create`) that fetches `projects.list` itself (not via `bind` — a composite's own UI need, not the node's data) to populate a project picker. Same "dynamic user input bypasses `ActionSpec.input` resolution" pattern as `ProjectBoard` (see CONTEXT.md §16) — status and title are user-driven, not derivable from render context.
- **10 new unit tests** (`tasks.data-sources.spec.ts`) — mirrors `projects.data-sources.spec.ts`'s coverage, plus an explicit security-property test new to this module: a client-supplied `assigneeId` param must be *overridden* by "own" scope, not merged with it (an own-scoped caller can't see someone else's tasks by passing a different id). 61 backend + 1 frontend total, all passing.
- **One transient infra hiccup during live verification, correctly diagnosed as not a bug:** a `project.create` call briefly hit a Prisma `P2028` ("unable to start a transaction in the given time") — a Supabase/Supavisor pooler timeout. Confirmed the API process itself was healthy (`/health` responded normally throughout) and only one clean Node process was running (no leaked connections from earlier restarts), then confirmed the identical request succeeded moments later. Documented as an operational note, not chased as a code bug.
- **One live-testing mistake caught and corrected before being mistaken for a real bug:** an initial test swapped a *single* user's role from Admin to Member and expected `task:update:own` to block them from updating a task — but that task was self-assigned while they were still Admin, so "own" correctly allowed it (same person, different role). Not a bug. Re-verified properly by creating a **genuinely separate second user** (real Supabase auth account, real distinct `User` row, Member role) — confirmed that user correctly gets `403` updating the first user's task, and correctly sees only their own tasks.
- **Real, worth-flagging Phase 1 gap found (not fixed, deliberately):** Member's role grants `task:read:team` (and `project:read:team`) rather than `own`. Since there's no department/team-*assignment* UI yet, a Member with no department set can't see team-scoped data — **including tasks/projects they just created themselves**, since read visibility and creation aren't the same check. This is the permission model working exactly as defined (fail-closed, not a bug), but it's a genuine UX gap: whether "you can always see what you created, regardless of your broader read scope" should be a rule is a real design question, not something to silently patch by inventing new scope semantics. Flagged for the user rather than unilaterally resolved — affects what a Member-role browser test will show today.
- **Verified live, end-to-end, against the real Supabase project**, matching all three of the plan's literal criteria plus the extra rigor above: task created under a project appears in `tasks.list`; `task.updateStatus` correctly moves "My Open Tasks" KPI count from 1 to 0; a genuinely separate Member user sees only their own tasks and is rejected updating someone else's. Test tenant and both test users cleaned up afterward.
- Full workspace typecheck, test (62/62), and build all pass. `apps/web/.next` cleared before starting the dev server for live testing, per the lesson documented at the end of Stage 8.

#### Decision: team-scope visibility gap deferred
- User asked directly: should "own creations are always visible" be layered on top of scope-based reads? **Decision: defer.** No department/team-assignment UI exists yet, so this mostly affects manual testing (a Member test user with no department), not real usage. No resolver/permission-model changes made. Revisit once departments are actually assignable to users.

#### Post-Stage-9 bug — stale manifest cache from an incomplete etag
- **User report:** the Tasks page showed only "No tasks yet" in the browser — no project dropdown, no title input, no "+ New Task" button — despite the backend correctly returning `task.create`/`task.updateStatus` actions when checked directly.
- **Root cause, found not assumed:** the manifest `etag` (`hash(blueprintVersion, tenantConfigVersion, permissionsHash)`, ARCHITECTURE §6.7) used `Tenant.blueprintVersion` — a version *number* ("1"), not a content hash. Every blueprint content fix across Stages 7, 8, and 9 (the `displayName` fix, the dead-link removal, adding `task.create`) edited the same version-1 row's content in place via the seed script's `upsert`, without bumping the version number. Since the etag never changed, and `Cache-Control: private, no-cache` (set in Stage 6) makes browsers automatically revalidate via `If-None-Match`, the server kept confirming stale cached responses were still valid via `304`. The user's browser had almost certainly cached `page.tasks` from Stage 7 — *before* `task.create` existed in the blueprint at all — and every fix since then was silently invisible to it.
- **Fix:** added `Blueprint.updatedAt` (new column + migration, `@updatedAt`-managed) and included it in the etag's hash input (`apps/api/src/config-engine/compiler.service.ts`). Selected alone (`select: { updatedAt: true }`), not the full `definition` JSON, to keep the cheap half of the pipeline (§14) actually cheap.
- **Verified the fix directly, not just assumed:** fetched `page.tasks`, noted its etag; re-ran the seed script (simulating exactly the kind of content edit that caused this); fetched again and confirmed the etag changed; then explicitly sent the *old* etag as `If-None-Match` and confirmed the server now correctly returns `200` (fresh content) instead of `304` — proving a browser holding the old cached etag will get the real, current content on its next request, with no manual cache-clearing required on the user's end.
- **Scope note:** the same staleness class of bug could theoretically affect `TenantConfig` too (its `version` field is meant to be bumped on every real change, per the Stage 4 "insert a new version, never mutate in place" invariant) — but unlike `Blueprint`, no code path mutates an existing `TenantConfig` row's content today (Stage 4's own test script did, but that was cleanup-tracked test-only activity, not a real workflow). Not fixed now since there's no active bug there; worth remembering if that invariant is ever violated by real code.
- Full workspace typecheck, test (61/61 — unchanged, no test coverage gap introduced or needed for this fix beyond what already existed), and build all pass.

#### User confirmed the etag/stale-cache fix
- User retested the Tasks page after the fix above: project dropdown, task input, and "+ New Task" button all render correctly, task creation works, and Dashboard KPIs update as expected. Fix confirmed working live, not just in isolated verification.

#### Session 2 continued — Stage 10 (Notifications, basic) (complete)
- **Notifications are deliberately own-scoped by construction, not by permission grant** — every authenticated user reads/writes only their own inbox (`userId: ctx.userId` in every query), so `notifications.list`/`notifications.unreadCount`/`notification.markRead`/`notification.markAllRead` all have **no `requiredPermission`** and no `isRowInScope()` call. Unlike Projects/Tasks, there's no role-based access question to answer — the filter *is* the scope. `apps/api/src/modules/notifications/` follows the same `DataSourceRegistry`/`MutationRegistry` registrar pattern as Projects/Tasks (`NotificationsRegistrar`, registered in `app.module.ts`).
- **A real, deliberate scope decision, not an oversight: only `task.create` (assigned to someone else) and `task.updateStatus` (by someone other than the assignee) emit notifications** — `project.create` does not. Reasoning: a task has one clear recipient (its assignee); a project has no "members"/"watchers" concept in Phase 1 beyond owner + department, so there's no single unambiguous recipient to notify without inventing a fan-out mechanism nothing asked for. The plan's own Stage 10 line only gave "task assigned" as its example trigger ("e.g."), not an exhaustive list — this reads that as the real requirement, not a stage cut. Revisit if/when Projects grow an actual membership concept.
- **Notification creation happens inside the same Prisma transaction as the triggering mutation** (`tx.notification.create(...)` inside `task.create`/`task.updateStatus`'s own `resolve`), not a separate post-commit hook — `tenantPrisma.run()` already wraps every mutation in a transaction, so this is atomic for free without inventing an event-bus/hook abstraction for two call sites.
- **Extracted the "who gets notified" branching into a pure, unit-tested function** — `notificationRecipientFor(assigneeId, actorId)` in `tasks.mutations.ts` (returns `null` for "no assignee" and "actor is the assignee" alike — you don't need to be told about your own action). Same rationale as `projectsWhere`/`tasksWhere`: pure logic gets extracted and tested directly rather than only exercised through a mocked `tx`. 2 new tests (`tasks.mutations.spec.ts`).
- **`NotificationBell` (`apps/web/src/modules/notifications/NotificationBell.tsx`) is fixed frontend chrome, not a registered SDUI composite** — same precedent as the sidebar nav in `workspace/layout.tsx`, which already reads live manifest data without going through the `Renderer`/registry. A notification bell needs to persist across every page, and Phase 1's page model has no mechanism for a node to live outside one specific page's tree (see CONTEXT.md §16's nav note) — so it's rendered directly in `WorkspaceLayout`'s header row, not added to any blueprint page. It polls `notifications.unreadCount` every 15s, fetches `notifications.list` on open, and supports per-item mark-read plus mark-all-read, with outside-click-to-close.
- **Verified live, end-to-end, against the real Supabase project** with a genuinely separate second user (Admin + Member, same tenant, per the Stage 9 lesson): Admin created a task assigned to Member → Member's `unreadCount` went 0→1, `notifications.list` showed the correct `task.assigned` row; Member marked it read → `unreadCount` back to 0; Admin changed the task's status (as a non-assignee) → Member's `unreadCount` went 0→1 again with a `task.status_changed` row; Member's `markAllRead` cleared it; confirmed Member updating their *own* task's status does **not** self-notify. All assertions scripted and passed programmatically, not eyeballed. Test tenant/users cleaned up afterward (throwaway script deleted, per usual practice).
- Full workspace typecheck, test (64/64 — 63 backend [61 prior + 2 new] + 1 frontend), and API rebuild all pass. **Note:** the API server was running a stale `node dist/main.js` production build from an earlier session (not `nest start --watch`), so source changes weren't hot-reloading — rebuilt (`nest build`) and restarted before live verification; confirmed `NotificationsModule dependencies initialized` in the clean boot log. Frontend (`next dev`) does hot-reload, no restart needed there.

#### User confirmed Stage 10 (Notifications) works live

#### Session 2 continued — Stage 11 (End-to-end smoke test) — Phase 1 exit criterion met
- **The plan's exit criterion requires running the full walkthrough against a freshly `prisma migrate reset` database** — a destructive operation (drops all data, reapplies every migration, reseeds) against the live Supabase project, since there is no separate local/throwaway DB in this setup. Before running it, checked the live DB and found one real tenant ("Magar Limited," 1 user) — **the user's own hands-on-testing account from Stages 7–10, not throwaway test data.** Stopped and asked the user explicitly (via a two-option question: run the real reset vs. verify against the current DB without resetting) rather than assuming it was safe to destroy. **User chose to run the full reset.**
- **Prisma itself has a built-in AI-agent safety gate on `migrate reset`** — it refused to run the first time, printing a message that identified Claude Code as the invoker and required a `PRISMA_USER_CONSENT_FOR_DANGEROUS_AI_ACTION` environment variable containing the user's *exact, freshly-given* consent text (explicitly stating that prior conversation turns don't count). Relayed Prisma's own required disclosure to the user in full (exact command, motivation, irreversibility, dev-vs-prod assessment) and got an explicit "Yes, you can proceed" before rerunning with that consent recorded. Worth knowing about if this ever needs to run again — it's not a one-time fluke, it's Prisma 7's standing behavior when it detects an AI-agent caller.
- **Real, minor finding: `prisma migrate reset --force` did not auto-run the seed script**, despite `seed: "tsx prisma/seed.ts"` being configured in `prisma.config.ts` (which normally makes `migrate reset` seed automatically). The reset output showed all 4 migrations applying cleanly but no seed step; confirmed by querying the `blueprints` table directly (0 rows). Worked around by running `pnpm run prisma:seed` manually immediately after. Not investigated further (not blocking, and Phase 1 has no automated deploy pipeline that would depend on this working unattended) — **flagging for later: don't assume `migrate reset` alone leaves a seeded DB in this project; always verify or reseed explicitly.**
- **Full scripted walkthrough, run against the freshly reset + manually reseeded DB, covering every clause of the plan's literal exit criterion:**
  1. Signup → real tenant + Admin user created.
  2. Bootstrap manifest inspected structurally: `schemaVersion`, all 3 nav items, the default page (`page.dashboard`, from the blueprint's own `dashboards.default`), and the 3-KPI dashboard grid all trace directly back to the IT blueprint fixture — confirming the "zero hardcoded pages" claim isn't just asserted, it's checkable.
  3. Created a project.
  4. Created a genuinely separate Member user in the same tenant (per the Stage 9 lesson) and a task assigned to them.
  5. Confirmed the notification fired: Member's `unreadCount` → 1, `notifications.list` showed the correct `task.assigned` row.
  6. Confirmed restricted-role pruning: Member's `page.projects` table has **no** `project.delete` action (absent, not disabled) — and, as defense-in-depth, confirmed calling `project.delete` directly as Member still returns `403` regardless of manifest pruning.
  7. Cleaned up the smoke-test tenant/users afterward, leaving the database in the state a real user should find it: migrations applied, IT blueprint seeded, zero tenants — genuinely ready for the user's own fresh signup, not carrying test residue.
- Full workspace typecheck and test (64/64 — unchanged, no new code this stage) pass against the post-reset state, confirming the reset didn't require any code changes to pass — the migration history and seed script really are self-sufficient.
- **This is the Phase 1 exit criterion, and it passed cleanly.** Phase 1 (per ARCHITECTURE.md §16: Configuration Engine + SDUI Renderer, proven end-to-end on one industry with two modules, plus basic in-app notifications) is functionally complete. The user's own "Magar Limited" account no longer exists — they'll need to sign up again to keep using the app in the browser.

#### Session 2 continued — Team invite feature (complete)
- **No new permission triple needed** — the IT blueprint's `role.admin` has granted `user:manage:tenant` since Stage 2; it was simply unused until now. `role.member` has no `user:*` grant, so it's a clean, free authorization boundary.
- **New module `apps/api/src/modules/users/`, exact registrar pattern as Projects/Tasks/Notifications:** `users.list` and `roles.list` data sources (both `requiredPermission: "user:manage"`, tenant-wide roster/lookup — no own/team/department scope concept, unlike Projects/Tasks); `user.invite` mutation (same permission gate, input `{ email, displayName, roleId }`).
- **`user.invite` needed a real injected dependency (`SupabaseAdminService`) that a plain exported mutation object has no way to receive** — every other mutation/data-source in this codebase is a plain constant, since none of them call an external service. Solved with a factory function (`createUserInviteMutation(supabaseAdmin)`) that `UsersRegistrar` — a real `@Injectable` with constructor DI — calls once at boot with its own injected instance, then registers the result. `SupabaseAdminService` had to be added to `AuthModule`'s `exports` (previously private to that module) and `AuthModule` imported into the new `UsersModule`.
- **Generates a one-time temporary password** (`generateTemporaryPassword()` in `users.mutations.ts`, `crypto.randomBytes(18)` as base64url — extracted as a small pure, unit-tested function, same convention as `notificationRecipientFor`/`projectsWhere`) rather than sending an invite email — Phase 1 has no email provider (a scope cut from the original plan's §6, still true). The mutation's response is the *only* place the password is ever transmitted; it's never persisted in our own DB, only in Supabase Auth. Same all-or-nothing rollback as `AuthService.signup` (orphaned Supabase auth user deleted if the Postgres write fails).
- **New nav item `nav.team` → `page.team`** in the IT blueprint fixture (`seed.ts`), gated by `requiredPermission: "user:manage"` at both the nav level (pruned entirely from Member's manifest, per the user's "Admin-only" decision) and the `user.invite` action level (defense-in-depth). No `moduleKey` — unlike Projects/Tasks, Team isn't an entitlement-gated module, it's core Admin tooling.
- **New composite `TeamMembers`** (`apps/web/src/modules/users/TeamMembers.tsx`): roster table bound to `users.list`; an Admin-only invite form (presence-gated on `user.invite` surviving pruning, same pattern as `ProjectBoard`/`TaskList`) that fetches `roles.list` itself (not via `bind` — same "composite's own UI need" pattern as `TaskList`'s project picker) to populate a real role dropdown instead of hardcoding "Admin"/"Member" strings. On success, shows the generated password once in a callout ("share this — it won't be shown again"). Registered in `register-all.ts`.
- **3 new unit tests** (`users.mutations.spec.ts`, covering `generateTemporaryPassword`'s length/charset/uniqueness). 66 backend + 1 frontend, all passing.
- **Verified live against the real Supabase project, in two parts, deliberately without ever touching the real Admin's password:** (A) a full happy-path run under a fresh throwaway tenant — signup → confirmed `nav.team` present for Admin → invited a teammate → confirmed the response included a usable temporary password → logged in as the invited user with that exact password and confirmed `Member` role + `nav.team` correctly absent from their manifest → confirmed the roster (`users.list`) shows both users → confirmed the invited Member gets `403` calling `user.invite` directly; (B) a negative check against the user's own real "Magar Limited" tenant, using the standing Member test account created earlier this session, confirming `nav.team` is absent and `user.invite` returns `403` there too — so the real tenant is provably in the correct, secured state without ever needing the real Admin's credentials.
- Reseeded the blueprint (`pnpm run prisma:seed`) to publish the new nav/page — this bumped `Blueprint.updatedAt` automatically (the Stage-9 etag fix), so the user's existing browser session will pick up the new "Team" nav item on next load with no manual cache-clearing needed. Rebuilt and restarted the API; confirmed `UsersModule dependencies initialized` in a clean boot log.
