Reconfirmed the documentation-sync practice as a standing completion criterion for every future stage.

#### Session 2 continued — Org Hierarchy Phase 1: RBAC/schema core, tier relabeling, department types (complete)
- **Planned via a dedicated plan-mode session** (`C:\Users\deepm\.claude\plans\linear-sniffing-gray.md`) after deep exploration of the exact current implementation (`scope-check.ts`, `permission-collapse.ts`/`scope.ts`, `grant-resolution.ts`, the manifest-schema `Scope`/`BlueprintRoleDef` types, `hr.mutations.ts`/`hr.data-sources.ts`, `tasksWhere`/`projectsWhere`, `users.data-sources.ts`) and a direct query of all 3 real tenants' actual role/department/user data before designing the migration — confirmed nobody held `role.hr-manager` or any Executive-tier role anywhere, and the existing 6-role chain maps 1:1 onto the new tier ladder by pure relabeling, in the same order, no reordering needed.
- **Three genuine decisions confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before writing code**: relabel the real tenants' roles now (not stage it separately) since it's a pure zero-risk label change; build the department-type bonus-permission mechanism generically now rather than as a one-off (the user's explicit choice, overriding the recommended simpler default); and include minimal frontend controls in this same phase so the new fields are genuinely testable, not just inert schema.
- **New 5-value scope enum** (`"own" | "team" | "department" | "department-subtree" | "tenant"`, `packages/manifest-schema/src/permission.ts`) — TypeScript's own exhaustiveness checking (`SCOPE_RANK`'s `Record<Scope,...>`, `isRowInScope`'s `default`-less switch) forced every dependent call site to handle the new value, a real safety property confirmed during implementation.
- **`department-subtree` resolution**: new `getDepartmentSubtreeIds` (`rbac/department-subtree.ts`, a recursive CTE over `Department.parentId`), deliberately kept as a separate on-demand async step rather than making `isRowInScope` itself async — preserves it as a pure, synchronous, unit-tested function; callers (`tasksWhere`/`projectsWhere`, now both `async`; the three `isRowInScope` sites in `projects.mutations.ts`/`tasks.mutations.ts`; `hr.mutations.ts`'s bespoke checks) only pay for the query when the resolved scope is actually `"department-subtree"`.
- **Department Type mechanism**: `Department.type` (nullable string) + a new `DepartmentTypeRoleLabel` table, materialized once at signup/reseed time (`materializeDepartmentTypeLabels`) — never live-derived from blueprint JSON, matching the standing `Role.permissions` precedent. Bonus permissions (today: HR's Manager tier) turned out to need **zero new runtime mechanism** — they're just another ordinary extends-chain blueprint role (`role.hr-manager`, re-pointed from `role.member` to `role.project-manager`), confirmed live-safe since nobody held it in any real tenant.
- **⚠️ Two real bugs found live during verification, both fixed**: (1) `materializeDepartmentTypeLabels`'s first cut did 47 sequential `upsert()` calls, which 500'd every fresh signup by blowing past `AuthService.signup`'s 5s interactive-transaction timeout (`P2028`) — fixed with one batched multi-row `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`. (2) `user.invite` itself could also blow the same 5s timeout when Resend's rejection (testing a non-owner recipient) took long enough — a **commit-time** failure happening *after* the mutation's own try/catch already returned, so its existing rollback logic never saw it, leaving an orphaned Supabase Auth account (same failure class as the earlier signup-blocking bug) — found and cleaned up live. Mitigated by raising `TenantPrismaService.run()`'s transaction timeout to 20s; the real fix (moving external HTTP calls out of the transaction entirely) is flagged as a genuine future follow-up, not solved here.
- **A real label-correctness bug caught and fixed before shipping**: the department-type label overlay initially applied unconditionally to anyone at a matching tier in a matching-typed department — meaning a generic "Manager" placed in an HR department would have shown as "HR Manager" despite lacking the actual bonus permissions. Fixed: the overlay now only applies an override-carrying label when the user's real `roleId` matches the stored `overrideRoleId`; label-only overlays (the common case, no permission implications) still apply unconditionally.
- **`departments.list`/`teams.list` gained real scope narrowing for the first time** (previously unconditionally tenant-wide once `department:manage` was held at any scope) — a real, previously undocumented gap closed alongside the Executive tier, found during exploration, not pre-planned.
- **Minimal frontend wiring**: a department-`type` selector on department creation (backed by a new `departmentTypes.list` data source) and a manager picker (new `user.setManager` mutation) in `OrgStructure.tsx`. `TeamMembers.tsx` needed zero changes.
- **Live-verified end-to-end, not just unit-tested**: a full fresh signup with the new blueprint; the label overlay in all three real scenarios (generic-tier overlay applying, generic-tier overlay correctly *not* applying outside its matching flavored role, and the distinct flavored role showing its own real label); `user.setManager`; and a complete real invite → temp password → first login → forced password change → `complete-first-login` flow for an actual Executive-tier user, who then successfully created a department *within* their own subtree (201) and was correctly rejected creating one *outside* it (403) — the department-subtree mechanism confirmed under real auth, not mocked scope objects. All 3 real tenants (`yash-1`, `magar-limited`, `new-start-limited`) reseeded to 9 roles (was 8) + 47 `DepartmentTypeRoleLabel` rows each, confirmed idempotent across two separate reseed runs. Every throwaway tenant/department/Supabase-Auth-account created during verification was fully cleaned up afterward.
- **100 backend tests + 1 frontend test, full monorepo typecheck clean** (up from 95 — 5 new tests for the `department-subtree` scope branch).

#### User provided UI_DESIGN.html as the primary design reference for the whole platform, plus a separate performance-optimization ask
- Explicit request: adopt the reference's dark "obsidian" theme, sidebar, header, typography, icons, cards, charts, command palette, spacing, and animations across the entire product — for real, not a prototype — while preserving the existing SDUI/RBAC/Configuration Engine architecture, migrating gradually module by module. Bundled with a separate ask: the platform "feels slow," optimize frontend rendering, API calls, DB queries, caching, lazy-loading, and the SDUI pipeline.
- Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before planning: switch fully to Material Symbols Outlined (not a lucide restyle); keep the light/dark toggle (obsidian becomes the new dark variant); treat design migration and performance work as two separate tracked efforts; general performance audit first, not a fix-blind list of pages.

#### Session 2 continued — full-stack performance audit (findings delivered, no fixes applied — its own future plan-mode session)
- A background research pass profiled frontend data-fetching, the SDUI rendering pipeline, backend query patterns, DB indexing, Prisma/transaction overhead, Next.js bundle configuration, and blueprint-compilation cost via static code reading (no load-testing). Ranked findings, highest impact first: (1) ~7 separate DB transactions per single bootstrap/page request, including `resolveIdentity` running twice; (2) zero client-side caching or ETag reuse despite full server-side 304 support, plus a filter-triggered refetch cascade where changing one `FilterBar` re-fetches every binding on a page, not just ones that reference it; (3) `compilePage` prunes the *entire* blueprint for a single-page request; (4) missing indexes on exactly the columns the most common scope-filtered queries filter by (`Task.assigneeId`, `Project.ownerId`/`departmentId`, `User.departmentId`); (5) duplicate ad hoc reference-data fetches across composites; (6) `recharts` bundled eagerly for every user regardless of whether their blueprint ever renders a `Chart`; (7) two smaller fixed-cost query-shape findings; (8) SDUI renderer/error-boundary overhead investigated and ruled out as a real contributor. See `CONTEXT.md` §39 for the full list. **Deliberately not acted on yet** — needs its own dedicated plan-mode session (the fixes touch RLS-dependent transaction structure, a real frontend caching-layer decision, and DB migrations), matching this project's standing working agreement.

#### Session 2 continued — Obsidian Design System migration: Phase 1, foundation + Dashboard (complete)
- **Planned via a dedicated plan-mode session** (reusing the `linear-sniffing-gray.md` plan-file path — its prior content, the now-complete Org Hierarchy Phase 1 plan, is fully recorded in `CONTEXT.md` §36 and not kept as a historical archive) after surveying the full existing UI kit and SDUI primitive surface. Confirmed the existing token architecture (CSS custom properties → Tailwind `@theme`, real light/dark variants, a single centralized `iconFor()` resolver) needed no restructuring — this phase is almost entirely *token-value* and *icon-mechanism* changes propagating through already-consistent component code.
- **A real, non-obvious finding caught before writing any font code**: `next/font/google` does not carry Material Symbols at all — confirmed by grepping Next's own bundled Google Fonts metadata rather than assuming the import would work. Self-hosted instead via the `material-symbols` npm package (ships its own static woff2 + ready CSS, bundler-resolved at build time — no runtime Google Fonts `<link>`, which would have added a render-blocking external request directly undercutting the parallel performance effort). Inter and JetBrains Mono confirmed present in Next's actual catalog and used via `next/font/google` normally.
- **Tokens**: `.dark` is now the obsidian palette from `UI_DESIGN.html`; `:root` (light) kept its existing values, designed in the same spirit since the reference is dark-only. New `--radius`/`--radius-lg`/`--radius-xl` and `--spacing-sidebar-width`/`--spacing-sidebar-collapsed` tokens added. Per-tenant branding overrides remain fully compatible (they override whichever theme's `--accent` is active).
- **Icons**: new `apps/web/src/ui/Icon.tsx` renders the Material Symbols ligature font; `iconFor()` now returns a glyph name string instead of a lucide component. Every direct lucide import across the `ui/` kit and every composite/page (~18 files) migrated to `<Icon name="..." />`. Both `lucide-react` and `geist` removed from `package.json`.
- **Sidebar collapse** (`workspace/layout.tsx`) — real collapse-to-64px-rail behavior, persisted to `localStorage`, desktop-only (mobile drawer unchanged), labels hide via a `collapsed && "md:hidden"` class (not unmount) so no state is lost on toggle.
- **Shared `.glass-panel` utility** — consolidates two previously-duplicated ad hoc `backdrop-blur` combos (`CommandPalette`, the workspace header) into one reusable class.
- **New sparkline support in `KpiCard`** — a plain inline SVG polyline, not Recharts (not worth a charting library for a 64×24px line). **Deliberately shipped without wiring in fake trend data** — no data source exposes real historical time-series yet; the capability sits ready, dormant, for when one does.
- **New `QuickActions` primitive** — config-driven dashboard shortcuts that `navigate()` to an existing page, reusing `RenderContext`'s own `navigate` rather than a new dispatch mechanism. **A real correctness fix made before shipping**: `items` is static blueprint data with no per-user permission pruning (unlike `manifest.navigation`), so without a fix a role lacking access to a target page would see a working-looking button leading to a 404 — fixed by cross-filtering against the already-pruned navigation manifest client-side.
- **`TaskList` gained an optional `title` prop and, for the first time, actually renders `Task.priority`** as a colored badge — the field already existed in the schema and the component's own interface, just was never surfaced.
- **Dashboard restyled**: `TaskList` (titled "Critical Task View," the viewer's own tasks) and `QuickActions` (4 real, per-viewer-filtered destinations) added alongside the existing KPI row/status chart/`ProjectBoard`. Reseeded and confirmed against all 3 real tenants.
- **Explicitly scoped to Dashboard only this phase** — every other module keeps Stage 12's original design-system *mechanism* (unchanged) but not yet the new obsidian *values* beyond what's shared shell-wide (fonts, icons, sidebar/header). Future phases migrate one module at a time.
- **Verification**: full monorepo typecheck clean, 100 backend + 1 frontend test passing (only `seed.ts`'s blueprint content and frontend code changed — no backend logic touched). No real browser/screenshot tool available in this environment (same disclosed limitation as Stage 12) — verified via direct inspection of the actual compiled CSS/HTML Next.js serves (confirmed the obsidian hex values, the Material Symbols `@font-face`, `.glass-panel`, and the sidebar tokens are all genuinely present) rather than a visual check. **The user's own hands-on browser review is still pending.**

#### Session 2 continued — Dynamic role-based sidebar + nested navigation: Sidebar Navigation Phase 1 (complete)
- **Planned via a dedicated plan-mode session** (`C:\Users\deepm\.claude\plans\refactored-finding-alpaca.md`) after research found the pruning/nesting *mechanism* already ~90% built — `NavItem.children` and `requiredPermission`-gated recursive pruning already existed server-side; the frontend simply never rendered nesting (a flat, non-recursive inline `<nav>` block in `workspace/layout.tsx`), and the blueprint's actual nav content was a flat 6-item list, nothing like the role-grouped structure the user's reference diagram called for.
- **Scope settled via `AskUserQuestion` before planning further**: several diagram items (Recruitment, Attendance, Reviews, Insights, Analytics, a Roles & Permissions admin UI, Company settings, plus Meetings/Team Management/Account) have zero real functionality anywhere in the codebase. User chose: build the generic mechanism + reorganize real modules into the right groups, **and** add nav entries + simple `EmptyState` "coming soon" placeholder pages for the modules that don't exist yet — not full functional builds of those modules.
- **`NavItem.pageId` is now optional** (`packages/manifest-schema/src/nav-item.ts`) — a nav item with `children` and no `pageId` is a pure disclosure group, never itself a link.
- **Two pruning refinements in `permission-pruner.ts`**: (1) a pure disclosure group that loses every child to permission pruning is now dropped entirely, not left as a dangling empty header; (2) `pruneByPermissions` now filters `pages` by each page's own root `requiredPermission` before pruning its nested actions/children — closes a real page/nav sync gap (a page absent from a user's nav could previously still be fetched whole via a direct `GET /api/workspace/pages/:pageId`; not a write-authorization bypass, since mutations/data-sources independently re-check permissions, but it violated the codebase's own "prune, not hide" invariant). `permission-pruner.spec.ts` extended with both cases (8/8 passing, up from 4).
- **`IT_BLUEPRINT_V1.navigation` restructured** (`apps/api/prisma/seed.ts`) from a flat 6-item list into role-grouped nested sections — a new "HR" group (Employees/Recruitment/Attendance/Reviews/Insights) and a new "Workspace Administration" group (Teams/Company/Roles & Permissions), plus new top-level Meetings/Team Management/Analytics/Account. **Zero new permission strings invented** — every gate reuses an existing permission already granted somewhere on the 7-tier ladder, which is what makes the tree resolve correctly per-role with no RBAC changes. Existing `page.team` ("Team" → "Employees") and `page.hr` ("Org Structure" → "Teams") were relabeled and moved into the new groups with their `id`/`pageId` unchanged, for tenant-override stability. Every page's own root `requiredPermission` now mirrors its nav item's, backfilled onto the 4 pre-existing pages too, closing the sync gap everywhere, not just for new content. A seed-time assertion now guards that the default dashboard can never carry a `requiredPermission`.
- **Two real bugs found and fixed as part of this stage, not after**: `layout.tsx`'s Cmd/Ctrl+K `commandItems` and `QuickActions.tsx`'s own client-side visibility cross-check both assumed a flat top-level `manifest.navigation` — moving real pages into nested groups would have silently broken both (lost palette entries; dashboard quick-action buttons quietly disappearing for roles that could still see them elsewhere). Fixed with one shared `flattenNavItems` utility (`apps/web/src/ui/nav-tree.ts`), reused in three places (also inside the new `WorkspaceSidebar` itself).
- **New `apps/web/src/ui/WorkspaceSidebar.tsx`** — a single reusable, recursive nav renderer with zero role/permission conditionals (it only ever receives an already-pruned tree), replacing the old inline flat block. Leaf vs. group is a pure function of `pageId`/`children` presence; a group containing the active page stays visually expanded regardless of manual toggle state; the desktop icon-only rail and mobile drawer share one code path via the same `collapsed && "md:hidden"` pattern every existing nav link already used, rather than a separate JS branch.
- **First test coverage for anything under `apps/web/src/ui/*`**: new `WorkspaceSidebar.test.tsx` (4 tests — leaf-vs-group rendering, collapse/expand, active-highlight at depth 2, navigate callback).
- **Diagram-to-permission-model simplifications stated explicitly rather than silently deviated from**: no per-tier relabeling of the same page (no "My Projects" vs. "Projects" — no mechanism exists for a per-viewer-tier label override, only tenant-wide ones); "Dashboard" doubles as "My Work"; the diagram's "Manager" bucket is read as "Lead tier and up" (where `project:create` first appears), not exactly `role.project-manager` alone.
- **Verification**: 104 backend tests passing (up from 93, +11 for the pruning fixes), 5 frontend tests passing (up from 1, +4 for `WorkspaceSidebar`), full monorepo typecheck clean. Manually verified live against the real Company Admin account (workspace `yash-1`): nested groups expand/collapse, a placeholder's `EmptyState` "coming soon" message renders, dashboard Quick Actions still work post-relabel.
- **Known Phase-1 limitation, not fixed here**: tenant-config nav overrides can only patch top-level nav items, not reach into a nested group's `children` — flagged, not addressed (no live tenant uses nav overrides today).
- Docs updated in sync: `ARCHITECTURE.md` §6.9/§7.8 (new), `CONTEXT.md` §41 (new, §40 renumbered §42), `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` §16 (addendum), this entry.

#### Session 2 continued — user adopted a one-module-at-a-time workflow; Company Administration roadmap delivered; Submodule A (Workspace Profile & Settings) complete
- **User adopted a standing workflow going forward**: plan a module and get approval → implement it completely (backend, frontend, DB, APIs, permissions, role-based visibility, UI) → test thoroughly → fix issues found → sync `CONTEXT.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md`/`CHANGELOG.md`/`ORG_HIERARCHY.md` → get approval → move to the next module. Recorded as a standing instruction, not a one-off ask.
- **A single "Roles & Permissions" module request grew into a full Company Administration capability** (Workspace Management, Organization Management, Role & Permission Management, Feature Management, Employee/Invitation Management, Security, Analytics, AI Controls, Meetings & Documents, Workflow Automation, Integrations, Notifications) — the user explicitly asked for this to be broken into logical submodules and planned one at a time, not built in one step.
- **Roadmap delivered as a dedicated plan-mode session, not code** — 14 named submodules (A–N), grouped into three tiers by how much already exists: Tier 1 (extends an existing module, buildable now — Workspace Profile & Settings, Roles & Permissions Viewer, Organization Lifecycle, Employee Lifecycle, Invitation Management), Tier 2 (real new subsystem with some scaffolding — Security & Audit, Custom Roles & Advanced Permission Config, Feature/Module Management, Notification Preferences), Tier 3 (blocked on a bigger subsystem that doesn't exist yet — Analytics & Reports, AI Controls, Meetings & Documents Admin, Workflow Automation, Integrations). One load-bearing constraint flagged for whenever Feature/Module Management is planned: a Company Admin may only *disable* modules already within their plan's entitlements, never enable something their subscription doesn't include — the Platform-Owner/Company-Admin boundary the user was explicit about.
- **User chose Submodule A (Workspace Profile & Settings) to build first.** Went through its own focused plan-mode session (research → one clarifying `AskUserQuestion` on business-hours data shape, where the user chose structured-per-weekday over the simpler free-text option recommended → `ExitPlanMode` approval) before any code.
- **New `Tenant.profile Json` column** (migration `tenant_profile`) — company description/website/contactEmail/address/timezone/structured businessHours, same untyped-JSON-bag treatment as the existing `branding` column. New `tenant.updateProfile` mutation (root-table factory, same pattern as `updateBranding`/`updateWorkspaceId`) shallow-merges a partial patch via a new pure `mergeTenantProfile`.
- **⚠️ A real gap caught during planning, before any code was written**: `WorkspaceManifestSchema.tenant` never exposed `profile` — without fixing this, the new mutations would have worked but the frontend would have had no way to read saved values back. Fixed as its own explicit step (`packages/manifest-schema`, `compiler.service.ts`), not left for testing to discover.
- **This codebase's first file-upload capability**: logo upload via a direct-to-Supabase-Storage signed-URL flow — a new `tenant.createLogoUploadUrl` mutation returns a short-lived signed upload URL (via a new method on the existing `SupabaseAdminService`, which now wraps Storage admin operations alongside Auth Admin ones), the browser uploads the file directly to Storage (never through our own API — no body-size-limit change needed anywhere), then the existing `tenant.updateBranding` mutation (extended to accept `logoUrl`) persists the result. The `logos` Storage bucket (public-read, 2MB limit, png/jpg/jpeg/webp/svg only) was created via a one-off script using the existing service-role client, not a manual Dashboard step.
- **New Settings cards** (`WorkspaceSettings.tsx`): Company Information, Timezone (`<Select>` populated from `Intl.supportedValuesOf("timeZone")`, no hardcoded list), Business Hours (7-day structured editor, one save per week, not per day), and a logo upload control added to the existing Branding card. Sidebar (`layout.tsx`) now renders the uploaded logo (or an initials-in-circle fallback) before the tenant name.
- **A real bug found and fixed before it could reach CI**: adding the Supabase browser client import to `WorkspaceSettings.tsx` broke the frontend test suite — Vitest doesn't load `apps/web/.env`, so the client's then-eager construction at module-import time threw the moment any test transitively loaded it via `registerAllComponents()`. Fixed at the root (`apps/web/src/lib/supabase-client.ts` is now a lazy singleton behind a `Proxy`, constructed on first real use), not by special-casing the test environment — every existing `supabase.auth.x`/`supabase.storage.x` call site is unchanged.
- **Verified against ground-truth data, not just a green test suite**: full live browser walkthrough (save each card, hard-reload, upload a real image, confirm the sidebar renders it), then the real `tenants` row was queried directly via the elevated DB role to confirm `profile`/`branding.logoUrl` matched exactly what was submitted. One verification-script-only snag, not an app bug: Node's ICU timezone data uses the older alias `Asia/Calcutta`, not `Asia/Kolkata`. Test-generated profile/logo data was cleaned out of the real tenant (`yash-1`) afterward — its pre-existing `accentColor` was left untouched.
- **112 backend tests passing (up from 104), 5 frontend tests passing, full monorepo typecheck/build clean.**

#### Session 2 continued — Company Administration Submodule C: Organization Lifecycle (complete)
- **User approved Submodule A, then requested Submodule C (Organization Lifecycle) next**, going through its own focused plan-mode session (research → written plan at `refactored-finding-alpaca.md` → `ExitPlanMode` approval) before any code, per the standing one-module-at-a-time workflow.
- **Goal: full Company Admin control over department/team structure** — create (already existed), edit, archive, delete, move teams between departments, assign department heads and team managers, designed so a future merge-departments/bulk-transfer feature composes from these primitives rather than needing new ones.
- **Three real design risks identified and resolved during planning, before any code**: (1) team-manager assignment must resolve through `DepartmentTypeRoleLabel` overrides, not just the generic tier role, or an HR-typed department's manager would silently lose HR Manager's real bonus permissions; (2) head/manager assignment must never delegate to `user.assignDepartment`'s own resolver, whose `"own"`-scope check would wrongly reject an Executive assigning within their subtree but outside their exact department; (3) `department.update`'s reparenting needed a cycle guard, since `getDepartmentSubtreeIds`'s recursive CTE had none and a self/descendant reparent would loop forever.
- **New nullable `Department.archivedAt`/`Team.archivedAt`** (migration `org_archived_at`) — reversible exclusion from default listings/pickers, deliberately not a `deletedAt`-style soft delete, since an archived row stays fully real and referenced. **Nine new mutations** in `hr.mutations.ts`: `department.update`, `department.setArchived`, `department.delete`, `team.update`, `team.moveToDepartment`, `team.setArchived`, `team.delete`, `department.assignHead`, `team.assignManager` — all reusing the existing `department:manage` permission, wired into `page.hr`'s actions in `seed.ts`.
- **Deletion is hard-blocked at the application layer, confirmed necessary by reading the actual migration SQL, not assumed**: `teams.department_id` is `ON DELETE RESTRICT`, but `departments.parent_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL` (silently orphans children) and `User.departmentId`/`User.teamId` have no FK at all. `department.delete`/`team.delete` reject with a specific `"Cannot delete: still has N teams, M users. Move or reassign them first."` message rather than a generic conflict.
- **`department.assignHead`/`team.assignManager` resolve roles via new `resolveDepartmentTierRole`** (checks `DepartmentTypeRoleLabel.overrideRoleId` first, falls back to the tenant's plain blueprint role) and do their own direct `tx.user.update` + `isRoleAssignableBy` check rather than delegating — exactly the two risks flagged during planning, both confirmed necessary rather than theoretical.
- **Frontend (`OrgStructure.tsx`)**: `departments` moved from `useDataBinding` to local-state + `callDataSource("departments.list", { includeArchived })`, matching the existing pattern already used for `teams`/`users`/`departmentTypes` in this file. New per-row Edit/Archive/Delete, a "Move to department" select for teams, "Assign head"/"Assign manager" selects, a `showArchived` toggle, and `window.confirm(...)` before deletes (no modal component exists in this codebase yet).
- **⚠️ A genuine, pre-existing production bug found and fixed during this submodule's live browser verification — unrelated to the feature itself, but actively blocking it.** The Org Structure page (and, proven via a control test, a second completely unrelated pre-existing page, `page.team`) rendered with every list permanently empty: the loading promise never settled and zero network requests were ever dispatched. Traced through the API server's own log to `ERR_HTTP_HEADERS_SENT` inside `WorkspaceController` — both `bootstrap()` and `page()` used `@Res({ passthrough: true })` but then manually called `res.status(304).end()` on a cache-hit followed by a bare `return`, so Nest's own response pipeline tried to *also* send a response afterward. React StrictMode's double-effect-invocation in dev makes the repeat-request/same-etag case the common path, not a rare one — this had likely been silently breaking repeat navigation to any page all along, just never diagnosed. **Fixed** by switching both methods to full manual `@Res()` control with an explicit `res.json(...)` call on the success path, removing the ambiguity rather than patching the symptom.
- **Verified end-to-end against the live, fixed API**: minted a real Supabase session for the test account directly via the Admin API (`generate_link` + `verify` with the service-role key) rather than needing its plaintext password, injected it into a Playwright browser's `localStorage`, then drove repeated navigation across `page.hr` → `page.team` → `page.hr` — deliberately re-triggering the exact same-etag repeat-request path the bug lived in. Every request returned a clean `200`; Org Structure rendered real department/team data with every new control present and functional. Delete-blocking, archive round-trip, move-team, and the HR-role-override resolution path were each separately confirmed against the live API with real data earlier in the same pass, then cleaned up — no throwaway data left in the real tenant (`yash-1`).
- **19 new backend tests, 131/131 backend tests passing (up from 112), full monorepo typecheck clean.** `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` confirmed to need no changes — this submodule reuses `department:manage` and the existing tier ladder throughout.
- Docs updated in sync: `CONTEXT.md` §45 (new, Document map renumbered §46), `ARCHITECTURE.md` §6.7 (the `@Res()` bug-fix note), §8.1 (`archived_at` fields + FK-constraint findings), §9.1 (HR module row), this entry.

#### Session 2 continued — Performance Audit & Optimization: measured, fixed, re-measured (complete)
- **User paused the Company Administration roadmap to fix a real "feels slow" complaint**, explicitly asking for measurement first, evidence-based fixes, and a before/after report — a deliberate contrast with the earlier static-code-reading audit (§39/the Obsidian-migration-adjacent session), which produced a ranked hypothesis list but no real numbers and no fixes. Planned via its own dedicated plan-mode session (reusing `refactored-finding-alpaca.md`'s path — Submodule C's plan before it is fully recorded above, not kept as a historical archive).
- **Root cause found by direct measurement, not assumption**: minted a real Supabase session via the Admin API (no password needed) and timed the live dev servers directly. A raw round-trip to the DB (Supabase Postgres, confirmed via the pooler hostname to genuinely be in `ap-northeast-2`/Seoul — real geography, not misconfiguration) costs ~150ms; one `TenantPrismaService.run()` transaction wrapper costs ~600ms end-to-end, measured with a standalone `pg` client bypassing the app entirely. Nearly every endpoint opened 2-3 of these transactions sequentially where one would do — `data-sources.controller.ts`/`mutations.controller.ts` each ran the current-user lookup, the permission resolution, and the actual resolver as three separate transactions; `ConfigEngineService.resolveIdentity()` opened 2 of its own and was called **twice** per bootstrap/page request (once via `getEtag()`'s pre-check, again inside `compileWorkspace`/`compilePage`); `compileWorkspace` then opened 2 more. Bootstrap cache-miss totaled ~7 transactions before touching any real content.
- **Fix: consolidate transaction *count*, not the transaction *model*.** `CurrentUserService`/`PermissionResolverService` gained `*WithTx(tx, ...)` variants sharing an already-open transaction instead of opening their own; `data-sources.controller.ts`/`mutations.controller.ts` now run all three steps as sequential awaits inside one `tenantPrisma.run()` call. `resolveIdentity()`'s 2 internal transactions were merged into 1. **The duplicate `resolveIdentity()` call was eliminated outright** — `getIdentity()` is the one call per request now, its result threaded into `compileWorkspace(identity)`/`compilePage(identity, pageId)` instead of either recomputing it. `compileWorkspace`'s role-labels + feature-flags queries were merged from 2 transactions into 1. No RLS/authorization logic changed anywhere — same `set_config`, same scope checks; only how many separate transactions carry them, and mutations with external I/O inside their own transaction (`user.invite`'s Resend call) are untouched.
- **Client-side ETag caching wired up for the first time**: `apps/web/src/lib/api-client.ts` never sent `If-None-Match` despite full server-side 304 support since Stage 6 — confirmed by reading the file. Added a tiny per-path `(etag, last body)` cache; a `304` now returns the cached body. No explicit invalidation needed — a real content change already produces a different etag, so it naturally falls through to a fresh `200`. `clearManifestCache()` added alongside the existing `clearAccessToken()` on logout/401.
- **Frontend fixes**: `sdui/renderer.tsx`'s `Renderer` wrapped in `React.memo` (keyed on `node` identity) with `renderChild` hoisted to a stable module-level function (previously recreated inline every render, defeating any memoization downstream); `sdui/use-data-binding.ts`'s refetch effect now depends only on the filter values a binding's own `params` actually reference, not the whole page-wide filters object (previously, any one `FilterBar` change re-fetched every binding on the page).
- **DB indexes added** (`Task.assigneeId`, `Project.ownerId`/`departmentId`, `User.departmentId`, migration `perf_add_missing_indexes`) — confirmed genuinely missing by reading the schema. Explicitly won't move any number at today's real-tenant row counts; added anyway as a zero-risk, zero-downtime migration.
- **`Chart` (recharts) switched to `next/dynamic`** in `sdui/primitives/index.ts` — measured via a real `next build` before/after to have negligible actual effect (First Load JS was already ~103-105 kB either way), contradicting the earlier static audit's emphasis on bundle size. Kept as the more correct approach regardless, reported honestly as not a measured win.
- **Before/after (same live servers, same real tenant, cold vs. cold, warm vs. warm)**: bootstrap cold **6.0s → 4.2s (-29%)**; `page.hr` cold **4.5s → 2.5s (-45%)**; `page.team` cold **4.4s → 2.9s (-33%)**; data-source calls (`departments.list`, `teams.list`, `users.list`, `projects.list`, `tasks.list`) down **12-39%** each. **The already-cheap `304` pre-check path showed no clear net change** (~2.3s before and after) — reported honestly rather than rounded up, since merging its 2 sub-queries into 1 transaction also removed the parallelism they used to get from running alongside the root blueprint-meta query.
- **⚠️ A real finding this pass did not fix, flagged rather than silently dropped**: a real in-browser client-side navigation to `page.hr` still took ~7-7.5s end-to-end even after every fix above, because `OrgStructure` fires ~4 concurrent data-source calls on mount and — unlike a controlled direct-fetch test of the same 4 calls (which completed in ~2.9s, correctly bounded by the slowest one) — the real browser number sits much closer to their *sum*. Candidates not isolated further this pass: React StrictMode's dev-only double-effect-invocation roughly doubling concurrent load, and/or DB pool contention under that doubled load. Batching a composite's data-source fan-out and any change to the connection-pooling model itself were explicitly out of scope for this pass per the approved plan.
- **Verified against the real app**: full backend suite green throughout (131/131, no logic changed — a call-shape refactor), full monorepo typecheck clean, 5/5 frontend tests green (one transient worker-pool crash on a first run, not reproducible on re-run, unrelated flakiness). A real browser session round-tripped through repeated `page.hr` ↔ `page.team` navigation and a final screenshot confirmed Org Structure renders real data with every control intact — no regression.
- **Superseded the earlier static-reading performance audit** (its findings list is now marked accordingly in `CONTEXT.md` §39) — two of its findings turned out to be the real dominant cost (confirmed), one turned out to have negligible real impact (bundle size), and two remain genuinely un-actioned (`compilePage`'s full-blueprint pruning cost, per-composite data-source fan-out) rather than silently dropped.
- Docs updated in sync: `CONTEXT.md` §47 (new, Document map renumbered §48, §39 marked superseded), `ARCHITECTURE.md` §6.7 (client-cache note), §8.1-8.2 (new indexes), §8.3 (the cross-boundary transaction-consolidation pattern), this entry. `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` untouched — nothing here changes the authority model.

#### Session 2 continued — Second Performance Pass: architecture-level fixes (complete)
- **User reported the app was "definitely faster" after the first pass but still not "instant and fluid," and asked for a second, deeper pass** — re-profile from scratch, don't assume the architecture is already right, and be open to real architectural changes if the evidence supports them.
- **⚠️ The headline finding, and the dominant fix of this entire pass: sidebar navigation was doing a full browser page reload on every click — this app had been behaving like a multi-page site for its primary navigation path this whole time, not a SPA.** Proven directly: a `window.__marker` global set after landing on a page vanished after clicking a sidebar link (only a hard navigation tears down `window`). Root cause: `next/link` was imported **nowhere** in the codebase — `WorkspaceSidebar.tsx`'s nav items were plain `<a href>` tags. Every click refetched `bootstrap`/`notifications.unreadCount` and remounted the entire app from scratch — measured at **~5.8-5.9s for `page.hr`, ~2.2-2.4s for `page.team`, on every click, including revisits**, against a production build (not a dev-mode StrictMode artifact). The app already had a working SPA path (`CommandPalette`, `QuickActions`, both via `router.push()`) — it just wasn't the one most people click. **Fixed**: sidebar leaf links now render `<Link href>` — same markup, real client-side transitions, automatic route-JS prefetch.
- **Two context providers (`RenderContextProvider`/`BootstrapContextProvider`) were passing a fresh object literal as `value` on every render**, causing every consumer anywhere in the tree to re-render on any unrelated layout state change. Fixed with `useMemo`/`useCallback`, placed before the component's early returns per the Rules of Hooks.
- **Adopted TanStack Query as the client-side data-cache layer underneath `useDataBinding` — the real architectural decision this pass made.** Only page *structure* was cached before this (pass 1's manifest ETag cache); the actual data underneath had zero caching, zero cross-component dedup, zero stale-while-revalidate. `useDataBinding`'s external contract stayed the same (`{data, loading, error, refetch}`, plus an additive `queryKey`), so every primitive needed zero changes. Chosen explicitly over hand-rolling a second cache: request dedup and SWR staleness are exactly the kind of thing a dedicated library gets right that a second hand-rolled cache would likely get subtly wrong (race conditions on rapid navigation, in particular).
- **Extended the same cache to composites, not just simple bound primitives — found necessary during this pass, not originally scoped.** `OrgStructure` bypassed `useDataBinding` entirely (4 hand-rolled fetches); `ProjectBoard`/`TaskList`/`TeamMembers` each had secondary ad hoc reference-data fetches alongside their primary bound one. All migrated onto a new shared `useDataSourceQuery(source, params)` hook (same query-key scheme, so a composite and a bound primitive asking for the same source+params now share one cache entry) — this is what finally closes the original static audit's long-flagged, never-fixed "duplicate ad hoc reference-data fetches across composites" finding.
- **New `POST /api/data/batch`**: several sources in one HTTP round trip, one transaction (identical consolidation pattern to pass 1's single-source route) — must be registered before the single-source `:source` route in the controller, or Nest's router matches `batch` as a source name (verified live). `OrgStructure` (4 sources) migrated onto it via a new `useDataSourceBatchQuery` hook, which also seeds each result into the individual per-source cache. Measured: **4 sources together ~2.6s, down from ~7-8s summed as 4 separate calls.**
- **Hover-intent prefetch** on sidebar links (`onMouseEnter` → fetch the target page's tree, walk it for blueprint-bound primitives' data, prefetch those too) — doesn't reach a composite's own internal reference-data needs, a disclosed limitation, not a bug.
- **⚠️ A real bug this pass's own measurement caught, not assumed**: a genuine click is preceded by a hover in the real DOM event sequence, so the new hover-prefetch and the actual navigation's fetch to the identical page-tree path could both fire before either resolved — `page.hr` briefly regressed to ~3.6-3.9s after adding prefetch (from ~1.9-2.3s once actually fixed). Fixed generally in `api-client.ts`: an in-flight request to the same path is now awaited and shared instead of a second one firing. Exactly the kind of bug the "measure again after every change" discipline exists to catch before it ships invisibly.
- **Backend: `compilePage`/`compileWorkspace` no longer prune every page in the blueprint for a request that only ever needs one** (flagged in pass 1, fixed this pass). `pruneByPermissions` gained an optional `onlyPageId`; `ConfigEngineService` split into an unpruned `resolveEntitledBlueprint` step (so `compileWorkspace` can read `dashboards.default` — untouched by pruning — before pruning even runs) plus an explicit prune call at each of the two real call sites. 2 new unit tests; **133 backend tests passing (up from 131)**.
- **Optimistic UI for two representative mutations, not a rewrite of every mutation site**: `TaskList`'s status dropdown and `ProjectBoard`'s member-toggle checkbox both patch the query cache immediately (`queryClient.setQueryData` via the binding's own now-exposed `queryKey`), roll back on error, reconcile via the existing `refetch()` either way. A third candidate (`department.setArchived`) was considered and explicitly not done — its data lives in the batch query's combined cache entry, and patching that correctly needs cross-component prop-plumbing disproportionate to what two clean examples already prove.
- **`List` virtualization (`react-window` v2), gated above a 50-row threshold** — inert at every real tenant's current data volume, verified instead with synthetic data (a new `List.test.tsx`, 200 rows via a `{const}` binding) rather than polluting a real tenant with 50+ throwaway rows. Caught and fixed a real accessibility gap in the same pass: the first implementation forgot to spread react-window's `ariaAttributes` onto the row. **`Table` deliberately left unvirtualized** — a real `<table>`'s markup doesn't compose with react-window's positioned-row model without a structural rewrite, and there was no safe way to verify that rewrite against real row counts. Disclosed, not silently skipped.
- **A real, unrelated environment bug found and fixed**: adding `@tanstack/react-query` made Vitest's default `forks` pool crash outright (zero tests run, no useful error) — reproduced reliably, gone under `pool: "threads"` in `vitest.config.ts`. An environment quirk of the fork pool under the heavier dependency tree, not an app or test bug.
- **Before/after, same real account, production build both times, cold vs. cold**: `page.hr` navigation **~5.8-5.9s (full reload) → ~1.9-2.3s (real SPA transition)**; `page.team` navigation **~2.2-2.4s → ~65-99ms**; `bootstrap`/`notifications` **refetched on every click before → never refetched on in-app navigation now** (the layout genuinely stays mounted); `OrgStructure`'s 4 sources **~7-8s summed → ~2.6s in one batch**.
- **Verified against the real, running app**: the window-marker test (proves real SPA transitions), a full functional walkthrough (repeat navigation; the "Show archived" toggle correctly firing exactly one fresh `/api/data/batch` call with the updated param, confirmed live), and a final screenshot confirming Org Structure still renders real data with every control intact. Full backend suite green (133/133), full monorepo typecheck clean, frontend suite green (7/7, up from 5).
- **Environmental note, not a code finding**: hit the already-documented `nest start --watch` stale-server quirk again mid-pass (a transient 500, gone after a manual restart) — consistent with its existing documented pattern, not a regression.
- **Genuinely left open, stated plainly**: `Table` virtualization; a composite's internal reference-data needs still aren't reachable by hover-prefetch; `department.setArchived`'s optimistic UI (deliberately deferred); and the DB-latency floor itself (~150ms/round-trip to the Seoul-region Supabase project, pass 1's finding) — no amount of client-side caching removes that floor for a genuinely cold fetch, it only avoids re-paying it for data already in hand.
- Docs updated in sync: `CONTEXT.md` §48 (new, Document map renumbered §49), `ARCHITECTURE.md` §7.3 (TanStack Query), §7.5 (Renderer memo note, carried over from pass 1 and never previously recorded here), §7.8 (the `next/link` fix), §6.5 (single-page pruning), §7.1 (`List` virtualization), §8.3 (the batch endpoint), this entry.

Same per-submodule workflow reaffirmed as standing: research → plan → approval → implement → test/optimize → document → move to the next submodule only once approved.

#### Session 2 continued — Phase 1, Submodule 1: Comments & Mentions (complete)
- **First submodule of the approved Core Workspace Modules roadmap.** Went through its own focused plan-mode session (research confirming no `Comment`/`Mention` model, table, data source, mutation, or UI existed anywhere in the codebase → written plan → `ExitPlanMode` approval) before any code, per the standing workflow.
- **New `Comment`/`CommentMention` models** (`comment.prisma`) — `entityType` ("project" | "task", free-text vocabulary, same treatment as `Task.status`) + `entityId` with no FK, the same no-FK precedent already accepted for `User.departmentId`/`teamId`/`managerId`. Soft-delete (`deletedAt`), matching `Project`/`Task`.
- **No new permission triples.** `comment.create`/`comments.list` set no static `requiredPermission`; a new `assertCommentTargetInScope(tx, ctx, entityType, entityId)` reuses the existing, already-correct `projectsWhere`/`tasksWhere` helpers directly (not duplicated) to confirm the specific target row is in the actor's scope — the same "bespoke inline scope check, no static `requiredPermission`" idiom `department.create`/`team.create` already established, extended to a case the generic dispatcher can't express since the correct permission depends on a runtime `entityType`. `comment.delete` is ownership-only, zero permission gate, mirroring `notification.markRead`'s precedent.
- **Mentions are client-resolved, not server-parsed from free text**: `mentionedUserIds: string[]` input, validated server-side against the target's own project membership (a task inherits its project's membership), generalizing `assertAssigneeIsProjectMember`'s existing concept to a set check (`resolveValidMentions`, pure and unit-tested). Invalid ids and self-mentions are silently dropped (the latter reusing `notificationRecipientFor`'s "telling someone about their own action isn't a notification" reasoning). One `Notification` per valid mention (`type: "comment.mention"`), created in the same transaction as the comment — the first extension of the `Notification` model beyond Tasks since it was built.
- **Frontend: no new blueprint-registered primitive** — a per-row expandable thread can't be expressed as a static blueprint node, so `CommentThread.tsx` is a plain shared component (the same "internal sub-component, never separately registered" precedent as `OrgStructure.tsx`'s list-item components), mounted per-row behind an expand toggle in `TaskList.tsx`/`ProjectBoard.tsx`, using `useDataSourceQuery` (the cached/deduped pattern from the second performance pass). `mentionCandidates` in both composites come from data already fetched for other purposes — no new network request introduced.
- **19 new backend tests, 146/146 backend tests passing, full monorepo typecheck clean, 7/7 frontend tests passing** (unchanged — verified live instead of adding new frontend tests this submodule).
- **Verified end-to-end against the live, real app**: direct authenticated API calls (comment creation + mention notification delivery + self-mention correctly dropped, ownership-based delete rejection and success, list exclusion after delete) plus a full real Playwright browser walkthrough (toggle → post → visible with author/timestamp → delete icon shown only for the author), all against the real tenant (`yash-1`) with cleanup afterward.
- **⚠️ A real environmental finding, unrelated to the feature's correctness**: hit genuine Supavisor connection-pool exhaustion (`P2028`) during verification, root-caused to multiple orphaned `nest start --watch`/`next dev` processes accumulated across this long session's earlier restarts, each still holding its own live Prisma connection pool despite no longer owning a bound port. Fixed by enumerating actual process command lines (not just listening ports) and killing every stray instance. Lesson recorded for future long sessions: verify old dev-server instances actually exited via full process-list inspection, not just that a new one is listening.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change — this submodule reuses `project:read`/`task:read` scope logic verbatim, no authority-model impact.
- Docs updated in sync: `CONTEXT.md` §49 (new, Document map renumbered §50), `ARCHITECTURE.md` §8.2 (new tables), §9.1 (new module row), this entry.

#### Session 2 continued — Phase 1, Submodule 2: Channels & Direct Messages (complete)
- **User approved starting Submodule 2 next**, going through its own focused plan-mode session (research → written plan → `ExitPlanMode` approval) before any code, per the standing workflow. Unlike Comments & Mentions, this submodule needed genuinely new infrastructure — the first use of Supabase Realtime anywhere in this codebase — and it surfaced a real, multi-layered chain of findings, most caught by this submodule's own live verification rather than assumed away.
- **One unified `Conversation` model for both Channels and DMs** (`type: "channel" | "dm"`), `ConversationMember` (membership + `lastReadAt`), and soft-deleted `Message` — mirrors Slack/Discord's own internal shape rather than two parallel schemas. Membership is the entire scope model, no new permission triples: `assertConversationMember` mirrors Comments' `assertCommentTargetInScope` existence-then-scope shape; `conversation.addMember` deliberately handles both "invite someone else" (requires existing membership) and "self-join a public channel" (no gate) in one mutation; `conversation.createDm` is idempotent; `conversation.archive`/`message.delete` are creator/author-only.
- **⚠️ Decision made and disclosed, not silently assumed: private channels are not visible to Company Admins who aren't members** — same strict model as Slack/Teams. Deferred to a possible future Audit/Compliance submodule rather than built speculatively.
- **⚠️ Real bug caught by two truly concurrent requests during this submodule's own verification: a check-then-create race in `conversation.addMember`'s self-join.** A double-click self-join 500'd on a raw unique-constraint violation — and, surprisingly, **Prisma 7's own `upsert` threw the identical raw error under genuine concurrency** rather than compiling to an atomic statement, while a caught-exception recovery attempt also failed, since Postgres aborts the whole transaction the instant one statement in it errors (`25P02`) — a followup query on that same transaction can't succeed either. **Fixed with raw `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING *`** (`tx.$queryRaw`) — genuinely atomic, verified against 5 truly concurrent requests resolving to the identical row.
- **The real architectural wrinkle flagged in the approved plan, confirmed necessary: a second, JWT-based `realtime_membership` RLS policy** alongside every table's existing GUC-based `tenant_isolation` policy, since the browser's Realtime connection never sets the `app.tenant_id` session GUC our backend uses. Uses the `tenant_id` claim `custom_access_token_hook` already stamps on every JWT.
- **⚠️ That same policy introduced a second bug: infinite recursion.** `conversation_members`' own policy queried `conversation_members` from inside its own `EXISTS` — self-referencing RLS, which Postgres rejects (`42P17`) the instant anything touches the table. **Fixed with a `SECURITY DEFINER` helper function** (`is_conversation_member`, same technique as `custom_access_token_hook`) whose internal query bypasses RLS, breaking the recursion.
- **⚠️ A third infrastructure finding: every table in this project had only ever been granted to the custom `app_runtime` role, never Supabase's built-in `authenticated` role** — Realtime evaluates a subscriber's filter as `authenticated` (mirroring PostgREST), and with zero privileges there, subscribing failed with a deeply misleading `P0001 invalid column for filter` error. **Fixed with `GRANT SELECT ... TO authenticated`.**
- **⚠️⚠️ The most significant finding: even with all three fixes above, `INSERT` `postgres_changes` events are never delivered by this Supabase project's Realtime service — confirmed by exhaustive elimination, not assumed.** A bare Node client (no React/browser involved) with a fresh JWT, RLS temporarily fully open (`USING (true)`), `REPLICA IDENTITY FULL`, and a 100+ second wait all still received nothing on INSERT — while `DELETE` events on the same tables, same mechanics, delivered correctly, proving the pipeline works end-to-end for at least one event type and ruling out the websocket/auth/topic layer wholesale. This is an external, disclosed limitation of this environment, not an app bug, and not fixable from application code or SQL alone. **Fixed the actual user-facing problem, not just documented it**: `messages.list` now polls (`refetchInterval: 4000`) while a conversation is open — this is the real delivery guarantee; the Realtime subscription stays wired up as a non-load-bearing best-effort accelerator, verified live to correctly deliver within the poll window in both directions across two real browser sessions.
- **A related, pre-existing RLS gap found while reading every migration for this plan, fixed as a bundled prerequisite**: `comments`, `comment_mentions`, and `department_type_role_labels` never got the standard `tenant_isolation` policy when created — a silent drift from the documented "every module table carries `tenant_id` + RLS" invariant, closed before three more new tables joined them.
- **New `nav.chat`/`page.chat`** (no placeholder existed before, unlike `nav.meetings`'s "coming soon" stub) — visible to every tenant member, no `requiredPermission`. `ChatWorkspace` is a blueprint-registered composite (unlike `CommentThread`), since a whole chat page is a real top-level page, not a per-row expansion. First real cursor pagination in this codebase (`messages.list`'s `before` param).
- **20 new backend tests, 166/166 backend tests passing (up from 146), full monorepo typecheck clean, 7/7 frontend tests passing** (unchanged — verified live instead, same precedent as Comments & Mentions).
- **Verified end-to-end against the live, real app**: direct API calls (channel create/join/browse, private-channel 403s on both read and self-join, DM idempotency, mention notifications, unread-count drop on markRead, ownership-gated delete) plus a full two-real-browser-session Playwright walkthrough proving actual cross-session delivery in both directions. This submodule's schema/infrastructure-heavy debugging generated an unusually large number of throwaway test channels across many iterations — all confirmed deleted outright (not just archived) from the real tenant (`yash-1`) afterward; `SELECT count(*)` on all three chat tables returned 0 before finishing.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change. Docs updated in sync: `CONTEXT.md` §50 (new, Document map renumbered §51), `ARCHITECTURE.md` §4 (the GUC-vs-JWT RLS split finally documented, plus the new Realtime-policy pattern and disclosed limitation), §8.2 (new tables), §9.1 (new module row), this entry.

#### Session 2 continued — Phase 1, Submodule 3: Meetings (complete)
- **Third submodule of the Core Workspace Modules roadmap** — scheduled meetings + live video calls, replacing the `nav.meetings`/`page.meetings` "coming soon" `EmptyState` placeholder. Went through the same research → plan → `ExitPlanMode` approval → implement → test → document cycle as every submodule before it, including two `AskUserQuestion` rounds: video vendor (**Daily.co**, chosen over LiveKit/Jitsi/custom WebRTC — hosted rooms + a prebuilt embeddable call UI, fastest integration for a solo developer) and two implementation forks surfaced by the design pass (extend the mutation framework properly vs. mirror the `user.invite` stopgap — user chose to extend it properly; strict `projectsWhere`-style visibility vs. participant-floor-always-on — user chose the floor).
- **⚠️ Closed a previously-flagged architectural gap, not worked around a second time: `MutationDefinition` gained optional `preResolve`/`rollbackPreResolve` hooks** (`mutation-registry.service.ts`/`mutations.controller.ts`). Every registered mutation used to run entirely inside one `tenantPrisma.run()` transaction with no way to do "external I/O, *then* open the transaction, roll back the I/O if it fails" — exactly the gap `tenant-prisma.service.ts`'s own comment named as "a genuine follow-up" after `user.invite`'s Resend call inside a transaction once blew a 5s timeout. Now: Daily's room-creation HTTP call runs *before* the transaction opens, and rolls back (best-effort, logged, never rethrows) if the transaction subsequently fails — mirroring `AuthService.signup`'s Supabase-Auth-user rollback. Fully backward compatible (`pre` is optional at the type level purely so every existing `resolve(input, ctx, tx)` call site across every `*.mutations.spec.ts` file keeps compiling unchanged); `MutationRegistry.register` had to become generic over `Pre` too, not just `I` — `rollbackPreResolve`'s parameter is contravariant, and the original signature forced `Pre` to `unknown` at every call site, rejecting Meetings' real `CreatePre` type. New `mutations.controller.spec.ts` covers the wiring directly.
- **New `Meeting`/`MeetingParticipant` models** — `departmentId` (nullable, mirrors `Project.departmentId`, visibility-widening only, never a hard gate), `cancelledAt` (binary, mirrors `Conversation.archivedAt`), `dailyRoomName`/`dailyRoomUrl`. `MeetingParticipant` mirrors `ProjectMember` exactly. **Standard `tenant_isolation` RLS policy shipped in the same migration as the tables** — deliberately avoiding the earlier gap where comments/chat tables briefly went live without it. No Realtime policies/grants needed — Daily owns live in-call state, not this DB.
- **Three authorization shapes for three different questions**: *who may schedule, and how broadly* is RBAC-shaped (`meeting:create:<scope>`, mirroring `project:create`'s exact tier progression — first appears at Lead/`team` scope); *who sees a meeting in their own list* is **participant-floor-always-on** (even an Intern with zero `meeting:read` grant always sees a meeting they organize or were personally invited to — a deliberate divergence from `projectsWhere`'s stricter shape, confirmed with the user); *who can join a specific meeting* is membership-only (`assertMeetingParticipant`, exact mirror of `assertConversationMember`).
- **⚠️ Two real bugs found and fixed during implementation/verification, not silently assumed away:**
  1. `users.list` (reused by `ProjectBoard`'s member picker) is gated on `user:manage`, which Lead/Manager tiers never hold — even though they DO hold `meeting:create`. Reusing it for the participant picker would have left the exact tiers this feature targets unable to invite anyone through the UI. **Fixed with a new `meetings.inviteCandidates` data source, gated on `meeting:create` instead** (no new permission triple) — `users.list`/`ProjectBoard` untouched.
  2. Action-level permission pruning reads a static `requiredPermission` field directly off each blueprint `ActionSpec` node — it does **not** dynamically look up the mutation's own registered gate. The first cut of `page.meetings`'s `meeting.create` action omitted this field; server-side authorization was already fully correct (confirmed via `/me/permissions`), but the compiled manifest showed "Schedule meeting" to every role regardless of grant — a "prune, not hide" violation, caught live during this submodule's own scripted verification. **Fixed** by adding `requiredPermission: "meeting:create"` to that one action entry, mirroring `tenant.createLogoUploadUrl`'s existing precedent.
- **"Start now" is not a separate mechanism** — the frontend's quick-start button calls the identical `meeting.create` mutation with `scheduledStart` set to now. **Scope kept deliberately tight for v1**: no recurring meetings, no scheduled reminder notifications (an immediate `meeting.invited` notification fires on invite; a real "starting soon" reminder needs `@nestjs/schedule`, earmarked for a later Phase 3 submodule, not pulled forward), no in-meeting text chat (Chat's `Conversation`/`Message` system not wired in this round).
- **Call view**: a full-viewport overlay rendered inside `MeetingsWorkspace` (`DailyCallFrame.tsx`, `@daily-co/daily-js`'s prebuilt-UI mode, no custom call UI), not a dedicated route or new Modal primitive.
- **31 new backend tests, 197/197 backend tests passing (up from 166), full monorepo typecheck and build clean.** No new frontend tests this submodule (verified live instead, same precedent as Comments & Mentions/Chat).
- **Verified end-to-end against a live, throwaway tenant** (scripted, never the real Admin's password): fresh signup confirms `page.meetings` compiles to the real `MeetingsWorkspace` node; a Lead-tier invitee's compiled manifest correctly includes `meeting.create` post-fix, a Practitioner-tier invitee's correctly prunes it post-fix; `meetings.inviteCandidates` succeeds for the Lead and 403s for the Practitioner; `meetings.list` returns `[]` cleanly for both grant levels with zero meetings. Throwaway tenant/users/department deleted afterward. **⚠️ Disclosed, not silently skipped: no real `DAILY_API_KEY` was available in this environment**, so actual room creation, `meeting.getJoinInfo` token issuance, `meeting.cancel`'s room cleanup, cross-user participant-visibility/notification firing, and the Playwright call-view walkthrough are unverified against the live Daily API — confirmed instead that `preResolve` genuinely reaches Daily's API (fails cleanly on an invalid key, before any DB write) and that the failed attempt writes zero `Meeting` rows. **Complete this verification once the key is provided.**
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change. Docs updated in sync: `CONTEXT.md` §51 (new, Document map renumbered §52), this entry.

#### Session 2 continued — Meetings: video vendor swap, Daily.co → self-hosted Jitsi (complete, live verification pending Docker)
- **Right after Meetings shipped on Daily.co, the user asked to swap to a genuinely $0 option for now, with an explicit stated intent to integrate "the best" vendor again later** — a deliberate, temporary stopgap, not a reversal of the architecture. Two follow-up decisions confirmed live: Jitsi's free public server (`meet.jit.si`) was ruled out (research confirmed it caps embedded sessions at 5 minutes — not usable for real meetings); **self-hosted Jitsi via `docker-jitsi-meet`, running locally via Docker Compose, for local dev only**, was chosen over provisioning a cloud VPS. Went through its own dedicated plan-mode session (a Plan agent live-fetched `@jitsi/react-sdk`'s README and `docker-jitsi-meet`'s handbook to verify assumptions rather than guess) before any code, per the standing workflow.
- **⚠️ Docker is not installed in this environment — a real, disclosed blocker, not silently worked around.** All code shipped and passes typecheck/test/build; JWT token signing was verified locally via a throwaway script (decoded and inspected the exact claim shape). Actually starting the Jitsi containers, and any live join/audio/video verification, remains blocked until Docker Desktop is installed — `infra/jitsi/README.md` documents the exact remaining steps (everything up through downloading and configuring `docker-jitsi-meet`'s own `.env` is already done).
- **A genuinely useful side effect: `meeting.create`/`meeting.cancel` got simpler, not more complex.** Self-hosted Jitsi has no "create a room via REST API" step at all — a room exists implicitly the moment someone joins it — so both mutations reverted from factories using the `preResolve`/`rollbackPreResolve` hooks back to plain constants with zero external-service dependency. Those framework hooks themselves were deliberately left in place, untouched, in `mutation-registry.service.ts`/`mutations.controller.ts` — general-purpose infrastructure for whatever future mutation needs "external I/O before the transaction" next, not ripped out just because Daily.co (their original motivating case) is gone.
- **Schema made vendor-neutral, not just Daily→Jitsi-specific**, anticipating the user's own stated "swap again later" plan: `dailyRoomName`/`dailyRoomUrl` collapsed into a single `videoRoomName` (migration: rename + drop column) — no stored URL at all, since a join URL is always computable as `{domain}/{roomName}` from one shared env var.
- **New `JitsiService`** (replaces `DailyService`) has one method, signing an HS256 JWT locally via `jose`'s `SignJWT` — the same package already used elsewhere in this codebase to *verify* Supabase JWTs, so no new dependency, and no HTTP call at all (a real simplification vs. Daily's REST wrapper).
- **New `infra/jitsi/` directory**: "ours" (README, a PowerShell script downloading the latest tagged `docker-jitsi-meet` release — explicitly not a `git clone`, per Jitsi's own guidance) kept cleanly separate from "theirs" (the downloaded release itself, gitignored). JWT auth enabled server-side (`ENABLE_AUTH=1`, `AUTH_TYPE=jwt`, `ENABLE_GUESTS=0`) — every join requires a valid token from our own `meeting.getJoinInfo`, no guest/lobby tier.
- **⚠️ A networking assumption from planning was corrected during setup, not silently carried forward**: Jitsi's own `env.example` documents that plain HTTP (`:8000`) redirects to HTTPS (`:8443`) by default, contradicting the initial "localhost is a secure context over HTTP" reasoning. `NEXT_PUBLIC_JITSI_DOMAIN` now points at `:8443`; each browser profile will need to click through the self-signed certificate warning once.
- **Real, disclosed risks flagged rather than asserted as solved** (documented in `infra/jitsi/README.md`): JWT auth setup on `docker-jitsi-meet` has a documented history of genuine multi-day community friction; the `sub` claim value (`"*"` vs. the real domain) is unverified against a live server; moderator-flag placement has known version-dependent quirks. None resolved yet — exactly what live verification needs to confirm once Docker is available.
- **2 net tests removed** from `meetings.mutations.spec.ts` (the Daily-specific `preResolve`/`rollbackPreResolve` tests for `meeting.create`, no longer applicable), remaining tests updated for the new shapes. **195/195 backend tests passing** (down from 197, expected), full monorepo typecheck and build clean. `meetings.data-sources.spec.ts` and `mutations.controller.spec.ts` needed zero changes.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change. Docs updated in sync: `CONTEXT.md` §52 (new, Document map renumbered §53), this entry.

#### Session 2 continued — Jitsi live verification: infra + backend confirmed, browser check still needed (complete as far as this environment allows)
- **User installed Docker Desktop**; asked for full end-to-end verification (containers healthy, Jitsi loads, two browsers can join, audio/video + moderator controls work, JWT auth enforced, unauthorized users rejected) and to update docs once done.
- **Started the stack, confirmed genuinely healthy**: `docker compose ps` — all 4 containers (`prosody`, `jicofo`, `jvb`, `web`) up, stable, zero errors in any container's logs after 20+ minutes of runtime. Read the *generated* Prosody config directly (not just the input `.env`) and confirmed it matches the design exactly: `authentication = "token"`, `app_id`/`app_secret` identical to this repo's root `.env`, `asap_accepted_issuers`/`asap_accepted_audiences` set, `allow_empty_token = false`. `enable_domain_verification = false` was also found here — this retroactively resolves the earlier-flagged "is the `sub` claim's value correct" uncertainty; it isn't checked by this deployment at all.
- **⚠️ Corrected a networking assumption a second time, based on live evidence, not documentation**: the prior entry's `NEXT_PUBLIC_JITSI_DOMAIN=localhost:8443` (self-signed HTTPS) was itself based on `env.example`'s comment claiming `:8000` redirects to HTTPS. Live-tested: `curl http://localhost:8000/` returns the real Jitsi Meet page directly, `200 OK`, no `Location` header — the comment doesn't describe this deployment's actual behavior. Reverted to `NEXT_PUBLIC_JITSI_DOMAIN=localhost:8000` — simpler, no certificate-warning click-through needed, and correct per the original (pre-research) reasoning that `localhost` is always a browser-trusted secure context.
- **Restarted both dev servers** to pick up `JWT_APP_ID`/`JWT_APP_SECRET` (added since they last booted) and the corrected `NEXT_PUBLIC_JITSI_DOMAIN`.
- **Ran a full scripted backend pass of the entire Meetings flow against the live Jitsi server** (throwaway tenant + Lead + participant + a genuinely separate cross-tenant outsider, cleaned up afterward): `meeting.create` generates a real room name; `meeting.getJoinInfo` mints real signed tokens for both the organizer and the participant, decoded and checked (correct `room` claim, `context.user.moderator` correctly `true` for the organizer and `false` for the participant, `iss`/`aud` matching the deployed secret exactly); `meetings.list` visibility (participant floor) confirmed for both; `meeting.invited` notification fired for the participant only (self-exclusion confirmed); the outsider's `meeting.getJoinInfo` attempt correctly 404'd; `meeting.cancel` succeeded and a subsequent join attempt correctly 404'd.
- **⚠️ Explicitly did not attempt a hand-rolled XMPP/WebSocket auth test, and searched twice (not once) for a browser-automation tool before concluding none exists in this environment.** Real protocol-level "does Jitsi's server accept a valid token and reject an invalid one" happens at the MUC-room-join step over the XMPP/WebRTC wire — reproducing that correctly without either a real browser or exact, verified knowledge of Jitsi's client-side handshake risks producing a test that's wrong in a way that looks like a pass or fail for the wrong reason. Judged the risk of a misleading home-grown test worse than clearly disclosing the gap — consistent with this project's own standing practice of saying "not verified here" rather than fabricating confidence.
- **What remains unverified by me, and cannot be, without browser tooling**: real audio/video flow between two participants, the visual moderator-vs-participant UI difference, and true server-side JWT accept/reject at the protocol level. **`infra/jitsi/README.md` now has a ~5-minute manual checklist** (7 concrete steps, including an explicit unauthorized-access check: hitting the raw Jitsi room URL with no token and confirming it's blocked) for the user to close this gap themselves.
- Docs updated in sync: `CONTEXT.md` §52 (amended in place with the verification results — no new section, since this is completing the prior entry's disclosed gap, not new architecture), `infra/jitsi/README.md` (new "Status" and "Manual verification" sections), this entry.

#### Session 2 continued — Phase 1, Submodule 4: Announcements (complete)
- **Fourth submodule of the Core Workspace Modules roadmap** — a department- or company-wide broadcast feed, replacing nothing (no placeholder existed; confirmed via grep before starting). Went through its own focused plan-mode session (research → written plan, including two `AskUserQuestion` rounds — which submodule goes next, since the roadmap doc itself was unrecoverable this session, and posting authority tier — → `ExitPlanMode` approval) before any code, per the standing workflow, while the Supabase-project-pause issue from the prior health-check session was deliberately deferred per the user's own explicit instruction ("we will check that afterwards, do the next task").
- **Posting authority starts at Department Head, not Lead** (confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`) — an announcement is an official broadcast, not team chat, so no Lead/Manager/Practitioner/Intern grant exists at all. `announcement:create:department` (Department Head) → `:department-subtree` (Executive) → `:tenant` (Company Admin, plus HR Manager as a judgment call flagged during planning: HR already holds tenant-wide `user:manage`/`department:manage`, and company-wide HR broadcasts are exactly its use case).
- **New RBAC helper, `getDepartmentAncestorIds`** — the read-side inverse of `getDepartmentSubtreeIds` (§29/§31), walking `Department.parentId` up instead of down via the same recursive-CTE shape. Resolving the *reader's* own ancestor chain once per request turns read visibility into one `WHERE department_id IS NULL OR department_id = ANY(ancestorIds)` check — an announcement targeting department D is visible to D and everything below it, since D necessarily appears in any descendant reader's own chain.
- **Notifications fire as one batched `notification.createMany` call, never a sequential loop** — the first submodule where this actually mattered in practice, not just in principle: an announcement's audience can be a whole department subtree or the entire tenant, unlike every prior notification call site's small hand-picked list, and this codebase has a real precedent bug (`materializeDepartmentTypeLabels`'s 47 sequential inserts once blowing a 20s transaction timeout) that made the batched choice non-negotiable.
- **`announcement.delete` (author-only soft delete) shipped in v1, not deferred** — every other content-creation feature here ships a way to retract a mistake (`comment.delete`, `message.delete`, `meeting.cancel`); admin-can-delete-anyone's-post (moderation) was considered and explicitly deferred.
- **The permission-pruning lesson from Meetings (§51/the entry above) was applied proactively this time** — `page.announcements`'s `announcement.create` action carried `requiredPermission: "announcement:create"` from the first draft, not discovered live a second time.
- **26 new backend tests, 214/214 backend tests passing (up from 195), full monorepo typecheck/test/build clean.**
- **Verified end-to-end against a live, throwaway tenant** (scripted, never the real Admin's password, cleaned up after): a real department tree (Root → Child → Grandchild, plus Sibling under Root) with a Department Head, Executive, and two Practitioners invited and activated through the **real first-login password-change flow** (`/auth/complete-first-login`) — not just signed in, matching what an actually-invited user experiences. **19/19 checks passed**: action-level pruning, all three creation-tier scope boundaries (including the implicit-tenant-wide-rejection case), bidirectional ancestor-chain read visibility, notification batching confirmed by direct DB query (exact recipients, not just "some rows exist"), and delete ownership.
- **⚠️ One live-verification finding worth recording, not a code bug**: the first verification pass put the Executive at the tree's own Root (matching the plan's literal scenario text) and expected posting to `Sibling` to be rejected as "outside the subtree" — but `Sibling` is a direct child of `Root`, so it's genuinely inside Root's subtree, and the backend correctly allowed it. The scope logic was right; the test scenario's own department placement was internally inconsistent (an actor sitting at the tree's root has no "outside" within that tree). Fixed by moving the Executive to `Child` instead, where the subtree (`{Child, Grandchild}`) genuinely excludes `Sibling`.
- **Supabase connection flakiness recurred during this submodule's live verification** (signup and invite calls intermittently 500ing or resetting mid-request, never the same call twice) — consistent with this free-tier project's known instability this session. Not a code defect; the throwaway verification script gained a 3-attempt retry-with-backoff around every live call to absorb it.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change — reuses the existing 5-value scope enum throughout. Docs updated in sync: `CONTEXT.md` §54 (new, Document map renumbered §55), this entry.

#### Session 2 continued — Phase 2, Submodule 1: Project Documents (complete)
- **First submodule of Phase 2 (Documents & Knowledge)**, chosen over Phase 1's one remaining item (Presence & Status) by explicit user choice. Went through its own focused plan-mode session — but the *first* plan presented (`ExitPlanMode`) was **rejected by the user**, not approved: a Google-Drive-style standalone module (folder hierarchy mirroring `Department.parentId`, tenant/department-wide sharing, cycle prevention) was the wrong shape entirely. The user redirected: document collaboration should live **inside each Project**, alongside Tasks/Comments/Members, with a broader feature set than originally pitched — upload/download/preview/replace, real version history, an optional approval status, comments, activity history, and search, kept lightweight rather than a full file-management system. A second plan was researched and written from scratch around that redirect, and approved.
- **The revised, project-scoped design turned out structurally simpler than the rejected folder-hierarchy one, not bigger** — a `Document` attaches to an existing `Project` and inherits its visibility/scope model directly (`projectsWhere`, reused as-is), with zero new hierarchy concept, denormalized department inheritance, or cross-department move semantics to build.
