- **RBAC scope-checking reuses `Task`'s exact precedent**: `requireDocumentInScope` fetches a `Document` plus its parent `Project`'s `{ownerId, departmentId}` and gates via `isRowInScope`, identical in shape to `tasks.mutations.ts`'s `requireTaskInScope`. **⚠️ A real, pre-existing gap in that exact precedent, deliberately not repeated**: confirmed by reading `projects.mutations.ts` directly that `task.create` accepts any `projectId` with zero scope validation against it. `document.create`/`createUploadUrl` do not copy this — the target project is validated via `isRowInScope` before any Storage credential is minted, the same deliberate stricter-than-precedent choice already made for Announcements/Meetings.
- **Tier ladder mirrors `project:create`/`update`/`delete`'s exact progression** (Lead → Manager → Department Head → Executive → Admin) — documents are everyday collaboration content, not an official broadcast, so they follow Projects' ladder rather than Announcements'. Update/delete are **scope-gated, not ownership-only** (a Manager can replace/delete a teammate's document within their own department) — a deliberate divergence from Announcement/Comment's author-only delete. No `document:read` triple at all — visibility is purely "can the actor see the parent project," per `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` §12's binding instruction to default to the org graph.
- **Comments extended, not rebuilt**: `comments.mutations.ts`'s `ENTITY_TYPES` gained `"document"`; `assertCommentTargetInScope`'s new branch resolves a document's parent project and reuses the exact `projectsWhere` check the `"project"` branch already does. **⚠️ A second dispatch point was found and fixed in the same pass, not just the obvious one**: `getProjectMemberIds` (mention-candidate resolution) used a binary `project`-vs-everything-else ternary that would have silently mis-handled `"document"` as `"task"`, always returning zero mention candidates for document comments. Refactored into an explicit three-way `resolveProjectIdForEntity` dispatch before this ever shipped. Zero new comment schema/mutation/frontend component — `CommentThread.tsx` is reused unchanged (aside from widening its `entityType` prop's union type), mounted per-document exactly as it already is per-project/per-task.
- **Real version history, not an in-place overwrite** — a deliberate expansion beyond the rejected first plan's own design (which had proposed upsert-in-place with no history). `document.replace` mints an upload URL for a **fresh** Storage path, never the current one; `document.finalizeReplace` snapshots the pre-replace state into a new `DocumentVersion` row before advancing the live `Document` fields, so no past version's file is ever orphaned or lost, and `document.getFileUrl` accepts an optional `versionId` to fetch any historical version's bytes.
- **A lightweight `DocumentActivity` log** (upload/replace/rename/approval-status-change/delete), logged inline in the same transaction as the triggering mutation, deliberately excluding comments (which already have their own list/UI). **Approval status is a plain nullable field gated by the existing `document:update` triple**, not a dedicated `document:approve` permission or a workflow engine — a deliberate lightweight call, flagged explicitly for the user to revisit if dedicated approver roles are ever wanted. No notification on upload/approval-change, matching `project.create`'s own confirmed "creation events don't notify" precedent.
- **New private Storage bucket (`documents`), created via script** — confirmed via this codebase's own §44 record that the `logos` bucket precedent (a stale code comment nearby claims "manually, via the Dashboard") was actually a one-off `storage.createBucket()` script; the same approach was used here, run once and deleted. **⚠️ A real API quirk found live, not assumed**: `createBucket()`'s own `fileSizeLimit`/`allowedMimeTypes` options silently failed to persist in this environment — confirmed by immediately re-fetching the bucket and finding both fields `undefined`. **Fixed by calling `updateBucket()` right after `createBucket()`**, which does persist them (re-verified via a fresh fetch showing the real 25MB limit and full MIME allowlist). Download/preview authorization mirrors `meeting.getJoinInfo` — `document.getFileUrl` carries no `requiredPermission`, just a visibility check, then mints a short-lived signed Storage URL; `mode: "view"` renders inline (preview), `mode: "download"` forces a save-as.
- **28 new backend tests** (documents module + 4 new Comments tests for the `"document"` branch and its `getProjectMemberIds` fix), full backend suite **242/242 passing**, full monorepo typecheck/build clean.
- **⚠️ An unrelated environment finding mid-submodule, not a code defect**: `pnpm -r run test` OOM'd across nearly every backend suite (`Jest worker ran out of memory`) — root-caused to genuine system memory pressure (~2.5GB free of 16GB, several dev servers/Docker/editor processes running concurrently), confirmed not a regression by running `npx jest --maxWorkers=2` directly, which passed all 242 tests cleanly against the identical code. Also hit, again, the by-now-familiar issue of `pnpm -r run build` overwriting the running `next dev` server's `.next` directory mid-session, corrupting its served CSS — recognized immediately from the earlier occurrence this session and fixed the same way (kill, clear `.next`, restart).
- **Verified end-to-end against a live, throwaway tenant** (scripted, never the real Admin's password, cleaned up after — including every uploaded Storage object, not just DB rows): two departments, two projects (one per department), a Lead/Manager/Practitioner all in one department, invited and activated through the real `/auth/complete-first-login` flow. **22/22 checks passed**, including real byte-for-byte Storage round trips (PUT real bytes to a minted signed upload URL, GET them back via a minted signed download URL, confirm exact match — for both the original upload and the post-replace v2), a disallowed MIME type rejected before any Storage credential was minted, cross-department upload correctly 403ing while same-department cross-role replace/delete correctly succeeded, a version snapshot confirmed after replace, the activity log confirmed to contain both `uploaded` and `replaced`, a document comment posted by a user with only project-level authorization, and search-by-filename.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change. Docs updated in sync: `CONTEXT.md` §56 (new, Document map renumbered §57), `ARCHITECTURE.md` §8.2 (new tables), §9.1 (new module row, Comments' `ENTITY_TYPES` note), this entry.

#### Session 2 continued — AI Assistant: full technical architecture approved (design only, nothing built yet)
- **The user asked to jump ahead of the 5-phase Core Workspace Modules roadmap's own ordering** (Phase 5, AI & Analytics, was last) to design the AI Assistant next — explicitly not a simple chatbot: deep integration across Projects, Tasks, Meetings, Documents, Chat, HR, CRM, and Analytics, semantic search, natural-language commands, proactive insights, full RBAC-safe context awareness, and provider abstraction (Claude/OpenAI/Gemini/Ollama). Explicitly requested as **design-for-approval only** — "don't start implementation yet."
- **This is not a from-scratch invention — `ARCHITECTURE.md` §10-11 (Session 1, 2026-07-14) already approved pgvector + Claude + a named tenant-safe RAG pattern that was simply never built.** This design extends that foundation to the much larger scope now requested rather than replacing it.
- **Two decisions confirmed with the user before finalizing the design** (both are the recommended options, not forced defaults): (1) **AI-initiated mutations are confirm-before-execute** — the AI resolves a natural-language command into a concrete, previewable action; nothing writes until the user approves it in the UI, reads need no gate; (2) **meeting summarization's v1 input is manual notes**, not a transcript/recording pipeline — a new `notes` field on `Meeting`, no Jitsi recording/transcription work (Jitsi itself is a temporary stopgap).
- **Live-verified before designing, not assumed**: pgvector 0.8.2 confirmed available (not yet enabled) on the real Supabase project via a direct query against `pg_available_extensions`. Confirmed via direct file reads that no AI/LLM SDK, no `@nestjs/schedule`, and no AI provider API key exist anywhere in this codebase today — every piece of this is genuinely new. **Confirmed an unimplemented `aiPrompt` `ActionSpec` kind already exists** in `packages/manifest-schema/src/ui-node.ts` (a real, if never-built, hook from the original design), currently a no-op in `apps/web/src/sdui/use-action-dispatch.ts` — this design implements it rather than inventing a parallel mechanism. **Confirmed CRM and true Analytics have zero schema/code** — this design builds a proven per-module "AI adapter" pattern against the five modules that do exist (Projects/Tasks/Meetings/Documents/Chat, plus HR for entity resolution) so a CRM/Analytics adapter can slot in identically once those modules themselves exist; this pass does not build them or guess their shape.
- **Key architectural decisions**: two separate provider seams, not one — completion (`CompletionProvider`: Anthropic default, OpenAI/Gemini/Ollama adapters) and embedding (`EmbeddingProvider`: OpenAI `text-embedding-3-small` default) are distinct configuration axes, since Anthropic has no embeddings endpoint at all. A two-stage tenant+scope-safe RAG retrieval process makes §11's already-approved prose mandate concrete: Stage 1 (coarse tenant-filtered pgvector query) + Stage 2 (mandatory per-chunk re-check against the *owning module's own* visibility function — `projectsWhere`, `assertProjectVisible`, etc. — before any retrieved text reaches a prompt; any chunk that fails is silently dropped). Natural-language commands are tool calls that are thin wrappers around *already-registered* mutations/data-sources, so RBAC is inherited, never reimplemented — `aiCommand.confirm` re-derives `ctx` fresh and dispatches the real mutation through its real transaction, so a permission change between preview and confirm is caught for free rather than needing a second authorization path to keep in sync.
- **New infrastructure this introduces, flagged explicitly rather than assumed**: `@nestjs/schedule` (first real consumer — embedding backfill + AI reminder digests), an outbox-pattern `EmbeddingJob` table (this codebase's first), `@nestjs/throttler` (rate limiting), a document text-extraction step (Documents currently stores opaque bytes with no extraction), and `zod-to-json-schema` (tool schema generation from existing mutation input schemas).
- **A phased roadmap (A through F) was proposed and approved as the sequencing plan, not as a green light to build all of it now** — Phase A (provider abstraction + a workspace-unaware general chat assistant, proving the provider seam and the `aiPrompt`/`AiPanel` frontend surface) is the recommended starting point. **Per this codebase's standing practice, each phase still gets its own dedicated research → plan → approval cycle before any code** — this entry records the architecture's approval, not an implementation.
- Nine explicit judgment calls were flagged in the design for the user's visibility rather than presented as settled (default embedding provider, default model tier, no v1 streaming, model-mediated entity disambiguation, single-field meeting notes, deferred conversation-history search, deferred response caching, `TenantConfig.overrides.ai` placement, no send-message-on-behalf-of tool) — see the plan file for full reasoning on each.
- No docs updated beyond this entry and `CONTEXT.md`'s status line — no code shipped, so no `ARCHITECTURE.md`/`ORG_HIERARCHY.md` "as built" sections yet; those get written when Phase A actually ships.

#### Session 2 continued — AI Assistant, Phase A: Provider Abstraction + Basic Chat (complete)
- **First buildable slice of the approved AI Assistant architecture** — the user confirmed "go ahead with Phase A," which went through its own dedicated research → plan → approval cycle (per standing practice) before any code, distinct from the earlier architecture-level approval. Deliverable: a workspace-unaware general chat assistant, proving the `CompletionProvider` abstraction is genuinely swappable and that the long-dormant `aiPrompt` manifest hook is real and buildable, before any RAG/tool-calling complexity layers on top in later phases.
- **⚠️ Two real findings from this submodule's own research changed its scope from the architecture doc's original sketch, not silently absorbed:**
  1. **Both `FeatureFlag` and `Plan.entitlements` — the two seams the architecture doc proposed reusing for tenant AI gating — were confirmed non-functional today, not just unused.** Traced the actual pruning pipeline directly: `FeatureFlag` rows are fetched into the compiled manifest but consumed by zero pruning logic anywhere, and no mutation exists to set one; `Plan.entitlements` filtering is real, tested, wired code in `entitlement-filter.ts`, but `Tenant.planId` is never populated anywhere in this codebase, making it a permanent no-op. Building either into a working gate was disproportionate scope for "prove basic chat works." **Phase A gates purely on whether the platform operator has configured a provider API key at all** — a new global (not per-tenant) `aiAvailable: boolean` on the compiled manifest. Per-tenant/per-plan AI gating remains real future scope, explicitly flagged rather than silently dropped.
  2. **A real gap in the `preResolve` mutation-framework hook, found by trying to use it for its actual intended purpose.** `aiMessage.send` needs to read a conversation's prior messages before calling the model — and the model call itself must never sit inside a DB transaction (Prisma's interactive-transaction timeout would abort it for any non-trivial response, not a hypothetical risk). `preResolve` previously only received `{tenantId}`, with no way to verify *whose* conversation it was before including its private history in a request sent to a third-party API. **Fixed by extending `preResolve`'s context to `{tenantId, authUserId}`** — the controller already resolves `authUserId` before opening the transaction, it just wasn't threaded through. Minimal, additive, backward-compatible; zero existing `preResolve` implementations broke.
- **⚠️ A live, mid-implementation scope change: two completion-provider adapters shipped, not the planned "Anthropic only."** The user had a working Gemini key immediately available and not yet an Anthropic one, so a Gemini adapter (`@google/genai`) was added alongside the planned Anthropic one (`@anthropic-ai/sdk`) in the same pass. This *strengthened* the submodule's own core goal rather than diluting it — the `CompletionProvider` interface is proven swappable against two real vendors from day one. `AI_COMPLETION_PROVIDER=gemini` is the active configuration; switching to Anthropic later is a one-line env change, no code change, exactly per the interface's own design intent.
- **⚠️ Two real Gemini findings caught during this submodule's own live verification, neither assumed from documentation**: (1) `gemini-2.5-flash` — the natural first choice based on general knowledge — is retired for new API keys, confirmed via a real `404 "no longer available to new users"` from the live API, not stale docs; corrected to `gemini-3.6-flash`, the current GA default. (2) `thinkingConfig.thinkingBudget: 0` (Gemini's own documented way to disable extended-thinking mode) is **rejected as an invalid argument by gemini-3.6-flash specifically** — confirmed by writing a minimal, isolated repro script outside the app entirely before concluding it wasn't an application bug. Thinking is left enabled; the response token budget was sized with real headroom (2048, not the originally-planned 1024) since thinking tokens count against the same budget and a too-tight budget is a documented failure mode (empty response, `finishReason: "MAX_TOKENS"`).
- **New `AiConversation`/`AiMessage` schema**, ownership-gated (`userId = ctx.userId`) exactly like `Notification` — no RBAC triple for either. The completion call runs entirely inside `preResolve`, never inside `resolve()`'s transaction; `resolve()` only persists the already-computed messages and bumps `title`/`updatedAt` — a fast, external-I/O-free write. Conversation titles auto-generate from the first ~50 characters of the first message, no extra LLM call spent on titling. **No polling, unlike Chat** — `aiMessage.send` is one synchronous mutation returning the assistant's reply directly; there's no background job for a poll to catch up with, and a personal AI conversation has no second party who could update it concurrently.
- **`aiPrompt` implemented for the first time since the original v1 architecture defined its schema shape** — `use-action-dispatch.ts`'s no-op branch now calls a new `openAiPanel(preset?, context?)` render-context method. Not exercised by any real blueprint action yet (no module has a preset registered — that's Phase B+'s per-module AI adapters); verified instead by a direct `renderHook` unit test of the dispatcher. New `AiPanel.tsx`, a shell-mounted slide-over drawer (same "not blueprint-registered" precedent as `NotificationBell`/`CommandPalette`), opened via a header "Ask AI" trigger shown only when `aiAvailable`.
- **23 new backend tests, full backend suite 256/256 passing. 2 new frontend tests, full frontend suite 9/9 passing.** Full monorepo typecheck/build clean.
- **Verified end-to-end against live, throwaway tenants with real model calls** (never the real Admin's password, cleaned up after): **13/13 checks passed**, including a real multi-turn conversation (the assistant correctly recalled turn-1 context when asked about it in turn 2), `aiAvailable` correctly reflecting live server config, conversation title auto-generation, and — the security-critical case — a second user in a separate tenant receiving a 404 both reading and attempting to send into the first user's conversation, confirmed to 404 *before* any provider call was made, not just before any write.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change. Docs updated in sync: `CONTEXT.md` §58 (new, Document map renumbered §59), this entry.

#### Session 2 continued — AI Assistant, Phase B: Embeddings / RAG (Documents) (complete)
- **Second buildable slice of the approved AI Assistant architecture** — the user confirmed "go ahead with Phase B," which went through its own dedicated research → plan → approval cycle before any code, same as Phase A. Deliverable: real retrieval-augmented Q&A over Documents, per the architecture's own "Deliverable: RAG Q&A over one real module" framing — Projects/Tasks/Meetings RAG remain future phases.
- **⚠️ Two real scope changes from the original architecture sketch, found by this submodule's own research, applied proactively rather than re-discovered live**: (1) **no `OPENAI_API_KEY` exists in this project** — the architecture doc's OpenAI-default for embeddings was revised to **Gemini** (`gemini-embedding-001`), reusing the existing `GEMINI_API_KEY` rather than standing up a third vendor. Confirmed live that a commonly-cited default, `text-embedding-004`, is already deprecated — the exact class of mistake Phase A already hit once with its completion model. (2) `gemini-embedding-001`'s Matryoshka truncation (768/1536 dims) requires **manual re-normalization to unit length** to stay correct — a silent correctness pitfall with no error thrown. Phase B uses the full untruncated **3072-dim** output instead, trading storage for avoiding that risk on a first implementation.
- **A real Prisma+pgvector risk was verified empirically before building on it, not trusted from documentation**: Prisma 7.8.0 (this project's version) falls inside a range with an open, confirmed bug where `Unsupported("vector")` fields can cause "Drift detected" errors under `migrate dev`'s shadow-database diffing — a command this project already never uses for an unrelated pre-existing reason (hand-written SQL + `migrate deploy` + explicit `generate`). A scratch `vector(3)` table was created, migrated via the real workflow, round-tripped with real raw-SQL cosine-distance queries, confirmed fully correct, then torn down — before the real schema was written.
- **Two-stage tenant+scope-safe retrieval (`RetrievalService`)**: Stage 1 is a raw-SQL, tenant-filtered, over-fetching nearest-neighbor query with an optional `{sourceType, sourceId}` narrow-filter (one code path serves both open search and a single-document-scoped conversation). Stage 2 is a **mandatory** per-candidate re-check via `assertProjectVisible`, reused completely unchanged from Documents — never a new authorization path. Stage 1 (+ RLS) closes cross-tenant leakage; Stage 2 is what actually stops **same-tenant, different-scope** leakage (a Lead scoped to Project A seeing a chunk from Project B in the same tenant) — confirmed live to be the meaningful distinction, not just defense-in-depth restated.
- **A second real `preResolve` framework gap, same class as Phase A's `{tenantId}` → `{tenantId, authUserId}` fix**: `assertProjectVisible` needs a full `DataSourceContext` (`effective` permissions, `userDepartmentId`), which `preResolve`'s context alone can't construct. Rather than widening the framework again, `aiMessage.send`'s factory now also takes `PermissionResolverService` directly and builds the full context itself in its own short transaction — mirroring exactly what `DataSourcesController.resolve()` already does for every real data source.
- **This codebase's first outbox pattern (`EmbeddingJob`) and first `@nestjs/schedule` consumer.** `document.create`/`document.finalizeReplace` only enqueue a row (mirroring `logActivity`'s existing plain-function shape); `EmbeddingJobProcessorService` polls every 15s. Per-chunk content hashing means an edit to one page of a large document only re-embeds what changed. **Cross-tenant claiming was a genuinely new problem** — `TenantPrismaService.run()` is the only way to read RLS-protected tables, and RLS requires `app.tenant_id` set per call, so no single query can see every tenant's due jobs at once. Solved via per-tenant round-robin (`tenantPrisma.root.tenant.findMany` — a legitimate root-table read, same precedent `aiMessage.send`'s own tenant-name lookup already uses). Tx-boundary discipline applied to the processor itself: claiming, doing the actual work (Storage download, extraction, the embedding call), and writing results back are three separate steps — external I/O never runs inside a transaction.
- **Document text extraction deliberately scoped to `text/plain`/`text/csv`/`application/pdf` only** — Documents' upload allowlist is broader; everything else gets no job at all this phase, a deliberate scope cut, not a silent drop. **PDF extraction uses `unpdf`, not `pdf-parse`** — confirmed live that `pdf-parse`'s current major ships a hard native dependency (`@napi-rs/canvas`) for no benefit here, while `unpdf` has zero required native deps and its real API matched what was assumed before installing it.
- **⚠️ One real bug found only by live verification, not unit tests — exactly why live verification, not just green tests, gates calling a submodule complete**: the raw-SQL `INSERT INTO embeddings` never set `updated_at`, which (unlike Prisma's client-managed `@updatedAt` fields) has no database-level default — every real embedding write 500'd with a not-null violation. A mocked-`tx` unit test can't catch this class of bug at all. Fixed by setting `updated_at = now()` explicitly on both the insert and its `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`.
- **A real, previously-undiscovered Phase A gap found and closed in this pass**: `AiPanel.tsx` accepted `preset`/`context` props since Phase A but never wired them into the `aiConversation.create` call. Phase B connects it for real — `aiConversation.create` now accepts `{preset?, contextRef?}`, persisted onto the conversation and read back by `aiMessage.send`'s retrieval step to narrow Stage 1's query. `"documents.summarize"` auto-sends a first message so opening it is a single click; `DocumentsPanel` gained two new per-document buttons ("Summarize", "Ask AI about this document"), gated on a new `aiAvailable` field threaded onto `RenderContextValue` — one retrieval code path, two new UX entry points, zero new backend preset-branching, no new mutations.
- **9 new backend test files/additions, full backend suite 278/278 passing (up from 256)** — including a unit test for the Stage-2-specific same-tenant-different-scope case, not just live. Frontend: existing 4 files/9 tests still passing (mocks updated for the new `aiAvailable` field); no new frontend unit tests added, verified live instead. Full monorepo typecheck/build clean.
- **New npm dependencies**: `unpdf`, `@nestjs/schedule`. **New env vars**: `AI_EMBEDDING_PROVIDER` (`gemini` default), `AI_EMBEDDING_MODEL` (optional override) — reuses `GEMINI_API_KEY`, no new secret.
- **Verified end-to-end against live, throwaway tenants with real Gemini embedding + completion calls** (never the real Admin's password, cleaned up after — tenants, the Lead's Supabase Auth account, and all `embeddings`/`embedding_jobs` rows): **12/12 checks passed**, including a real hand-built single-page PDF uploaded/extracted/chunked/embedded/correctly-answered-from (not just a plain-text file); the **security-critical Stage 2 case** (a Lead who is a member of Project A but not Project B correctly retrieves Project A's planted secret but never Project B's, even though Stage 1 alone would have let it through); a lighter cross-tenant sanity pass; and `contextRef` narrowing confirmed both directions (scoped to Document A never surfaces Document B's secret even for an Admin who could otherwise see it unscoped, and still answers correctly for the document it IS scoped to).
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change — retrieval reuses `assertProjectVisible`'s existing scope model exactly. Docs updated in sync: `CONTEXT.md` §59 (new, Document map renumbered §60), this entry.

#### Session 2 continued — Business-value-prioritized build order: approved

- **The user reviewed overall platform state and asked to prioritize by business value rather than continue mechanically through the original 5-phase Core Workspace Modules roadmap** — evaluate each remaining module's importance to the platform, prioritize what's essential for a production-quality enterprise workspace, and document anything deferrable as a future enhancement instead of building it, presenting the proposed order before starting each module.
- Surveyed what's shipped vs. genuinely open across both paused roadmaps (Core Workspace Modules, Company Administration) and proposed: **Calendar → Attendance → Leave Management**, reasoning that Calendar closes the biggest structural gap (Meetings and Task due-dates already exist but have nowhere to live visually) and Leave Management pioneers the approval-workflow pattern `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` already specifies for `User.managerId` but nothing has used yet. Recommended deferring Presence & Status, a standalone Shared Folders/Wiki, Employee Reviews, CRM, Analytics/Reports, and Company Admin Submodules B/D–N as documented future enhancements rather than building them now.
- **Approved as proposed** — Calendar first, then Attendance, then Leave Management, with priorities to be revisited (Analytics/AI Phase C/CRM among the candidates) once those three ship.

#### Session 2 continued — Core Workspace Modules, Phase 3, Submodule 1: Calendar & Scheduling (complete)

- **First submodule of the newly-approved build order.** Went through its own focused plan-mode session (3 parallel Explore agents researching existing calendar-adjacent code/schema, the Announcements module as the closest precedent, and scheduling/notification infrastructure, then a Plan agent producing a detailed implementation plan, spot-verified against the live repo) before any code, same standing workflow as every prior submodule.
- **Fixed constraint carried over from the original roadmap approval, not re-litigated**: no event recurrence — every calendar item (Meeting, `CalendarEvent`, Task due date) is a single point-in-time instance.
- **New `CalendarEvent` model gets an explicit `isPrivate` boolean, not an overloaded nullable `departmentId`.** Announcement's `departmentId: null` already means "tenant-wide broadcast"; collapsing "personal" onto that same nullable field would have repeated the exact "two meanings, one nullable column" bug class this codebase has hit for real twice before (`isRowInScope`'s "own is a floor" fix; `tasksWhere`'s assigned-to-me-must-OR fix). `isPrivate=true` rows are always `departmentId: null`, visible only to their author; `isPrivate=false` rows reuse Announcement's ancestor-chain broadcast model exactly, including an always-on author floor so an author always sees their own private entries plus anything they've broadcast regardless of current department.
- **`calendarEvent:create:own` is a universal baseline grant**, added directly to `role.intern` (the permission ladder's root) rather than gated behind Department Head like Announcement's own floor — every tier inherits it via `extends`, making personal calendar entries available to literally everyone without a bespoke bypass of the permission-triple system. Escalating broadcast tiers (`department`/`department-subtree`/`tenant`) mirror Announcement's exact role-tier mapping.
- **A new unified `calendar.list` data source aggregates three sources for a bounded date range** — Meetings (`meetingsWhere`, unchanged), CalendarEvents (`calendarEventsWhere`, new), and Task due-dates (`tasksWhere`, unchanged, with a `dueDate` range filter layered on top) — sequential queries against one `tx`, never `Promise.all` (the same standing warning every data source in this codebase carries), tagged by `itemType` and sorted by `start`.
- **Reminders are a new outbox pattern, `CalendarReminder`, deliberately mirroring `EmbeddingJob`'s shape exactly** (polymorphic `sourceType`/`sourceId`, `status`/`attempts`/`lastError`/`availableAt`, same compound index) — the one necessary addition is `recipientUserId`, since a reminder is inherently per-person, unlike embedding a document which has one output regardless of who reads it. `CalendarReminderProcessorService` mirrors `EmbeddingJobProcessorService`'s claim/release/backoff shape verbatim (60s poll vs. embeddings' 15s, batch size 20 vs. 5 — reminders are cheaper per-job and don't need RAG-freshness cadence). Same hard tx-boundary rule as embeddings: the email send never runs inside a `tenantPrisma.run()` transaction — the in-app `Notification` write and `EmailService.sendReminderEmail` (never throws, same shape as `sendInviteEmail`) are two separate steps. A cancelled meeting/deleted event/deleted task with a pending reminder is gracefully marked done at fire time, not failed — verified live, not just asserted.
- **Task due-date reminders are in scope but create-time-only** — Task has one `assigneeId`, not a participant list, and no mutation touches `dueDate` after creation. `task.create` gained one optional `reminderMinutesBefore` field (`.refine`-guarded to require a `dueDate`). Disclosed, not silently accepted as complete: reassigning a task afterward does not retarget or cancel its already-enqueued reminder — it still fires for whoever was the assignee at creation time.
- **`TaskList.tsx` finally surfaces `Task.dueDate`** — a schema/API field that's existed since Stage 9 but was never rendered or settable through the UI (only usable via a raw mutation call before now). Added a due-date column (danger-toned when overdue, mirroring `tasksWhere`'s own `overdue` semantics) and a create-form date input + reminder preset selector.
- **Frontend: no new npm dependency for the calendar grid** — a hand-rolled month-grid (plain `Date` arithmetic, CSS grid) plus an agenda/list view toggle, consistent with the project's existing hand-rolled Obsidian design system (no heavy third-party UI library beyond `recharts` for `Chart`). Flagged as a deliberate build-vs-buy call, proceeded without escalating to the user first — unlike Meetings' video vendor, a calendar grid carries no comparable budget/infrastructure weight.
- **⚠️ A real, pre-existing, platform-wide bug found and fixed during this submodule's own live verification, unrelated to Calendar itself** (same class as the `ERR_HTTP_HEADERS_SENT` fix found during Announcements' verification — found live, small, fixed anyway rather than deferred): `MutationsController` and `DataSourcesController` both called `.parse()` on Zod input schemas completely unwrapped — every mutation and every data source across the entire app returned a raw 500 on invalid input instead of a 400, since only `auth.controller.ts` ever caught `ZodError`. This has been true since Stage 8 (Projects), not introduced here — Calendar's own new `.refine()` guard on `task.create` is simply what surfaced it during verification. Fixed by mirroring `auth.controller.ts`'s existing catch/rethrow-as-`BadRequestException` shape in both controllers (a small shared `parseOrBadRequest` helper in `DataSourcesController`, which has three call sites; inline in `MutationsController`, which has one). Full backend suite re-run afterward, unaffected (319/319, no regressions) — this only changes previously-500 invalid-input responses to 400, never touches any previously-valid path.
- **41 new backend tests** (`calendar.data-sources.spec.ts`, `calendar.mutations.spec.ts`, `reminder-outbox.spec.ts`, and — deliberately, not by default — new coverage for `calendar-reminder-processor.service.spec.ts`'s pure helpers, since `EmbeddingJobProcessorService` itself has no spec file at all today; the poller's real per-`sourceType` branching and its status as the first background job with a user-facing email output justified adding it anyway), plus extensions to `meetings.mutations.spec.ts`/`tasks.mutations.spec.ts` for the new reminder-enqueue paths. Full backend suite **319/319 passing** (up from 278). Frontend: existing 4 files/9 tests unchanged — no new frontend unit tests, verified live instead, same precedent as every prior UI-touching submodule.
- **Verified end-to-end against a live, throwaway tenant with a real department tree** (Root → Mid [Executive] → Leaf, Root → Sibling [Department Head] — deliberately avoiding the "Executive at Root trivially subtrees everything" mistake already hit once for Announcements), 4 role tiers activated through the real first-login flow: **34/34 checks passed** — full department/subtree/tenant broadcast authorization matrix, personal-event isolation between same-tenant users, ancestor-chain read visibility, `calendar.list`'s three-source merge/sort, correct `availableAt` computation for both meeting and task reminders, the **live poller actually firing** a backdated reminder (real `Notification` write, `sendReminderEmail` invoked) and gracefully skipping a deleted source, delete ownership, and cross-tenant isolation.
- **⚠️ Still genuinely open, disclosed rather than glossed over: no screenshot/browser-automation tool exists in this environment**, so the month-grid's real visual layout and the Agenda view's real rendering are the one piece this pass couldn't verify directly — the user's own browser review is still pending, same standing limitation as every prior UI-touching stage.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change — reuses the existing 5-value scope enum and department-tree helpers exactly, per §0/§277's explicit mandate that Calendar (named there specifically) not invent its own scoping. Docs updated in sync: `CONTEXT.md` §60 (new, Document map renumbered §61), `ARCHITECTURE.md` §8.2/§9.1 (new tables, new module row), this entry.

#### Session 2 continued — Core Workspace Modules, Phase 3, Submodule 2: Attendance (complete)

- **Second submodule of the business-value-prioritized build order.** Unlike every prior submodule, went through its own research phase and found **Attendance had never been scoped beyond its name anywhere in this project's history** — `CHANGELOG.md`/`CONTEXT.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md` only ever mentioned it as a sequencing label; the one document that reportedly itemized Phase 4 ("CRM & Workforce") was already disclosed as unrecoverable. Its plan therefore made real, explicitly-disclosed product-scope judgment calls, the same posture Calendar's own plan took when it hit the identical situation.
- **v1 scope: a day-level status record, not a clock.** A per-user, per-day `AttendanceRecord` (`present`/`absent`/`late`/`half_day` — free-text vocabulary, matching `Task.status`), self-marked once per day for *today only* (no backdating), with an optional note. No check-in/check-out timestamps or duration tracking — the existing `event_available` nav icon (a checkmark-calendar, not a clock) was weak-but-real supporting evidence this was always meant to be day-level, and a minute-level clock is a second core concept this build phase's "essential and lean" framing didn't ask for.
- **A real, non-obvious RBAC finding, caught during design, not live**: `attendance:update` never grants `"own"` scope anywhere in the seed ladder — deliberately, not an oversight. The correction path's subject is always a *different* `User` row; plugged into `isRowInScope` as `{ ownerId: null, departmentId: target.departmentId }` (the exact `assertInviteesInScope` precedent, `meetings.mutations.ts:41-59`), the `"own"` branch (`subject.ownerId === actor.userId`) can never be true — an `attendance:update:own` grant would have been a silent, structural no-op. The tier ladder starts `attendance:update` at `"department"` (Department Head); self-correction is special-cased *before* that check ever runs. Confirmed live: a Practitioner gets 403 correcting even their own row via `attendance.correct`, since they only ever hold `attendance:create:own`.
- **`attendance:create:own`/`attendance:read:own` are universal-floor grants** added directly to `role.intern`, same mechanism as Calendar's `calendarEvent:create:own`. `attendance:read`/`attendance:update` are paired, both starting at Department Head — not staged earlier the way `meeting:read` starts at Practitioner, since attendance is HR-adjacent personal data closer in sensitivity to `user:manage`/`department:manage`. `role.hr-manager` picked up the now-familiar tenant-wide bonus grant (`+attendance:read:tenant`/`+attendance:update:tenant`), the same channel every submodule since Announcements has reused.
- **Two data sources, not one — a real, disclosed deviation from the approved plan.** The plan called for a single `attendance.list` covering three modes (self / other-user / team-roster) selected by parameter presence, but implementing it revealed a genuine ambiguity: "no `userId` param" would have had to mean both "give me my own history" and "give me my whole team," decided only by the actor's granted scope — a confusing, implicit call-site contract. Split into `attendance.list` (self or one explicitly-named other user) and `attendance.roster` (team/department roster, always explicit) — two clearly-named entry points instead of one overloaded one, changed during implementation once the ambiguity became concrete, not silently.
- **Correction fires exactly one `Notification`** (`type: "attendance.corrected"`, the established `<entity>.<pastTenseEvent>` convention), skipped entirely for self-correction (`notificationRecipientFor`-style "don't notify yourself" exclusion, re-implemented locally per this codebase's established per-module-duplication convention).
- **`markedById` is a real, separate column** distinguishing self-marked from manager-corrected — no FK, matching every other actor-id column in this schema. **No `@db.Date` column introduced** — confirmed zero existing usage of that Prisma type anywhere in this schema; `date` stays a plain `DateTime`, normalized to UTC midnight at both write sites, matching `Task.dueDate`'s precedent.
- **The dead `nav.attendance`/`page.attendance` `EmptyState` placeholder (live since Sidebar Navigation Phase 1) is replaced with the real module** — `requiredPermission` changed from the placeholder's generic `user:manage` to `attendance:read`, `page.attendance`'s content changed from a `Heading`+`EmptyState` stub to the real `AttendanceWorkspace` composite.
- **28 new backend tests** (`attendance.data-sources.spec.ts`, `attendance.mutations.spec.ts`), full backend suite **347/347 passing** (up from 319). Frontend: existing 4 files/9 tests unchanged — no new frontend unit tests, verified live instead.
- **Verified end-to-end against a live, throwaway tenant** with the same department-tree shape Calendar's own verification established (Root → Mid [Executive] → Leaf, Root → Sibling [Department Head]), 5 role tiers activated through the real first-login flow: **29/30 checks passed** — full department/subtree/tenant correction-authorization matrix, same-day resubmission upserting not duplicating, future-date rejection, notification behavior (fires for corrections, skipped for self-correction), `attendance.list`/`attendance.roster`'s scope-correct results, and cross-tenant isolation. **The one non-passing check was a bug in the verification script itself** (it searched the top-level navigation array for `nav.attendance`, which actually lives nested inside `nav.hr-group.children` — a pre-existing, unchanged group structure) — not a product defect, confirmed by the adjacent check (`page.attendance` compiles to the real `AttendanceWorkspace` node and is reachable) passing correctly.
- **⚠️ Hit the exact Supavisor connection-pool contention (`P2028`, "Unable to start a transaction in the given time") already documented once before in this project** — the `CalendarReminderProcessorService`/`EmbeddingJobProcessorService` pollers' per-tenant transactions contending with this submodule's own verification script under a small connection pool. Transient, resolved by retrying; not a code defect. Worth remembering for any future submodule's own live verification.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change — reuses the existing 5-value scope enum exactly, per §0/§277's general compliance mandate. No approval-workflow logic was built — that's explicitly Leave Management's pattern to pioneer via `User.managerId`, not Attendance's. Docs updated in sync: `CONTEXT.md` §61 (new, Document map renumbered §62), `ARCHITECTURE.md` §8.2/§9.1 (new table, new module row), this entry.

#### Session 2 continued — Roles & Permissions Management (priority insert ahead of Leave Management, complete)

- **The user explicitly prioritized this ahead of Leave Management**, calling it "a core administrative capability for every enterprise workspace," superseding the previous "Leave Management is next" plan — the business-value-prioritized order resumes with Leave Management after this ships. Same standing workflow as every prior submodule (research → design → approval → implement → test → document), with the design phase unusually conservative: this module writes directly into the `roles`/`role_assignments` tables every other module's authorization already depends on, so every design decision was verified against the actual current source before being presented, not recalled from memory.
- **Zero Prisma migration, zero change to permission resolution.** `Role.sourceBlueprintRoleId` was already nullable and already the load-bearing custom/blueprint discriminator (`materializeBlueprintRoles` filters on it; `isRoleAssignableBy` reads it for the Admin bypass) — no redundant `isCustom` flag added. Creating/editing a role is just writing `Role.permissions` (a `Json` string-array column) directly; `PermissionResolverService`/`collapsePermissions` needed zero changes, confirmed by reading them fresh, not assumed from prior familiarity.
- **Blueprint-derived roles are view-only in-app — a hard constraint, not a preference.** `materializeBlueprintRoles` unconditionally overwrites `label`/`permissions`/`extendsRoleId` on every role it manages, on every reseed. Editing one in-app would silently vanish on the next `prisma db seed` run — a real, confirmed data-loss trap, not hypothetical. `role.updateCustom`/`role.delete` reject any role with `sourceBlueprintRoleId !== null` outright (400), even for Company Admin — the guard checks the field, not the actor's identity. "Clone into a custom role, then customize the clone" (`role.clone`) is the supported path for "close to Executive but slightly different."
- **The one genuinely new mechanism: `assertPermissionsGrantableByActor` (`apps/api/src/rbac/permission-grant-check.ts`), closing a real, previously-unguarded gap.** `isRoleAssignableBy` (§31) has only ever guarded *which role* an actor can assign; nothing anywhere in this codebase guarded *which permissions* a role definition could contain — confirmed via exhaustive grep, not assumed. Reuses `broaderScope`/`SCOPE_RANK` (`rbac/scope.ts`) unchanged: every `resource:action:scope` triple requested on create/update/clone must be within the actor's own effective grants at an equal-or-broader scope, or the whole call throws, all-or-nothing. Built even though only Company Admin can reach `role:manage` today — both because the user named this explicitly as a requirement and because it's what makes a future delegated `role:manage` grant (Delegation, still deferred) safe by construction, the identical justification `isRoleAssignableBy` already gives for `user.invite`'s own currently-inert check.
- **Custom roles are always flat/standalone (`extendsRoleId: null`) — a deliberate v1 narrowing of an already-anticipated future item, not an oversight.** `ARCHITECTURE.md` already anticipated a fuller "roles with their own `extends`+delta shape" version of this feature as later Phase 3 work; building that now would mean live cycle detection and re-running delta-application logic at mutation time — real scope the user's requirements didn't ask for, on a system this task was explicitly told to treat conservatively. **Direct, natural consequence, not a new rule**: `isRoleAssignableBy`'s existing chain-walk will never find a standalone role as an ancestor of any non-Admin actor's own chain, so only Company Admin can assign a custom role via `user.changeRole` — reused completely unchanged. Confirmed live: a `role:assign`-holding-but-non-Admin actor was rejected (403) assigning a custom role; Admin succeeded.
- **A hand-maintained `PERMISSION_CATALOG`** (`rbac/permission-catalog.ts`) is the single source of truth for the frontend's "group by module" picker — `resource`/`action` are unvalidated free-form strings (`packages/manifest-schema/src/permission.ts`), so there's no way to derive this by introspection. Covers the 11 real resources with an actual enforced consumer; **`task:delete` deliberately excluded** — confirmed via grep it's granted at every scope in `seed.ts`'s role ladder but has zero consuming mutation anywhere in `tasks.mutations.ts`/`tasks.data-sources.ts`, a real pre-existing dead/anticipatory grant this module didn't introduce. A standing maintenance obligation is documented in the file's own header comment: any future module's new permission triple must be added here in the same PR or it's silently ungrantable through this UI.
- **`roles.listDetailed` is a new, richer sibling data source — the pre-existing `roles.list` is completely untouched.** Its one real consumer, `TeamMembers`' role picker, needs nothing this module adds; bloating its payload for an unrelated invite dropdown would be pure downside for zero benefit. Same "two narrow, consumer-shaped data sources over one over-fetching shared one" precedent `attendance.list`/`attendance.roster` already established. **Role *assignment* isn't duplicated in the new module's UI at all** — any custom role created here appears automatically in `TeamMembers`' existing role picker (`roles.list` is unfiltered by `sourceBlueprintRoleId`), so `user.changeRole` is reused completely unchanged, zero new assignment logic.
- **`users.effectivePermissions` is deliberately distinct from the existing `GET /me/permissions`** — the latter is self-only and its own code explicitly documents it as "not a permanent API surface consumers should depend on." The new data source lets any `role:manage` holder look up *any* user's resolved permissions, reusing `PermissionResolverService.resolveEffectivePermissionsWithTx` directly (zero new resolution logic) via a factory-function pattern (`createUsersEffectivePermissionsDataSource`) — the first time this codebase's existing "factory mutation for an injected service" pattern (`createUserInviteMutation`) was applied to a data source instead of a mutation.
- **A real correctness detail caught during design, not live**: `parsePermissionString` throws a plain `Error`, not an `HttpException`, on a malformed permission string — the same bug class as the `MutationsController`/`DataSourcesController` uncaught-`ZodError` gap Calendar's own verification found and fixed (§60), one level deeper (inside a resolver, not a controller's own `.parse()` call). Fixed locally inside `permission-grant-check.ts`'s own wrapper, not a shared-controller change — confirmed via a dedicated test that a malformed triple 400s cleanly rather than 500ing.
- **43 new backend tests** (`permission-grant-check.spec.ts`, `roles.mutations.spec.ts`, `roles.data-sources.spec.ts`), full backend suite **377/377 passing** (up from 347). Frontend: existing 4 files/9 tests unchanged — no new frontend unit tests, verified live instead, same precedent as every prior UI-touching submodule.
- **Verified end-to-end against a live, throwaway tenant** — no department tree/scope matrix needed this time, since `role:manage`/`role:assign` are tenant-wide-only permissions, not department-scoped: **28/28 checks passed**, including creating a custom role from a held permission subset; **proving escalation prevention is real, not "Admin can do anything anyway"** by constructing a `role:manage`-holding-but-non-Admin test actor (via the module's own mechanisms — a narrow custom role, assigned to a test user through the module itself) that could grant exactly what it held and was rejected (403) on one triple it didn't; cloning a blueprint role producing a byte-for-byte-identical permission array; both edit and delete rejected (400) on a blueprint role even for Admin; a non-Admin actor rejected (403) assigning a custom role while Admin succeeded; delete correctly blocked (409) while a role has an active assignment and succeeding once unassigned; every one of the 4 new mutations and 3 new data sources 403ing for a plain-tier actor; `users.effectivePermissions(self)` matching `GET /me/permissions` exactly as an independent cross-check; and — the load-bearing claim the whole design rests on — **re-running a full `prisma db seed` against the live database left the custom test role's row completely, byte-for-byte untouched**, confirmed by comparing the exact row before and after, direct empirical proof (not just static code reading) that blueprint materialization really is structurally blind to custom roles. One transient `ECONNRESET` hit the verification script's own fetch call immediately after the reseed subprocess completed (the reseed itself succeeded; only the following HTTP check needed a quick, separate follow-up script) — the same class of live-infrastructure transient already documented for Supavisor contention in Attendance's own verification, not a product defect.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change — §0's compliance mandate (reuse the same scope enum, same tier ladder concepts, no parallel authorization system) is satisfied by construction, since this module adds no new authorization *mechanism*, only an authoring UI over the existing one. Docs updated in sync: `CONTEXT.md` §62 (new, Document map renumbered §63), `ARCHITECTURE.md` (§8.2's `roles` row, §9.1 new module row), this entry.

#### Session 2 continued — Docker/npm/pnpm storage relocated off C: (environment, not a feature)

- **Between Roles & Permissions Management and Delegation, the user asked to improve the local dev environment**: Docker Desktop's WSL2 data disk, npm's cache, and pnpm's cache-dir were all defaulting onto a chronically low-space `C:` drive. Root-caused a real prior incident along the way: the disk-full `overlayfs` I/O error already documented in `infra/jitsi/README.md` (2026-08-03) hadn't just blocked new container creation — it had silently zeroed out the `nginx`/`jicofo`/`jvb` binaries inside the *already-cached* Jitsi images, producing a misleading `exec format error` on next start (architecture matched host/image; the real cause was only found by inspecting the binaries' byte sizes directly inside a throwaway container). Fixed by removing and re-pulling the three affected images.
- **Docker's data relocated via an NTFS junction**, not the in-app Settings UI — this Docker Desktop version's containerd-snapshotter backend exposes no "Disk image location" setting reachable via its settings file, CLI, or the registry (checked all three before concluding this). `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Docker\wsl` was stopped (`docker desktop stop` + `wsl --shutdown`), robocopied to `D:\DockerData\wsl`, verified byte-for-byte on both vhdx files, then replaced with a junction — Docker Desktop reads/writes the same logical path, unaware anything moved. Verified by a full restart showing every image/container intact.
- **npm's 5.08GB cache** (unused, fully regenerable) was cleared and relocated to `D:\npm-cache` (`npm config set cache --global`). **pnpm's own cache-dir** relocated to `D:\.pnpm-cache` via a line in the user-level `~/.npmrc` rather than `pnpm config set --global`, which fails in this environment (`configured global bin directory ... is not in PATH`, a pre-existing Volta-related quirk, not something this change caused). pnpm's much larger main content-addressable store needed no action — it already defaults **per-drive** on Windows (hardlinks can't cross drives), so it was already on `D:` simply because this repo lives there.
- Also removed two completely unused cached images (`supabase/postgres`, `supabase/studio` — zero backing containers or volumes, confirming this project genuinely uses hosted Supabase Cloud day-to-day, not a local stack, even though `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` documents local Supabase as a fully supported option).
- **C: free space went from ~17.7GB to ~24.8GB.** Full monorepo `typecheck`/`test`/`build` re-verified clean afterward (377/377 backend, 9/9 frontend — this was before Delegation's own new tests existed).
- Docs updated in sync: `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` (new "Docker storage location" section — the junction procedure, cache relocation, why), `infra/jitsi/README.md` (two dated updates tying the corruption root-cause/fix back to its existing disk-space incident notes), `CONTEXT.md`'s session status line. No CONTEXT.md numbered section (this is environment/tooling, not a shippable feature) — this entry is the durable record.

#### Session 2 continued — Delegation (complete)

- **The exact item §31/§62 deferred, built now at the user's explicit re-prioritization** — rather than resuming Leave Management next, per the business-value-prioritized order. Same standing workflow as every prior submodule: 3 parallel Explore agents (multi-role-assignment support; Roles & Permissions Management's own reusable internals; role-hierarchy/grant precedents) → a Plan agent → the plan spot-verified against the live repo (exact current file paths/line numbers/signatures read directly before presenting) → `ExitPlanMode` approval → implement → test → document.
- **§31's central open fork — "grant to a role, or a user-specific override" — resolved via `AskUserQuestion`, not assumed: per-user override.** Confirmed directly with the user that "grant to a role" is now redundant with Roles & Permissions Management's own clone-role → edit → reassign flow, which didn't exist when Delegation was first deferred; building it again would just duplicate shipped capability. Two smaller forks resolved the same way: **no auto-expiry** (permanent until explicitly revoked, no poller/cron implied), and **revocation gated identically to granting** (any `role:manage` holder may revoke any delegation, not only its original grantor).
- **Research confirmed multi-role-assignment couldn't be quietly repurposed for this.** The schema/resolver already tolerate a user holding multiple `RoleAssignment` rows (`PermissionResolverService` already used `findMany`, unioning correctly) — but every write path (`user.changeRole`, HR's `replaceUserRole`) enforces exactly one row per user by explicit design (`deleteMany` + `create`, commented "a promotion/demotion replaces what someone IS, not adds a second role"). Reusing it for Delegation would have meant relaxing an intentional, differently-motivated invariant elsewhere in the codebase. Built a genuinely new model instead.
- **New `PermissionDelegation` table** (`apps/api/prisma/schema/rbac.prisma`, migration `20260804141420_permission_delegations`): one row per `resource:action:scope` triple (not a `Json` array like `Role.permissions`, so each grant revokes independently), soft-revoked (`revokedAt`/`revokedById`, same audit-trail convention as Announcement/Comment soft-delete), no relations to `User` (same no-FK treatment as `Announcement.authorId`), a partial unique index (`WHERE revoked_at IS NULL`) keeping at most one active delegation per (tenant, user, permission) — a revoked-then-re-granted triple gets a fresh row, not a reactivated one. RLS policy verified verbatim against the most recent precedent migration before writing it, not guessed.
- **The two hardest pieces were already built and are reused completely unchanged**, exactly as `assertPermissionsGrantableByActor`'s own doc comment anticipated when Roles & Permissions Management built it: `assertPermissionsGrantableByActor` gates `delegation.grant`'s escalation check verbatim; `PERMISSION_CATALOG`/`permissions.catalog` and `users.effectivePermissions` are reused unchanged by the frontend. **The one real backend addition, and the highest-risk change**: `PermissionResolverService.resolveEffectivePermissionsWithTx` now unions active delegation grants alongside role grants before `collapsePermissions` — a single additive, sequential (not `Promise.all`, same shared-`tx` discipline `isRoleAssignableBy`'s own precedent bug established) query. This one function is the sole integration point — `GET /me/permissions`, blueprint pruning, and `users.effectivePermissions` all pick up delegated grants automatically, zero changes needed to any of them.
- **`delegation.grant`/`delegation.revoke`, gated by `role:manage`** — the same permission Roles & Permissions Management already gates on, not a new one, since this is administrative capability over the same trust boundary. `grant` checks catalog-membership before escalation (same order as `role.createCustom`), pre-checks for an already-active duplicate (409) before writing, and creates rows via a sequential loop of individual `create()` calls — **deliberately not `createManyAndReturn`**, an unverified surface against this project's Prisma 7 + `PrismaPg` driver-adapter setup with zero existing precedent in this codebase (this project has been burned by exactly this class of "assume the newer API just works" mistake before — the embedding-job `updated_at` bug). `revoke` has **no** escalation check at all — deliberate: revoking only removes authority, never adds it. **Self-delegation is deliberately left unblocked** (unlike `user.changeRole`'s self-role-change guard) since `assertPermissionsGrantableByActor` already caps it at what the actor holds — blocking it would be friction with no security benefit.
- **`delegations.list`, per-user not tenant-wide** — same "two narrow, consumer-shaped data sources" discipline as `attendance.list`/`attendance.roster` and `roles.listDetailed`/`roles.list`; a delegation is only ever meaningful in the context of one specific person.
- **Frontend extends `RolesPermissionsWorkspace.tsx`'s existing `UserPermissionViewer` in place**, not a new component — it already owned the user-picker + effective-permissions state this needed. `DelegationGrantEditor` mirrors `RoleEditorPanel`'s checkbox-per-catalog-entry + scope-`<Select>` loop exactly, plus a UX-only "already held via role" badge (a redundant delegation is harmless, not blocked server-side). `ActiveDelegationsList` lists active grants with per-row revoke. Both refetch `users.effectivePermissions` after any change.
- **A real, pre-existing papercut found during live verification, not a Delegation defect**: `role.updateCustom` 400s if you resubmit a role's own current `permissions` array unchanged, because that array can contain `task:delete:*` — deliberately excluded from `PERMISSION_CATALOG` (§62's own documented dead-grant finding) — and `assertKnownPermissions` correctly rejects anything not in the catalog, including a permission the role already legitimately holds. Worked around in the verification script by filtering it out; flagged here as a reproducible papercut for any future clone-then-edit flow.
- **21 new backend tests** (`permission-resolver.service.spec.ts` — new, no prior spec existed for this service; `delegation.mutations.spec.ts`; `delegation.data-sources.spec.ts`), full backend suite **398/398 passing** (up from 377). Frontend: existing 4 files/9 tests unchanged — no new frontend unit tests, verified live instead, same precedent as every prior UI-touching submodule.
- **Verified end-to-end against a live, throwaway tenant — 31/31 checks passed**: a non-Admin `role:manage`-holding actor constructed via the module's own mechanisms (clone a low tier, add `role:manage:tenant`, invite+assign, complete the real first-login flow); the Company Admin delegating `user:invite:tenant` to one HR Manager and a second, otherwise-identical HR Manager confirmed *not* to inherit it — the load-bearing proof of the per-user-not-role-wide decision; the delegated HR Manager actually calling `user.invite` successfully — proving the grant is load-bearing at the mutation gate, not just visible in a read-only viewer; the non-Admin actor rejected (403) delegating a permission they don't hold; that same actor successfully revoking a delegation the Company Admin originally granted — the load-bearing proof of the symmetric-revocation decision; re-revoke → 404, re-grant-while-active → 409, unknown permission → 400, malformed triple → 400 not 500; a plain-tier actor 403'd on all three new endpoints; `delegation.granted`/`delegation.revoked` notifications confirmed fired to the target and skipped on self-action; and reseed-safety — a full `prisma db seed` against the live tenant left every `permission_delegations` row byte-for-byte untouched, while the two new blueprint actions correctly pruned in/out per role. One transient `ECONNRESET` hit immediately after the reseed subprocess, same class already documented in §61/§62's own verification runs — absorbed with the same 3-attempt retry-with-backoff precedent, not a product defect.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change — §0's compliance mandate is satisfied by construction: no new authorization *mechanism*, only a per-user layer unioned into the same existing resolver/scope-enum machinery. Docs updated in sync: `CONTEXT.md` §63 (new, Document map renumbered §64), `ARCHITECTURE.md` (§8.2 new table, §9.1 new module note), this entry.

#### Session 2 continued — Analytics & Insights, Phase A (complete)

- **The user's next major priority after Delegation, explicitly re-ordered ahead of resuming Leave Management** — described as wanting Analytics to become "the central intelligence layer for the entire workspace," not a standalone reporting page. Full research → architecture → design-review → approval → implementation → test → document workflow: 3 parallel Explore agents (existing analytics-adjacent code + `Dashboard`/`KpiCard`/`Chart` precedent; RBAC/org-hierarchy model + performance-audit history/standing query discipline; SDUI composite system + confirming zero existing Analytics scaffolding) → 4 `AskUserQuestion` rounds resolving genuine architectural forks → a Plan agent producing a full architecture document plus a file-level Phase A plan → the highest-risk claims spot-verified against live source a second time before writing the final plan → `ExitPlanMode` approval → implementation.
- **Four forks confirmed with the user before any code, not assumed.** (1) **Platform Owner is out of scope** — `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` §13 already treats cross-tenant Platform Owner analytics as a deliberately undesigned future subsystem (its own internal-staff auth model, bypassing every table's `tenant_id` RLS); Company Admin is the top-level dashboard audience here. (2) **The two pre-existing dead placeholders, `page.insights`/`nav.insights` (gated `user:manage`) and `page.analytics`/`nav.analytics` (gated `settings:manage`), are consolidated into one module** — `page.insights`/`nav.insights` retired entirely, `nav.analytics` opened to broad visibility with every widget independently gated by its own metric's `requiredPermission` instead of one page-level gate. (3) **Phased delivery** — Phase A (engine + Company Admin dashboard: Projects/Tasks/Attendance/Meetings) → Phase B (remaining role dashboards, real per-department trend) → Phase C (remaining modules + drill-down breadth) → Phase D (configurability/scale tuning) — approved as a sequencing plan, only Phase A built now, same precedent as the AI Assistant's A-F roadmap. (4) **"Real-time" means live-on-load + periodic background refresh, never push-based** — consistent with this codebase's own confirmed, disclosed Realtime reliability limitation from Chat (§50).
- **New `MetricRegistry`** (`apps/api/src/metrics/`), mirroring `DataSourceRegistry`/`MutationRegistry`'s exact shape — the extensibility seam that lets every current and future module contribute metrics without redesigning the engine: `ProjectsRegistrar`/`TasksRegistrar`/`AttendanceRegistrar`/`MeetingsRegistrar` each gained one constructor param and a few `this.metrics.register(...)` calls; the Analytics engine itself never changes to support a new module, it only ever iterates `metricRegistry.list()`. A future CRM module (still zero schema/code, confirmed again this pass) does the identical thing once it exists.
- **A `MetricDefinition` has two kinds — `scalar` (backs a `KpiCard`) and `breakdown` (backs a `Chart`)** — matching the two widget shapes that already existed rather than inventing a third. Every `computeLive` implementation is **required** to call the owning module's own already-correct `*Where()` builder (`projectsWhere`, `tasksWhere`, `meetingsWhere`) or, for Attendance (no single exported `attendanceWhere`), the newly-exported `resolveScopedRosterUserIds` — the single most important authorization guarantee here: a metric can never diverge from what a user can already see through the module's own real UI, because it runs the literal same query-scoping function. Directly satisfies "tenant isolation and strict RBAC so analytics never expose unauthorized data" by construction, not a separately-maintained analytics-specific check.
- **New `AnalyticsSnapshot` table — a scheduled recompute, deliberately not an outbox/job-queue like `EmbeddingJob`/`CalendarReminder`.** Those exist because a mutation enqueues a discrete, one-off unit of external work that must happen exactly once, with retries; a snapshot recompute is the opposite shape — a fixed, always-the-same list of registered snapshot-capable metrics, recomputed on a fixed 15-minute schedule, where a missed tick is harmless (the next one just recomputes "today" fresh). No `status`/`attempts`/backoff columns. `AnalyticsSnapshotProcessorService` still reuses the outbox processors' own per-tenant round-robin claiming idiom and the standing sequential-never-`Promise.all`-on-one-shared-`tx` discipline (CONTEXT.md §9) — caught and fixed once during implementation itself, not left in: an early draft of `attendanceRateByDepartmentMetric` used `Promise.all` for two reads sharing one `tx`, fixed to sequential before it ever shipped. Deliberately no DB-level unique constraint on `(tenant, metric, department, period)` — a `departmentId`-inclusive compound unique index hits Postgres's "`NULL` is distinct from `NULL`" semantics, the same nullable-column footgun class `isRowInScope`'s "own is a floor" fix and `tasksWhere`'s assignee-must-OR fix already taught this codebase; idempotency is enforced operationally instead (delete-then-reinsert *today's* rows every tick).
- **Widget-level permission pruning is genuinely new territory, not a rerun of existing pruning.** There's no static `UINode` tree for a zero-prop composite's internal widgets, so `permission-pruner.ts`'s tree-walking `pruneNode` doesn't apply directly — its private `isAllowed` was extracted into a new shared `rbac/permission-gate.ts` (`isPermissionGranted`, zero behavior change, `permission-pruner.spec.ts` still passes unmodified) so `analytics.dashboard`'s own resolve loop can reuse the identical predicate per-metric. Confirmed via exhaustive search before building this: no page anywhere in `seed.ts` had previously exercised "same page, different `requiredPermission` per sibling content widget."
- **⚠️ A real, live-verified finding worth recording, not a bug**: a Practitioner (`role.member`) and Company Admin see the **same set** of 9 widgets, not a smaller set for Practitioner — because `role.member` already holds `project:read`/`task:read`/`attendance:read` at an `"own"`-scope universal floor (the same pattern `calendarEvent:create:own` already established). Presence-only gating is working exactly as designed; the tier difference shows up in each widget's *value* (own-scoped vs. tenant-scoped), not its presence. Proving genuine per-widget absence required constructing an actor holding literally none of those three grants (an empty-permissions custom role, via Roles & Permissions Management) — confirmed live to see only the one ungated widget.
- **No new permission triples for Phase A** — every metric maps onto one existing cataloged `resource:action` pair; `meetings.heldThisWeek` has no `requiredPermission` at all, mirroring `meetings.list`'s own always-on-participant-floor precedent. `permission-catalog.ts` needed no edit — flagged as a real, deliberately deferred Phase B/C question once a genuinely cross-module aggregate is wanted.
- **`Chart` extended with a `type` prop** (`donut`/`bar`/`line`/`area`, default `donut`, every existing static call site unaffected) and an optional non-schema `onSegmentClick` — its own doc comment had explicitly anticipated this exact extension since it was first built. **`KpiCard` needed zero prop-schema change** — its `trend` array was always the right shape, it simply never had a real producer before now; its comment is updated to say so, not left stale.
- **Drill-down is composite-internal, not built on `openModal`/`openDrawer`** (confirmed still-inert no-ops) — plain local React state in `AnalyticsWidgetCard`, the same pattern `AttendanceWorkspace`/`RolesPermissionsWorkspace` already use. A new `analytics.trend` data source serves 90-day history, gated the same way as the dashboard's own trend inclusion (exactly `"tenant"` scope in Phase A).
- **40 new backend tests**, full backend suite **438/438 passing** (up from 398). Frontend: existing 4 files/9 tests unchanged — no new frontend unit tests, verified live instead, same precedent as every prior UI-touching submodule.
- **Verified end-to-end against a live, throwaway tenant — 28/28 checks passed**: real cross-module data created through the modules' own real mutations correctly reflected in live metric values; a Department Head's `tasks.completionRate` showing a correct `value` but no `trend` (Phase A's honest tenant-only-trend scoping); the zero-grant-actor proof of genuine per-widget pruning described above; drill-down from `projects.activeCount` returning real matching rows; unauthenticated access rejected (401); simulated historical snapshot rows (inserted via the elevated migration-role connection, bypassing RLS — the same class of setup access used for verification elsewhere in this project) correctly surfaced as a real multi-point `trend` once present, and confirmed **not** to leak across a second, independent tenant; `analytics.trend` correctly 403ing a non-tenant-scope caller and returning `[]` (not an error) for an unknown `metricKey`.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change — §0's compliance mandate is satisfied by construction: no new authorization mechanism, every metric's row-level scoping is the literal same code path the owning module's own UI already uses. Docs updated in sync: `CONTEXT.md` §64 (new, Document map renumbered §65), `ARCHITECTURE.md` (§8.1/§8.2 new table, §9.1 new module row), this entry.

#### Session 2 continued — Analytics & Insights, Phase B: Filters & Views (complete)

- **The natural next Analytics slice per Phase A's own approved roadmap** — "start with the Phase B," directing work on Part 8 (Executive Dashboards & Role-Based Views) + Part 9.1 (filter-aware `MetricRegistry`) + Part 10 (Global Analytics-scoped filters) of the refined BI architecture. Own dedicated plan written and approved before any code, per this project's standing practice.
- **`AnalyticsFilters` (`from`/`to`/`departmentId`/`teamId`/`projectId`/`employeeId`) threaded as a new optional third parameter onto `computeLive`** on both `ScalarMetricDefinition` and `BreakdownMetricDefinition` — additive, every one of Phase A's 9 metrics kept compiling and behaving identically unchanged. `analytics.dashboard`'s `paramsSchema` grew from `z.object({})` to accept the same optional shape (backward-compatible). **The one real safety invariant, preserved rather than newly built**: every module's own `*Where()` builder already treats a caller-supplied narrowing value as an additive `AND` on top of its own scope-derived condition, never a replacement — confirmed by re-reading `tasksWhere`'s existing `assigneeId` handling, and now proven directly by a new test asserting a `departmentId` filter for a department the actor can't access is silently clamped to their own real scope, never honored past it (verified both by unit test and live, end-to-end).
- **`tasksWhere` extended with `departmentId`/`projectId` params, applied *before* scope branching** — so every scope path (including the early "own"/"tenant" returns, easy to miss since they return before the `OR`-scope block runs) inherits the narrowing uniformly. A task has no `departmentId` of its own — reached only through `project: { departmentId }`, the same indirection the department-subtree branch already used.
- **Department-scope trend, closing Phase A's own disclosed tenant-only-trend gap** — `analytics.dashboard`/`analytics.trend`'s trend-fetch widened from "exactly tenant scope" to "tenant scope (`departmentId: null`) or department scope (the viewer's own real `departmentId`)," via a new `resolveTrendDepartmentId` helper. A department-scope viewer with no department set (shouldn't happen, but possible) falls through to "no trend," never silently substituting the wider tenant-wide row. **`"department-subtree"` (Executive tier) is left as a real, disclosed gap, not solved here** — correctly aggregating a *percentage* metric across a multi-department subtree needs the underlying raw numerator/denominator, not each department's pre-computed percentage; `AnalyticsSnapshot` doesn't store that yet. Executive-tier viewers keep a correct live `value`, simply no `trend` — the same honest gap Phase A disclosed for every non-tenant scope, now narrowed to just this one remaining tier.
- **`tasks.completionRate`/`attendance.rateThisMonth`'s `snapshot.computeDaily` extended to return one row per department alongside the existing tenant-wide row** — Attendance reuses the exact `user.departmentId` join `attendanceRateByDepartmentMetric.computeLive` already had in the same file; Tasks needed genuinely new logic (no existing per-department breakdown to copy), grouping through `task.project.departmentId` since Task itself has no department column. A record/task whose owner has no department (or whose project has none) is counted in the tenant-wide figure but excluded from every per-department row, never guessed into one. No `AnalyticsSnapshot` schema/migration change — `departmentId` was already nullable and indexed for exactly this in Phase A.
- **⚠️ A real, previously-unverified gap found and worked around, not glossed over**: `departments.list`/`teams.list`/`users.list` — the three sources the new filter dropdowns need to populate — are gated on `department:manage`/`user:manage`, held only by Company Admin and HR Manager (confirmed in `seed.ts`), not the broader set of roles a filter bar might reasonably be used by. The new `AnalyticsFilterBar` gates those three queries on `["Company Admin", "HR Manager"].some(r => user.roles.includes(r))` (mirroring the exact `enabled:`-gate precedent `TeamMembers.tsx`/`CalendarWorkspace.tsx` already use for the same sources) and simply omits the four entity dropdowns for every other tier — the date-range control and all four view-preset tabs remain fully usable regardless, and no tier ever loses access to their own real, correctly-scoped data by not seeing a picker for data they couldn't browse anyway.
- **Four named view presets (Company/Department/Team/Personal) as starting filter states, not four separate dashboards** — a deliberate unification with the filter mechanism itself, avoiding two places that both answer "what data am I looking at right now." All four shown unconditionally to every tier: the safety rule ("a filter can only narrow, never widen, what a viewer's real permissions already grant") makes tab visibility a pure UX question, not a new authorization surface. "Personal" sets `employeeId` to the viewer's own id; "Company" clears entity filters; "Department"/"Team" reveal the filter bar's own dropdowns for an explicit pick rather than auto-selecting — the manifest's `user` object doesn't carry the viewer's own `departmentId` today, a small follow-up flagged rather than built speculatively into this pass.
- **New `AnalyticsFilterProvider`/`useAnalyticsFilters`** (`apps/web/src/modules/analytics/analytics-filter-state.tsx`) — deliberately separate from the shared, page-scoped `FilterStateProvider` (which resets on every page navigation and only supports single-select filters), mirroring its context+hook *pattern* without touching the shared implementation. One provider instance per `AnalyticsDashboard` mount, not remounted on view-tab switches, so a manually-picked date range/department survives Company→Department→Team→Personal switches. `AnalyticsDashboard.tsx` split into an outer component (mounts the provider) and an inner `AnalyticsDashboardContent` (reads filter state, builds `analytics.dashboard`'s params, renders the new `AnalyticsFilterBar`/`AnalyticsViewTabs` above the existing widget grid) — required because the query params now depend on filter context, only readable from inside the provider.
- **20 new/extended backend tests**, full backend suite **453/453 passing** (up from 438). Frontend: existing 4 files/9 tests unchanged — no new frontend unit tests (no browser-automation tool in this environment), verified live instead.
