- **Verified end-to-end against a live, throwaway tenant with a real two-department tree — 11/11 checks passed**: a Company Admin's tenant-wide `tasks.completionRate` trend vs. an Engineering Department Head's own department-scoped trend, both directly seeded into `analytics_snapshots` and confirmed to return the *correct, different* 2-row history per viewer (Phase B's core unlock); a Department Head's live values narrowed to Engineering's own real seeded data; a `departmentId` filter for Sales (a department the Department Head can't access) confirmed clamped to zero rather than honored; `analytics.trend` called directly returning real department-scoped history for a Department Head, where Phase A would have 403'd; an `employeeId` filter narrowing a tenant-wide 67%-present attendance rate down to one specific practitioner's own real 0% (marked absent). Snapshot rows were seeded directly (same GUC-setting idiom `AnalyticsSnapshotProcessorService` itself uses) rather than waiting on the 15-minute poller, matching Phase A's own live-verification precedent for this exact situation.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change — no new authorization mechanism, only an additive filter parameter layered on top of each module's existing scope-derived `*Where()` builder. Docs updated in sync: `CONTEXT.md` §65 (new, Document map renumbered §66), `ARCHITECTURE.md` (§8.1/§8.2 filter-awareness note), this entry.

#### Session 2 continued — the platform owner asked for Analytics to become Purnit's flagship Business Intelligence hub

- **A much larger scope than a normal next-phase request** — not "another dashboard with charts," a redesigned interactive dashboard, ~25 widget types, a two-tier (core vs. permission-controlled) widget catalog gated through the real Roles & Permissions module, role-tailored default dashboards, full drag/resize/save customization, real cross-module composite metrics, an "executive attention" widget group, and an architecture-only pass for future AI integration. Same standing workflow as every architecture-level request before it: research → architecture → review → approval, with implementation explicitly held back until a phase is separately approved.
- **Four forks confirmed with the platform owner before drafting the architecture, not assumed**: (1) **Platform Owner dashboard stays deferred** — `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` §13's cross-tenant, RLS-bypassing subsystem remains out of scope, reaffirming Phase A's own original decision rather than silently reopening it because it appeared in the new role list. (2) **`react-grid-layout`** is the dashboard-grid library for the eventual drag/resize customization phase — confirmed via research that **no drag/resize library of any kind exists in this codebase today**. (3) **Multi-phase delivery**, roughly: visual/widget-type depth → permission-controlled widget catalog + role-based dashboard defaults → drag/resize customization → cross-module composites + executive attention → interactive Kanban/Gantt → AI-readiness (design-only) → future-module metrics. (4) **Kanban Board and Gantt Timeline widgets are fully interactive**, not read-only as recommended — a materially bigger commitment (a second, purpose-fit drag library, `@dnd-kit/core`, plus a brand-new `task.updateDueDate` mutation that doesn't exist today), sequenced into its own later phase rather than bundled with the cheaper read-only widget work.
- Research confirmed (not assumed) before designing on top of it: `recharts@3.9.2`'s real exports (`ScatterChart`, `RadarChart`, `Treemap`, `Funnel`, `Sankey`, `RadialBarChart`, `ComposedChart`) cover most requested chart types with no version bump; `ProjectBoard.tsx` has **zero** drag-and-drop today despite looking Kanban-shaped; `PERMISSION_CATALOG` is a clean, hand-maintained, freely-extensible flat list, and `settings:manage`/`role:manage` are real precedent for presence-only (scope-irrelevant-in-practice) permissions; `RolesPermissionsWorkspace.tsx`'s permission-editing UI needs **zero** changes to pick up new catalog entries; Delegation is **fully generic** — any new permission triple becomes delegable the instant it's cataloged; `Notification` is real, reusable data for an Activity Feed widget with zero new schema; **no** "approval," "at-risk," "workload," or AI-usage-aggregation concept exists anywhere yet; Recruitment/CRM/Finance/Inventory remain zero-schema.
- Full architecture (Parts 18-28) approved as a sequencing document — not a green light to build all of it now. Documented in the reused plan file; no `CONTEXT.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md` section of its own yet (an architecture-only approval, same treatment the AI Assistant's and original BI architecture's own design-approval passes got before their first phase shipped).

#### Session 2 continued — Analytics & Insights, Phase C: Visual & Widget-Type Depth (complete)

- **The first phase of the newly-approved BI hub roadmap** — "do as per the plan," directing work to begin at Phase C, the cheapest and most immediately buildable slice (no new dependency, no RBAC change, no drag-and-drop) and a prerequisite for every later phase that renders through its primitives. Own dedicated file-level plan and approval before any code, per this project's standing practice.
- **`Chart` extended, not replaced** — `type` grows to add `stacked-bar`/`scatter`/`radial` (all real, already-installed recharts exports), plus two new non-schema props (`seriesKeys` for multi-series stacking, `xKey`/`yKey` for scatter's two-numeric-dimension shape) — additive, no version bump, every existing call site unaffected, same precedent as `bar`/`line`/`area`'s own earlier addition to an originally donut-only enum.
- **9 new primitives**: `Treemap@1`/`Funnel@1` (wrap real recharts exports, kept separate from `Chart` since their data shape doesn't fit its `nameKey`/`valueKey` convention), hand-built `Heatmap@1`/`CalendarHeatmap@1` (CSS-grid + `color-mix`, same "doesn't warrant a charting library" precedent `KpiCard`'s own `Sparkline` already set), `TimelineChart@1` (the standard recharts range-bar trick — a transparent offset segment stacked under a visible duration segment), `Table@3` (sortable/filterable/**groupable**/paginated, client-side over an already-capped fetch — `Table@2` untouched, every existing usage keeps working), `ProgressGoal@1`/`Leaderboard@1` (small, new), and `ActivityFeed@1` (a thin presentational wrapper over the already-real `notifications.list` — zero new backend surface).
- **6 new small metrics**, chosen specifically to give each new render kind real data, not Phase F's later judgment-call composites: `tasks.byProjectStatus` (the first genuinely new *pivot* in this codebase — every prior breakdown metric already returned one row per category; this one reshapes a `(project, status)` groupBy into one row per project), `tasks.statusFunnel` (fixed `todo → in_progress → done` stage order, not groupBy's arbitrary order), `tasks.completionLeaderboard` (desc-sorted, displayName-joined, top 10), `tasks.assigneeWorkload` (a plain two-number `{taskCount, completionRate}` comparison per assignee — deliberately **not** Phase F's later "overloaded" composite, no threshold, no judgment call), `attendance.statusByDepartment` (extends the same scoped-roster/department-join pattern `attendanceRateByDepartmentMetric` already used, Phase B), `meetings.timeline` (14-day window, no `requiredPermission`, same always-on-participant-floor precedent as `meetingsHeldThisWeekMetric`).
- **Which primitive renders a breakdown widget stayed a frontend dispatch choice** — extending the exact `BREAKDOWN_CHART_TYPE` map precedent from Phase A rather than adding a new backend field, keeping `MetricRegistry`'s extensibility contract untouched. **One real regression avoided along the way**: the plan's first draft would have re-presented `projects.statusBreakdown` as a Treemap, but that widget is the one with real click-to-drill-down wired (Phase A) — new primitives don't support that yet, so `tasks.byPriority` (no drill-down wired) was swapped to Treemap instead, proving the new primitive without silently dropping already-shipped interactivity.
- **`AnalyticsDrillDown` upgraded, not just extended** — its `ListPanel` now renders through `Table@3` (real sort/filter/paginate) instead of a plain capped-at-20-rows div list, deriving columns from the first result row since drill-down targets return heterogeneous shapes with no per-source column config; its `TrendPanel` gained a Line/Calendar toggle, the same 90-day history rendered as either a line chart or a `CalendarHeatmap`.
- **8 new/extended backend tests**, full backend suite **461/461 passing** (up from 453). Frontend: existing 4 files/9 tests unchanged — no new frontend unit tests (no browser-automation tool in this environment), verified live instead. Full monorepo typecheck/lint/production build all clean.
- **Verified end-to-end against a live, throwaway tenant — 9/9 checks passed**: real cross-assignee task data correctly pivoted/funneled/leaderboarded/scattered; a real `(department, status)` attendance grouping correctly excluding a departmentless user's record from every per-department row (the same disclosed exclusion behavior Phase B's own tests already established, not a bug); a real meeting correctly shaped into `{label, start, end}`; `notifications.list` confirmed to have real rows for `ActivityFeed`'s binding path. **Explicitly disclosed, not silently skipped**: actual chart *rendering* (does `Treemap` visually look right, does the CSS-grid heatmap lay out correctly) cannot be verified by me — no browser-automation tool exists in this environment; backend data-shape correctness plus a clean production build are the real verification available, a manual visual pass is the user's own follow-up.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change — zero new authorization mechanism, every new metric reuses its owning module's already-correct scope-filtering function exactly like every metric before it. No `CONTEXT.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md` section of its own yet — this entry is the as-built record; a dedicated section can be added once the BI hub roadmap has shipped enough phases to warrant one, same treatment several other reused-plan-file roadmaps in this project have gotten.

#### Session 2 continued — Phase C committed and pushed to `develop` (`de66a60`); Analytics & Insights, Phase D: Permission-Controlled Widget Catalog + Role-Based Dashboard Defaults (complete)

- **"push the changes and then start with the next phase"** — Phases A-C committed as one arc (three independently-approved, independently-verified phases that had never been committed) and pushed to `develop`, then this phase's own dedicated research → plan → approval cycle before any code, same standing practice.
- **Narrowed from Part 21's 10 named example categories to 3 real, buildable ones — disclosed, not silently dropped.** Part 18's own grounding table had already flagged most as blocked (Financial/Recruitment/CRM: zero schema; Custom Reports: Phase F/Reporting's job). This phase's own research found one more: a real `AuditLog` table exists (`apps/api/prisma/schema/audit.prisma`) but has **zero writers anywhere in `apps/api/src`** — the same dead-placeholder class `permission-catalog.ts`'s own header comment already documents for `task:delete` — so "Security Analytics" is blocked too. `PERMISSION_CATALOG`'s own documented "same PR as enforcement site" discipline means a catalog entry with nothing real behind it isn't a legitimate deliverable.
- **3 new, genuinely more-sensitive widgets, each with a real enforcement site**: `department.performanceLeaderboard` (Attendance), `tasks.productivityLeaderboard` (Tasks — doubles as the closest real thing to "Sensitive Workforce Analytics"), `ai.usageSummary` (AI Assistant's first metric, summing the already-real `AiMessage.usage.{inputTokens,outputTokens}`, zero prior aggregation). Gated by a new `"Analytics"` `PERMISSION_CATALOG` module (`analytics:departmentPerformance`, `analytics:productivity`, `analytics:aiUsage`) — only `role.admin` holds them by default, matching the request's own "explicit access granted by Company Admin" framing exactly.
- **⚠️ A deliberate, explicit exception to the standing "every `computeLive` must reuse the owning module's own `*Where()` scope builder" rule, stated as the load-bearing safety property of this phase, not buried in a comment**: the two leaderboard metrics do **not** call `attendanceRateByDepartmentMetric`'s/`tasksWhere`'s own scope resolution — the new permission itself *is* the scope, always tenant-wide when granted, same shape `settings:manage`/`role:manage` already are. Safe specifically because these are brand-new permissions nobody holds by default except Company Admin — explicitly **not** applied to already-shipped widgets relied on for their normal scope-limited behavior (re-tagging `attendance.rateByDepartment` etc. was considered and rejected — it would have regressed real, already-verified visibility for non-admin viewers).
- **A real architectural finding that reshaped the "role-based dashboard defaults" build**: `DashboardLayout.roleId` must reference a genuine per-tenant, materialized `Role.id` — never a blueprint-level id — so role-level default rows can't be seeded via `seed.ts`'s static blueprint JSON like every other page/nav default. They're created for real in `AuthService.signup`, right after `materializeBlueprintRoles` resolves real `Role.id`s, one batched `createMany` (not 6 sequential creates — `materialize-roles.ts`'s own doc comment records a real prior `P2028` transaction-timeout bug from exactly that mistake inside this same signup transaction). Phase D's own slice of the new `DashboardLayout` table is section-**ordering** only (which already-permitted module sections appear in which order) — no widget-level geometry, no drag/resize, no personal overrides; Phase E extends the same `sections` JSON column additively, no second migration, same principle `TenantConfig.overrides` already proves.
- **No backfill for tenants provisioned before this phase** — a missing role template is a low-stakes cosmetic fallback to the frontend's own insertion order (a new, optional `sectionOrder` field on `analytics.dashboard`'s response, absent = no reorder), not a correctness issue worth a migration for. Verified live, not just asserted: after deleting a fresh tenant's own `DashboardLayout` rows mid-script (simulating a pre-Phase-D tenant), `analytics.dashboard` still returned every widget correctly, just with `sectionOrder: undefined`.
- **12 new/extended backend tests**, full backend suite **475/475 passing** (up from 461). Frontend: existing 4 files/9 tests unchanged — no new frontend unit tests, verified live instead. Full monorepo typecheck/lint/production build all clean.
- **Verified end-to-end against a live, throwaway tenant — 13/13 checks passed**: 6 real `DashboardLayout` rows created automatically at signup; Company Admin seeing all 3 new widgets with real data (the productivity leaderboard correctly showing both real employees' rates); a freshly-invited Practitioner correctly seeing **none** of the 3 by default, with their own, different `sectionOrder`; **Delegation granting `analytics:productivity:tenant` to that same Practitioner, live, proving Part 21's "fully generic, zero Delegation-module changes" claim for real** — they then saw the productivity leaderboard including the Company Admin's own row, data their normal `task:read` scope could never have shown them; and the graceful no-template fallback confirmed against a real deleted-row scenario.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change — no new authorization mechanism, only two metrics that are deliberately, disclosedly gated by a brand-new tenant-wide-only permission instead of their module's usual scope builder. No `CONTEXT.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md` section of its own yet, same treatment as Phase C's own entry.

#### Session 2 continued — CSS/`.next`-cache corruption bug fixed; Analytics & Insights, Phase E: Dashboard Customization (complete)

- **"check the css as it is not responsive"** — a whole-app bug report ("elements overlapping or squished," every page). Root-caused via `curl`, not guessed: `/_next/static/css/app/layout.css` 404'd despite being referenced in the page HTML — the same stale-`.next`-build-cache-corruption footgun this project has hit before (running a production `next build` and then starting `next dev` against the same `.next` directory corrupts the chunk/CSS manifests). Fixed: killed the stray dev server, `rm -rf apps/web/.next`, restarted `next dev` fresh — confirmed CSS now returns a real 49KB stylesheet. **Standing rule reaffirmed**: after every `next build` production-mode run in this project, `.next` must be deleted before the next `next dev` start, or this exact symptom recurs.
- **"continue with Phase E"** — the natural next Analytics slice per the BI-hub roadmap. Three forks confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`, all resolved to their "(Recommended)" option: **one flat free-form canvas**, not module-grouped sections (today's `AnalyticsDashboardContent` grouping is dropped as a *structural* constraint the moment widgets are freely draggable — module becomes a small label, not a box a widget is confined to, matching how real BI tools like Tableau/Power BI/Grafana actually work); **drag/resize is desktop-only, narrow screens get a read-only stacked list** below a `768px` breakpoint (touch and RGL's mouse-based drag handles don't mix); **one active personal layout per person**, no named-layouts library — `save`/`reset` only, mirroring `TenantConfig`'s own "one active version, insert-a-new-version-to-change" model rather than inventing a picker.
- **`react-grid-layout@2.2.4` (latest, only version available) turned out to be a full v2 API rewrite from the well-known v1 shape** — confirmed directly against the installed package's own `.d.ts` files before writing any implementation code, not assumed from prior knowledge (which would have been wrong): no `WidthProvider` HOC — replaced by a `useContainerWidth()` hook (`{width, mounted, containerRef}`); no separate `react-resizable` CSS dependency — one single stylesheet; nested `gridConfig`/`dragConfig`/`resizeConfig` prop objects instead of two dozen flat props. This was flagged as the plan's single highest-risk claim and was verified before implementation, as intended.
- **`DashboardLayout.sections` (Phase D's ordered `string[]` of module names) was renamed to `widgets` and reshaped to `{key, visible, x, y, w, h}[]`** — real per-widget grid geometry, not just section ordering, exactly the extension Phase D's own schema comment had already flagged as this phase's job. The migration **clears every existing row rather than reshaping Phase D's old rows in place** — a real, disclosed choice, not an oversight: consistent with Phase D's own already-accepted "a missing template is a low-stakes cosmetic fallback, not worth a migration for" principle, a tenant provisioned before this phase simply has zero `DashboardLayout` rows until a new one is saved, gracefully falling back to the frontend's own auto-computed arrangement in the meantime. Two new partial unique indexes (`dashboard_layouts_one_active_per_user`, `dashboard_layouts_one_active_per_role`) enforce "at most one active row" per keying dimension — Phase D never needed them since it never wrote a second version; Phase E's own `save` mutation does.
- **`analytics.dashboard`'s resolver now checks the caller's own personal layout first, falling back to their role's template** — a real, three-tier graceful fallback (personal → role template → frontend auto-layout), never an error at any tier. Response field renamed `sectionOrder` → `layout`, a disclosed breaking change to Phase D's own just-shipped contract, with Phase D's own tests updated in the same PR rather than left stale.
- **3 new mutations** (`dashboard-layout.mutations.ts`, this module's first — Analytics was read-only through Phase D): `dashboardLayout.save` (no `requiredPermission` — every tenant member may save their own personal layout; versions it `TenantConfig`-style, deactivate-then-create inside the same transaction, never `UPDATE` in place), `dashboardLayout.reset` (deactivates the personal row without inserting a new one, falling through to the role template), `dashboardLayout.saveAsTemplate` (`role:manage`-gated, versions the *target* role's row, not the caller's own).
- **A genuine JSONB key-order gotcha caught in verification, not a product bug**: Postgres normalizes/reorders JSON object keys on storage, so a round-tripped `Json` column's `JSON.stringify()` output doesn't byte-match the original input even when semantically identical — required a `stableStringify()` helper (recursively sorts keys) in the verification script.
- **11 new/extended backend tests**, full backend suite **486/486 passing** (up from 475). Frontend: existing 4 files/9 tests unchanged — no new frontend unit tests, same precedent every UI-touching phase has followed. Full monorepo typecheck/lint/production build all clean.
- **Verified end-to-end against a live, throwaway tenant — 9/9 API-level checks passed**: a saved layout round-trips exactly; a second save increments the version and deactivates the first (confirmed via direct DB query, never an in-place update); reset correctly falls back to the role template; `saveAsTemplate` 403s for a non-`role:manage` actor and succeeds for Company Admin, with a *different* member of that same role immediately seeing the new template default. **Explicitly disclosed, not silently skipped: the actual drag/resize/edit-mode interaction itself cannot be verified by me** — no browser-automation tool exists in this environment. The API-level round-trip is the real verification available; frontend page-load and the compiled CSS bundle (confirmed to include `react-grid-layout`'s own stylesheet, ~53KB vs. the prior ~49KB) were sanity-checked via `curl` against a freshly-rebuilt dev server, but the visual drag/resize experience is the user's own follow-up, same disclosed gap as Phase C's own chart-rendering caveat.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change — zero new authorization surface, a layout only ever reorders/hides/geometrizes widgets already present in `analytics.dashboard`'s already-permission-pruned response. No `CONTEXT.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md` section of its own yet, same treatment as Phase C/D's own entries.

#### Session 2 continued — Analytics & Insights, Phase F: Cross-Module Composites + Executive Attention (complete)

- **"do the Analytics Phase F-I first then we will move further"** — directing all four remaining BI-hub phases as the next body of work, ahead of Leave Management. Each still gets its own dedicated research → plan → approval → implement → test → verify → document → commit cycle, per this project's own standing practice, applied identically 5 times already; this entry covers Phase F only.
- **Narrowed from the architecture pass's original 5-composite/6-question list, disclosed not silently dropped.** A single invented "Project Health" score blending Projects+Tasks+Meetings+Documents would need a fabricated weighting formula with no real basis — re-scoped instead to `projects.atRisk`, a ranked list of *specific* at-risk projects with a stated, adjustable threshold, more actionable for an executive than one opaque number. "Workload Analysis" turned out to only ever need one module's data (Tasks) — never actually cross-module — so it's `tasks.overloadedEmployees`, a plain breakdown metric, not a composite. "Which departments are underperforming" needed **no new metric at all** — Phase D's `department.performanceLeaderboard` already answers it (just look at the bottom of the same ranked list). "Team Performance" (blocked on Leave Management) and "AI Insights" (no clean way to blend token usage with a productivity percentage without inventing a second formula) stay deferred, alongside "what KPIs changed this week" — which needs a genuinely different **per-row**, not per-widget, permission-gating shape no existing metric supports, flagged as a real future refinement rather than forced in under time pressure.
- **A new `CompositeMetricDefinition` kind** — `ingredients: readonly string[]` + `combine: (values) => number`, resolved lazily at request time so registration order never matters. **Gating is AND-across-every-ingredient's-own-permission, not "narrowest scope wins"** — a deliberate, more-correct departure from the original architecture pass's own sketch, since two ingredients (e.g. `task:read` and `attendance:read`) are typically entirely different `resource:action` pairs with no single scope to compare; the actual safety property the architecture pass's own prose promised ("visible only to someone who could already see all three ingredients individually") is exactly an AND. A composite serializes on the wire as an ordinary `kind: "scalar"` widget — the frontend genuinely never needs to know a value was blended, zero new widget type.
- **One composite shipped**: `employeeProductivityScore` (`module: "Cross-Module"`), an unweighted average of `tasks.completionRate` and `attendance.rateThisMonth` — both already 0-100 percentages with matching semantics. A disclosed, adjustable judgment call, not a validated model — explicitly not incorporating meeting cadence or AI usage, since neither has a clean 0-100 shape today and forcing one in would mean a second, less-defensible formula just to use more ingredients.
- **3 new breakdown metrics, each with a stated, disclosed threshold**: `projects.atRisk` (>30% of a project's own tasks overdue, floor of ≥3 tasks against small-n noise — deliberately queries `Task` scoped to the caller's already-resolved visible-project list rather than through `tasksWhere`'s own separate scope, since risk is a property of the whole project, not just what the caller happens to be individually scoped to see), `tasks.overloadedEmployees` (>1.5x the scope-mean open-task count, a self-relative threshold, not a hardcoded absolute number), and `documents.pendingApprovals` — **Documents' first-ever `MetricRegistry` presence**, `DocumentsModule`'s registrar wired to `MetricRegistry` for the first time.
- **Zero new `PERMISSION_CATALOG` entries** — a real, notable difference from Phase D. Every one of these 4 widgets rides on `project:read`/`task:read`/`attendance:read`, permissions every role already holds at some scope from `seed.ts`'s existing blueprint, rather than needing a brand-new tenant-wide-only permission nobody holds by default (Phase D's leaderboards needed exactly that).
- **`DEFAULT_DASHBOARD_WIDGET_KEYS` (Phase E) prepended the 4 new keys for Company Admin/Executive/HR Manager/Project Manager** on freshly-provisioned tenants — a pure ordering nicety, not a new authorization mechanism (Phase E's own "prune, not hide" guarantee already means a role that doesn't list a key still sees the widget, auto-appended, if their real permissions grant it).
- **A real registration bug caught by live verification, not by the unit suite**: both new metrics (`projectsAtRiskMetric`, `tasksOverloadedEmployeesMetric`) were added to their module's `*.metrics.ts` file but initially never registered in that module's own registrar's `onModuleInit()` — unit tests exercise `computeLive` directly, so they passed regardless; only the end-to-end live-verification script (checking `analytics.dashboard`'s actual response) caught the widgets being silently absent. Fixed in `projects.module.ts`/`tasks.module.ts`, confirmed via a second live-verification run.
- **17 new/extended backend tests**, full backend suite **504/504 passing** (up from 486). Full monorepo typecheck/lint/production build all clean, zero new lint warnings.

#### Session 2 continued — Analytics & Insights, Phase G: Interactive Kanban & Gantt (complete)

- Continuing "do the Analytics Phase F-I first" — Phase G's own dedicated research → plan → approval cycle. Research confirmed directly against live source (not assumed): zero `@dnd-kit/*` package installed anywhere; `Task` has no start-date field at all, only `dueDate` (nullable); `Project` has zero date-range fields (ruling out a "Gantt of projects" without a schema change); no mutation had ever touched `dueDate` post-creation (`task.create`'s own doc comment already disclaimed this); `TimelineChart.tsx`'s own doc comment already earmarked "the interactive drag-to-reschedule Gantt widget (Phase G) is a distinct, later build sharing this same rendering approach" — this fork was anticipated, not invented now.
- **One real fork surfaced by this phase's own research, resolved via `AskUserQuestion` in favor of the lower-risk option**: Kanban's card-to-column drag stayed exactly as originally decided (`@dnd-kit/core` — a mature, stable library, confirmed React-19-safe via real peer-dep check, a clean fit for its own core use case). Gantt's "dragging a bar reschedules a due date" turned out to need real scrutiny — Task's bar would need a *synthetic* start edge (`createdAt`, never itself editable), and a true drag-resize would mean raw pointer-event tracking directly on a rendered `recharts` SVG segment, a materially riskier, first-of-its-kind interaction pattern for the exact same user-facing outcome a plain click-to-edit already delivers. **Resolved: clicking a Gantt bar opens an inline due-date editor** — same new mutation either way, only the frontend interaction differs.
- **This codebase's first SDUI primitives that trigger a mutation from something other than a click on a button**: `KanbanBoard@1` (generic, bindable to any list-shaped source; drag calls a configurable `updateMutation` with `{id, [updateValueKey]: newColumn}`; reuses `TaskList.tsx`'s own established optimistic-update-then-rollback pattern) and `GanttChart@1` (reuses `TimelineChart.tsx`'s stacked-transparent-offset-bar rendering trick in a new, distinct file per that component's own comment, adds `recharts`' first-class `Bar onClick` handler — not a hack — to open the inline editor). Both demonstrated on `page.analytics`, bound directly to the already-real, already-scoped `tasks.list` — **zero new backend data source needed** for either widget.
- **One new mutation, `task.updateDueDate`** — `task:update`-gated, reuses `requireTaskInScope` verbatim, the exact same scope-check precedent `task.updateStatus`/`task.reassign` already established. `task.create`'s own doc comment (which used to disclaim "no post-creation dueDate-editing mutation" as a real gap) was updated, not just left stale — noting the new mutation deliberately does **not** retarget an already-enqueued `CalendarReminder`, an honest extension of the existing "reminder retargeting on task reassignment" gap already tracked in this section's own deferred-items list.
- **A real, disclosed footgun caught mid-verification, not a code bug**: the two new `page.analytics` nodes didn't appear in a fresh tenant's compiled manifest at first — the blueprint's compiled content lives in the `blueprints` DB table, populated by `prisma db seed`, not read live from `seed.ts` on every request (unlike backend code, which `nest --watch` hot-reloads automatically). Fixed by re-running the seed script; a standing reminder for any future `seed.ts`-only change verified against a fresh signup.
- **5 new backend tests** (`taskUpdateDueDateMutation`'s own new spec block — `tasks.mutations.ts` previously had zero coverage of its row-level mutations at all, a pre-existing gap this phase didn't retroactively close for `task.updateStatus`/`task.reassign`, out of this phase's own scope), full backend suite **509/509 passing** (up from 504). Frontend: existing 4 files/9 tests unchanged — no new frontend unit tests, same precedent every UI-touching phase has followed. Full monorepo typecheck/lint/production build all clean.
- **Verified end-to-end against a live, throwaway tenant — 14/14 checks passed**: `task.updateDueDate`'s real round trip (set, then clear via `null`); `task.updateStatus` still moving a task between Kanban columns, unmodified by this phase; both new nodes present in a fresh tenant's real compiled `page.analytics` manifest; Company Admin's own nodes carrying the correctly-pruned `task.updateStatus`/`task.updateDueDate` actions; and — the core safety proof — a constructed `task:read`-only actor (a real custom role, `role.createCustom`) still seeing both widgets rendered (read-only, not hidden) with drag/edit actions correctly absent from their own pruned manifest, **and** independently rejected with a 403 by `task.updateDueDate` itself when attempted directly — defense in depth, not a UI-only guarantee. **Explicitly disclosed, not silently skipped**: the actual drag-a-card and click-a-bar interactions themselves cannot be verified by me — no browser-automation tool exists in this environment; the mutation round-trip and the manifest's own action-pruning are the real verification available, a manual pass is the user's own follow-up, same disclosed gap as Phase E's own drag/resize caveat.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change — zero new authorization mechanism, `task.updateDueDate` reuses the identical `task:update` scope-check every other row-level task mutation already relies on. No `CONTEXT.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md` section of its own yet, same treatment as Phase C/D/E/F's own entries.

#### Session 2 continued — Analytics & Insights, Phase H: AI Insights, First Slice (complete)

- Continuing "do the Analytics Phase F-I first" — Phase H's own dedicated research → plan → approval cycle. Research confirmed directly against live source: `AiProviderService.complete()`'s exact signature; the `aiPrompt` `ActionSpec` kind and `openAiPanel()` are real, already-shipped, working code (proven by an existing passing unit test) with **zero usage anywhere in `seed.ts`** — the premise "not wired to any blueprint action yet" held; `AiPanel.tsx`'s exact auto-send mechanism; `DocumentsPanel.tsx`'s real precedent for a composite calling `openAiPanel(preset, context)` directly, gated by `aiAvailable`.
- **A genuine architectural snag surfaced by this phase's own research, resolved via `AskUserQuestion` in favor of the lower-risk, already-proven path**: the original plan called this "a simple `analytics.insights` **data source**" — but `DataSourceDefinition` has no `preResolve` hook (confirmed: only `MutationDefinition` does, and the existing `aiMessage.send` chat mutation already runs its own LLM call there specifically so external I/O never holds the DB transaction open). Building it as a data source would have held a transaction open for the entire LLM round-trip — the exact incident class `tenant-prisma.service.ts`'s own comment already documents (a slow Resend call once pushed a mutation transaction past its timeout). **Resolved: zero new backend mutation or data source at all.** The dashboard already has every widget's live value in the browser — a new "Ask AI about this dashboard" button formats that into plain text client-side and sends it through the already-shipped chat flow.
- **`AiPanel.tsx`'s existing auto-send mechanism was generalized, not replaced**: a *string* `context` is now treated as the literal first message to auto-send, instead of only ever resolving a fixed per-preset literal from `AUTO_SEND_PRESETS`. Backward-compatible by construction — `documents.summarize`/`documents.qa` always pass an object `context`, so they keep working exactly as before; a string `context` is also never forwarded as `contextRef` (it isn't a real `{sourceType, sourceId}` retrieval scope, and would fail that mutation's own Zod validation if it were).
- **`formatWidgetsForAi`** (`AnalyticsDashboard.tsx`) builds the prompt entirely from `analytics.dashboard`'s own already-permission-pruned response — no re-fetch, no new authorization surface; the values a viewer sends to the AI are exactly the ones they can already see on screen.
- **A real `AiConversation`/`AiMessage` pair is created either way** — "Ask AI about this dashboard" shows up in the user's own AI chat history and counts toward `ai.usageSummary`'s totals like any other assistant reply, confirmed live, not just asserted.
- Zero new backend tests (zero backend files changed). No new frontend unit tests — same precedent every UI-touching phase has followed. Full monorepo typecheck/lint/production build all clean, zero new lint warnings.
- **Verified end-to-end against a live, throwaway tenant — 6/6 checks passed, with a real Gemini completion call, not a mock**: `aiConversation.create` succeeding with `preset` only and no `contextRef` (the new string-context path); a real, non-empty assistant reply with a populated `usage` field; `ai.usageSummary` genuinely increasing afterward; the new conversation and both its messages (user + assistant) correctly persisted and listed. **Explicitly disclosed, not silently skipped**: the actual button click / AI panel open / auto-send UI flow cannot be verified by me — no browser-automation tool exists in this environment; the mutation-level round trip is the real verification available, a manual pass is the user's own follow-up, same disclosed gap as every prior UI-interaction phase.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change — zero new authorization mechanism; the AI panel's own existing ownership-scoped, RBAC-resource-free model is entirely unchanged, just reached from one more place. No `CONTEXT.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md` section of its own yet, same treatment as Phase C/D/E/F/G's own entries.

Expected to pioneer the approval-workflow pattern `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` §9-§10 already specifies for `User.managerId`-based routing but that nothing has used yet (Attendance deliberately did not build any approval logic — direct manager/HR correction only, no chain).

#### Session 2 continued — Healthcare Domain, Phase A: Foundation (complete) — the platform's first non-IT industry blueprint

- **A new top-level priority, directed ahead of Leave Management**: "add the Healthcare Domain, but keep it limited... The objective is to prove that the existing multi-domain architecture can support Healthcare with minimal domain-specific additions while reusing the core platform." Explicit workflow requested and followed: Research → Architecture → Review → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Documentation — two research passes (blueprint/signup/RBAC-catalog mechanics; module-genericity of Project/Task/Meeting/Calendar/Comments) before any code, a full architecture document reviewed and approved via the same plan-mode cycle every major initiative this session has used, **then** implementation.
- **The central finding, confirmed not assumed**: `Blueprint{industry, version}` (unique on the *pair*, not `industry` alone), `ConfigEngineService` (100% generic over `tenant.industry`/`tenant.blueprintVersion`), and `AuthService.signup()`'s role-materialization pipeline were **already fully generic** — nothing IT-specific to work around except one hardcoded literal (every blueprint must define a role with id exactly `"role.admin"`). `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own roadmap note used to say "everything built so far is IT-only" — this phase is what closes that gap, for real, not just in the abstract.
- **A real, disclosed gap surfaced by research, not glossed over**: the request's own reuse list named an "Approval System" and "Activity Logs" — neither exists as a generic, reusable mechanism. Only `Document.approvalStatus` (a flat, unrouted 3-value field) and a documented-but-never-built `User.managerId`-routing design (`ORG_HIERARCHY.md` §9-§10, confirmed zero real consumers) exist. Resolved by interpreting "reuse the Approval System" as reusing `Document.approvalStatus` for medical-record sign-off — genuinely reusable, zero new code — rather than improvising a real routed workflow inside this initiative (that's the same build Leave Management was already expected to pioneer).
- **Two new tables, nothing else structural**: `Patient` (owns a 1:1 auto-created "chart" `Project` — internal plumbing, never surfaced as "Project" anywhere in the Healthcare UI, letting Tasks/Documents/Comments attach for free via `projectsWhere`/`tasksWhere` completely unchanged) and `Appointment` (its own dedicated model, **not** an extension of `Meeting`/`CalendarEvent` — a Patient cannot be a `MeetingParticipant` today, confirmed via direct code read: that model resolves participant ids strictly against real `User` rows, and a Patient has no login by design). Everything else — Care Plans (Tasks), Medical Records (Documents + Comments + version history + `approvalStatus`), Dashboards, Analytics (`MetricRegistry`), AI Assistant, Chat, Meetings, Calendar, Attendance, Roles & Permissions/Delegation, multi-tenancy, auth — is 100% reused, unmodified engine, new blueprint *content* only.
- **4 new roles mapped onto the existing universal 7-tier ladder** (`ORG_HIERARCHY.md`'s own compliance rule — no new authority model): Hospital Administrator (`role.admin`), Doctor, Nurse, Receptionist — all flat (no `extends` chains, unlike the IT blueprint's tier inheritance). **A real correctness finding from research, not assumed**: `isRowInScope`'s "own" check is an *ownership* match (`Project.ownerId`), not a *membership* match (`ProjectMember`) — since a patient's chart Project is owned by whoever registered them (often the Receptionist), granting Doctor/Nurse `project:read`/`task:*`/`document:*` at `:own` scope would have incorrectly denied a doctor their own patient's chart in some cases. Resolved by keeping those three grants at `:tenant` scope for clinical roles, reserving real `:own`-scope precision for the two resources this initiative is actually about (`patient:update:own`, `appointment:read/update:own`) — that's where the genuinely demonstrable RBAC differentiation lives.
- **Deliberately no Healthcare department taxonomy in Phase A** (`departmentTypes: []`, `Patient`/`Appointment` have no `department_id` column at all) — a disclosed MVP narrowing directly matching the request's own "keep the scope small" instruction, additive to add later, not a redesign.
- **New composites `PatientsWorkspace`/`AppointmentsWorkspace`** mirror `TaskList.tsx`'s exact established shape (inline create/booking form above a list, presence-gated controls via `actions`, inline status/assignment dropdowns) rather than inventing a new UI pattern. The Healthcare blueprint's own nav/pages also reuse Analytics, Chat, Meetings, Calendar, Attendance, and Roles & Permissions verbatim (same composite types, same actions/mutations, copied as blueprint JSON since `pages` is a per-blueprint map — zero new frontend code for any of them).
- **A real bug in my own verification script, not the product**: `projects.list` itself carries `requiredPermission: "project:read"` (a data-source-level gate, not just `projectsWhere`'s internal null-return) — a Receptionist with zero `project:read` grant is correctly 403'd outright, not handed an empty list. Caught and fixed mid-verification; ended up being a *stronger* proof of the RBAC-pruning claim than originally scripted.
- **12 new backend tests**, full backend suite **545/545 passing** (up from 509). Full monorepo typecheck/lint/production build all clean, zero new lint warnings.
- **Verified end-to-end against a live, throwaway Healthcare tenant — 27/27 checks passed**, including: the compiled manifest genuinely Healthcare-flavored (nav includes Patients/Appointments, excludes Projects/Tasks) and `tenant.industry` reporting `"Healthcare"`; a Doctor assigned to a patient updating that patient successfully while a *different* Doctor is correctly 403'd (own-scope enforcement) yet still sees the patient in a tenant-wide list (broad read, for referrals/coverage); a Receptionist registering patients and booking appointments but 403'd outright from `patient.updateStatus` and from `projects.list` entirely (zero chart/medical-record access — the concrete "different roles see genuinely different things" proof); the assigned Doctor auto-added as their patient's chart `ProjectMember` at registration time, successfully creating a real care-plan Task in it; a Nurse correctly seeing every appointment tenant-wide (broader than a Doctor's own-scoped view); and — the regression check that matters most — **a fresh IT tenant signup working completely unaffected**, its nav still showing Projects/Tasks, confirming the new blueprint's addition didn't disturb the existing one at all.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change — every Healthcare permission reuses the identical tier ladder/scope enum/`isRowInScope`/`*Where()` mechanisms every other module already relies on; no new authorization concept anywhere. Documentation fully synced across `CONTEXT.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md`/`CHANGELOG.md` **and** `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` (a new implementation-record addendum confirming the universal tier ladder holds for a genuinely different industry — the first real proof of that document's own "identical whether you're in Engineering, HR, or Sales" claim — and recording the `isRowInScope` ownership-vs-membership nuance found during this work). **Phases B (Calendar integration), C (Dashboards & Analytics), D (Medical Records UI + AI presets) remain approved-but-unbuilt** — each needs its own dedicated research → plan → approval cycle before code, same standing practice as every phase of the Analytics BI-hub before it.

That array only curates *initial ordering*, never *membership*. `DEFAULT_DASHBOARD_WIDGET_KEYS` still gained 3 new entries (`role.doctor`/`role.nurse`/`role.receptionist`) for a cleaner first-run dashboard — `role.admin`'s existing shared entry was deliberately left untouched.

Rather than inventing new `patients.count`-style plain data sources to mirror that bespoke style, Healthcare's `page.dashboard` deliberately stays its Phase A placeholder welcome heading (the stale "Phase C enriches this later" code comment was resolved, not silently left dangling) — role-specific widget curation is fully delivered through `page.analytics` instead, consistent with the original request's own "reuse the existing dashboard and analytics framework... do not redesign the dashboard" instruction.

#### Session 2 continued — Healthcare Domain, Phase D: Medical Records UI + AI (complete) — the last approved phase, initiative now closed out

- The user directed "Continue with Healthcare's remaining phases." Same full research → architecture/plan → review → approval → implementation → testing → documentation cycle followed.
- **A real, pre-existing gap confirmed by research, not glossed over**: no per-record detail-view routing exists anywhere in this codebase — a Patient Profile *page* as its own route isn't buildable without new routing infrastructure outside this phase's own minimal-footprint mandate (`CONTEXT.md` §16 already disclosed this: "no mechanism yet for a page parameterized by a specific record's id"). Resolved by reusing `ProjectBoard.tsx`'s own established list+inline-expand-per-row pattern instead — no new UI pattern, no new routing.
- **`PatientsWorkspace.tsx` gained a per-row expand toggle mounting `DocumentsPanel`/`CommentThread` completely unmodified** — a Patient's chart `Project.id` (`chartProjectId`, already returned on every `patients.list` row since Phase A) is all `DocumentsPanel` needs. No `entityType: "patient"` widening to Comments either: `DocumentsPanel` already nests a per-document `CommentThread(entityType:"document")`, which is exactly what "support comments... through the existing document system" (the original request's own words) asks for.
- **A real gap found by research, not assumed away**: `patient:read` (gates the Patient row) and `project:read` (gates its chart's Documents) are different permissions — Receptionist holds the former tenant-wide and zero of the latter, so mounting `DocumentsPanel` unconditionally would have 403'd inside the panel rather than failing gracefully. Resolved with one small, precise backend addition — `patients.list` now returns a `chartVisible: boolean` per row, computed by reusing `projectsWhere` unchanged (bounded to just that page's own chart-project ids, never every visible project tenant-wide) — rather than a fragile client-side permission heuristic. The expand toggle simply doesn't render when `chartVisible` is false.
- **`page.patients`'s blueprint node gained the identical `document.*`/`comment.create` action entries `page.projects`'s own node already has** — letting `PatientsWorkspace.tsx` derive `canCreateDocuments`/`canUpdateDocuments`/`canDeleteDocuments` the exact same way `ProjectBoard.tsx` does, zero new mechanism.
- **Two new AI entry points, both reusing Phase H's exact client-formatted-prompt pattern** (`openAiPanel(preset, plainStringContext)` — confirmed `preset` is unregistered/free-text server-side, zero backend change needed, same as Phase H's own `"analytics.insights"`): "Summarize Patient Notes" (formats the patient's own fields + its document list, both already-fetched/deduped via the existing TanStack Query cache) and "Summarize Appointments" (formats `AppointmentsWorkspace.tsx`'s own already-fetched rows, zero new fetch — deliberately visible to Receptionist too, since appointment scheduling isn't chart-specific, unlike Documents). The third requested capability, "searching medical documents," needed **zero new code at all** — `DocumentsPanel.tsx`'s existing per-document "Ask AI about this document" button already satisfies it the moment Documents is mounted on a patient.
- **Both new prompts bake an explicit "do not suggest a diagnosis or treatment" instruction directly into the formatted text itself** — a real, user-visible enforcement of the original request's own "no diagnosis/treatment-recommendation" constraint, not just an unread backend policy note.
- **2 new backend tests**, full backend suite **566/566 passing** (up from 564). Full monorepo typecheck/lint/production build all clean, zero new lint warnings.
- **Verified end-to-end against a live, throwaway Healthcare tenant — 18/18 checks passed**, including: `chartVisible` correctly `true` for Admin/Doctor/Nurse (`project:read:tenant`) and `false` for every Receptionist row (no `project:read` grant at all); `documents.list` against a patient's chart succeeding for Nurse (read-only — `project:read:tenant` with zero `document:*` mutation grants) and 403ing for Receptionist (`assertProjectVisible`); and a full real document upload (signed URL → Supabase Storage upload → finalize) plus a comment on it round-tripping correctly via `comments.list`. **Explicitly disclosed, not silently skipped**: the actual rendered expand toggle, the two new AI buttons, and any live LLM round trip from them cannot be verified by me — no browser-automation tool exists in this environment; the formatter functions are directly source-reviewable and `openAiPanel`'s call site was already proven correct at the mutation level by Phase H's own live Gemini round trip.
- Confirmed `ORG_HIERARCHY.md` needs no change — zero new authorization mechanism. No `CONTEXT.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md` section of its own beyond the Healthcare Domain row's own update, now marked complete.
- **Healthcare Domain is now complete — all 4 approved phases shipped, tested, and live-verified** (A: Foundation 27/27, B: Calendar Integration 6/6, C: Dashboards & Analytics 29/29, D: Medical Records UI + AI 18/18), proving the platform's config-driven multi-domain architecture holds for a genuinely different industry with only two new Prisma models (`Patient`, `Appointment`) and blueprint *content* — zero core-engine changes anywhere across all four phases.

#### Session 2 continued — Platform-Wide UI/UX Redesign, Phase A: Design Foundation (complete) — a new top-level priority

- The user directed a full platform-wide UI/UX redesign once the core platform and Healthcare Domain were both complete: "a consistent, modern, enterprise-grade design system... every module and every domain... Before implementing anything, prepare a complete UI/UX design system and architecture proposal for review." Full Research → UI/UX Architecture → Design Review → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Documentation workflow followed.
- **Three research passes before writing any architecture**, confirming rather than assuming: the existing "obsidian" design-system foundation (`CONTEXT.md` §38, shipped 2026-08-02) only ever restyled the Dashboard *page content* — every other module, including the entire Healthcare domain, still renders with shell-level tokens only (fonts/icons/sidebar chrome); the full `ui/` component library (`Button`, `Card`, `Input`, `Select`, `Badge`, `Alert`, `Toast`, `Dropdown`, `Skeleton`) is entirely hand-built with zero external UI-library dependency and is 100% token-driven via Tailwind utility classes; real gaps confirmed by direct inspection, not assumed — no `Dialog`/`Tabs`/`Tooltip`/`Checkbox`/`Radio`/`Textarea`/`Switch`/`Pagination` exist anywhere, every list-style composite renders an unpaginated flat `.map()` (the one real exception, `Table@3`, is fully built and registered but used in exactly one hand-wired spot, not through any blueprint page), form validation is hand-rolled 17 separate times with no shared hook, and the header has no search input/breadcrumbs/workspace-switcher at all (a `CommandPalette` exists and genuinely works but has zero visible trigger — keyboard-shortcut-only).
- **Two real forks resolved via `AskUserQuestion` before writing the architecture, not assumed**: (1) redefine the color foundation toward a **warm, neutral aesthetic**, replacing "obsidian" rather than evolving it; (2) the requested "workspace switcher" is **cosmetic profile-menu polish only** — schema research confirmed `User.tenantId` is a single required scalar FK (one login = exactly one workspace today, no join table), so real cross-workspace switching would be its own separate, much larger backend initiative, explicitly out of scope for a UI-only redesign.
- **A 13-part architecture document** was written and approved as a sequencing plan: a concrete new token specification (§3 below), a component-library plan distinguishing pure restyles from genuine gaps, shared list/table/form infrastructure, a navigation-shell plan, a Dashboard-redesign plan that explicitly **reuses Analytics' own already-shipped `DashboardLayout` customization engine** rather than building a second drag/resize/save mechanism from scratch, a batched module-by-module rollout (G1–G7), an Analytics visual-polish-only pass (explicitly no new metrics/primitives/engine changes, per the user's own instruction to preserve the already-built architecture), and an accessibility/performance close-out — **9 phases total (A–I)**, each needing its own dedicated research → plan → approval cycle before code, same standing workflow as every prior multi-phase roadmap (Analytics BI-hub, Healthcare Domain).
- **New palette**: light `#faf8f5` warm-ivory bg / `#b5651d` terracotta-copper accent; dark `#211e1b` warm near-black (brown undertone, not pure `#0a0a0a`) / `#e07a45` accent — every other hue (danger/success/warning/info) chosen for clear separation from the accent and from each other. Softened radii (8/12/16px, up from 6/8/12px). **Two entirely new token categories that didn't exist before**: a warm-tinted `color-mix`-based shadow/elevation scale (`--shadow-xs` through `--shadow-xl`, mapped into Tailwind's stock `shadow-*` utilities — zero per-component change needed) and a motion vocabulary (`--duration-fast/base/slow`, `--ease-standard`) deliberately scoped to `color`/`background-color`/`border-color`/`opacity`/`transform` only, never layout-affecting properties, so the "smooth transitions and micro-interactions" ask can't cost a performance regression.
- **The single highest-leverage move of this whole phase, confirmed by research not assumed**: every existing `ui/` component consumes the color/radius tokens via ordinary Tailwind utility classes (`bg-surface`, `text-text`, `border-border`) rather than hardcoding values — so rewriting `globals.css`'s raw hex values re-themes the *entire application* with **zero per-component code changes**. Phase A is deliberately scoped to prove exactly this before Phase B touches any component file.
- **Two real, pre-existing bugs fixed as part of this same pass**: `apps/web/src/sdui/node-error-boundary.tsx` and `unknown-node-fallback.tsx` were using raw inline hex styles (`color: "#c33"`) instead of the token system — the one place in the codebase where error-state visuals fell outside the `Alert`-token convention used everywhere else. Migrated to `Alert`-equivalent token classes (`border-danger/20 bg-danger/10 text-danger` for the error boundary, `border-dashed border-border text-text-muted` for the unknown-node fallback).
- **Zero new backend surface, zero business-logic change** — this phase touched exactly 3 frontend files, no API/schema/permission changes of any kind.
- **Verified at the build level**: the compiled production CSS bundle was grepped directly to confirm every new hex/shadow/radius token value is genuinely present, and every old "obsidian" value (`#0070f3`, `#4f46e5`, `#0a0a0a`) is fully gone, not just theoretically replaced in source. Frontend typecheck/lint (6 pre-existing warnings, 0 new, 0 errors)/9 tests/production build all clean. **Explicitly disclosed, not silently skipped**: the actual visual result — does the new palette genuinely read as "warm, premium, enterprise-grade" — is a real aesthetic judgment call no build-level check can confirm; no browser-automation tool exists in this environment, the same disclosed gap every UI-touching phase before this one has carried. Your own browser review is the real verification step before Phase B (component-library restyle + gap-fill) proceeds.

#### Session 2 continued — Platform-Wide UI/UX Redesign, Phase B: Core Primitive Library (complete)

- The user directed "go for Phase B." Same full research → plan → approval → implementation → testing → documentation cycle followed, with a research pass confirming `Dropdown.tsx`'s click-outside pattern and `CommandPalette.tsx`'s backdrop/Escape/overlay pattern (the two closest existing precedents for the new `Dialog`), `Table3`'s exact current pagination implementation (for a faithful extraction), and — via an exhaustive search — that **no focus-trap mechanism exists anywhere in this codebase today**, confirming `Dialog`'s Tab-cycling focus-trap and focus-restore-on-close are genuinely new patterns, not adapted from an existing one.
- **Additive-only extensions to 5 existing primitives, zero existing call site affected**: `Button` gained an `lg` size; `Input`/`Select` gained optional `label`/`error`/`hint` props (a bare call with none of these renders the exact same unwrapped element as before — confirmed as the explicit backward-compatibility rule going in); `Alert` gained `success`/`warning` tones; `Toast` gained `info`/`warning` tones. `sdui/primitives/Badge.tsx`'s tone enum was widened to match `ui/Badge`'s own already-wider tone set (`info`/`accent` added) — purely additive, no existing blueprint content invalidated.
- **8 net-new hand-built primitives** (`Dialog`, `Tabs`, `Tooltip`, `Checkbox`, `Radio`, `Textarea`, `Switch`, `Pagination`) — confirmed zero new npm dependency added (`package.json`'s full dependency list checked directly: no Radix/Headless UI/react-aria/MUI/Chakra/shadcn), matching the zero-external-UI-library convention every existing primitive in this codebase already follows. `Checkbox`/`Radio`/`Switch` use a `sr-only` native input + `peer-checked:` CSS pattern (real keyboard/screen-reader support for free from the native element, no custom keydown handling needed). `Tabs` uses the WAI-ARIA "automatic activation" roving-tabindex pattern (Arrow keys move and activate). `Tooltip` is deliberately CSS-only (`group-hover`/`group-focus-within`, no JS state, no positioning-engine dependency) since a simple above-trigger bubble needs no collision detection.
- **`Dialog` is the one genuinely new interaction mechanism this phase introduces**: adapts `CommandPalette`'s own backdrop/Escape/overlay pattern, plus a hand-built focus-trap (queries all focusable elements within the panel, wraps `Tab`/`Shift+Tab` at the first/last) and focus-restore-on-close (captures `document.activeElement` before opening, restores it on unmount) — both confirmed as first-of-their-kind in this codebase, not existing patterns being reused, so they got dedicated test coverage rather than being assumed correct.
- **`Pagination` extracted verbatim from `Table3`'s own prior inline pagination JSX** (`sdui/primitives/Table.tsx`) — same page-clamping logic, same self-guarding `onChange` calls, behavior-identical; `Table3` now consumes it instead of inlining its own copy. The `page`/`currentPage`/`totalPages` calculation itself stayed in `Table3` unchanged, only the JSX moved.
- **A real empirical finding caught during build-level verification, not assumed correct from the plan**: Tailwind v4's `duration-*` utility generator does **not** support arbitrary-named theme keys the way `ease-*`/`shadow-*`/`radius-*` already confirmed working in Phase A do — the plan's own `duration-fast`/`duration-base` bare utility classes silently failed to compile (present in source, absent from the built CSS, no error). Caught by the same "grep the compiled bundle" verification discipline Phase A established, not assumed to work because the plan said so. Fixed across all 11 affected call sites by referencing the CSS variable directly via Tailwind's arbitrary-value syntax (`duration-[var(--duration-fast)]`) instead of relying on theme-key-to-utility-class generation — re-verified by grepping the rebuilt bundle for the literal `transition-duration:var(--duration-fast)`/`var(--duration-base)` declarations.
- **12 new frontend tests** (`Pagination.test.tsx`, `Dialog.test.tsx`, `Tabs.test.tsx` — real interaction testing via `fireEvent`/focus assertions, not just render checks), full frontend suite **21/21 passing** (up from 9). Full monorepo typecheck/lint/production build all clean, zero new lint warnings, bundle size essentially flat despite 8 new components. Backend untouched — zero backend files touched, zero business-logic change.
- **No composite/module file adopts any of these new primitives yet** — deliberately out of scope for this phase, per the plan's own explicit scoping (Phase C onward, once the shared list/form infrastructure exists to adopt them into).
- **Explicitly disclosed, not silently skipped**: the actual visual/interaction feel (does the focus-trap feel right, does the tooltip fade nicely) still needs the user's own browser review — same disclosed no-browser-automation gap as Phase A, compounded here by the fact these are genuinely new interactive components with no page wiring them in yet to click through.

#### Session 2 continued — Platform-Wide UI/UX Redesign, Phase D: Form Validation Layer (complete)

- The user directed "go with next phase." Same full research → plan → approval → implementation → testing → documentation cycle followed, re-confirming the exact `creating`/`createError`/guard-clause pattern (`PatientsWorkspace.tsx` re-read in full) is unchanged since no prior phase of this redesign has touched a module composite yet, by design.
- **Two small, independently-adoptable hooks rather than one large one, per a real distinction found in the actual call sites**: the existing pattern is genuinely two separable concerns — async-submit boilerplate (present in essentially every handler, not just "create" forms; some show the error inline via `Alert`, others push straight to `useToast()`) and client-side field validation (today just a guard-clause silently disabling the submit button, never a real per-field message). `useAsyncAction` addresses the first, supporting both existing call-site shapes without forcing either. `useFormValidation` addresses the second.
- **A real design constraint confirmed from the actual call sites before writing any code, not assumed**: existing composites don't share one combined "form values" object — each hand-declares a separate `useState` per field. `useFormValidation` is deliberately designed to validate a plain object collected only at the call site from a composite's own already-existing separate variables, so adopting it later (Phase G) never forces a values-object rewrite.
- **A real generic-constraint bug caught by typecheck, not shipped broken**: `useFormValidation`'s first draft bounded its generic parameter to `T extends Record<string, unknown>`. TypeScript rejects a plain field-shape interface with no explicit index signature — exactly the shape every real composite's own fields are declared as — as satisfying that constraint. Caught immediately by the monorepo typecheck gate on the very first `use-form-validation.test.ts` run, fixed by loosening the bound to `T extends object` (the hook's own implementation needed no other change) and re-verified clean.
- **3 small composable validators** (`required`, `email`, `minLength`) — each a plain `(value) => string | undefined` function matching `useFormValidation`'s own contract exactly, not a validation-schema DSL. `email()` is deliberately a no-op on an empty string, deferring the "is it present" question to `required()` so the two compose independently on an optional-but-format-checked field.
- **Zero new npm dependency** (no `react-hook-form`/`Formik`), matching every prior phase of this redesign and the user's own "avoid unnecessary UI libraries" instruction.
- **21 new tests** (`use-async-action.test.ts`/`use-form-validation.test.ts`/`validators.test.ts` — real async-state and validation-logic coverage via `renderHook`/`act`, deferred-promise control for the pending-state transition), full frontend suite **54/54 passing** (up from 33). Full monorepo typecheck/lint/production build all clean, zero new lint warnings, bundle size unchanged. Zero backend files touched, zero business-logic change.
- **Nothing adopts either hook yet** — same "build the infra, don't touch a composite" discipline every phase of this redesign has followed so far (A: tokens, B: primitives, C: list infra); migrating real forms onto `useAsyncAction`/`useFormValidation` is explicitly Phase G's job.
