#### Session 2 continued — Platform-Wide UI/UX Redesign, Phase F: Dashboard Redesign (complete)

- The user directed "go for Phase F." Same full research → plan → approval → implementation → testing → live-verification → documentation cycle followed — **the first phase of this redesign to touch real backend logic**, not just frontend infrastructure or the shell, so it's also the first to get a full live-verification pass against a throwaway tenant.
- **A shallow reading of the architecture's own "reuse Analytics' DashboardLayout customization engine" framing could suggest literally embedding the `AnalyticsDashboard` composite on the Dashboard page — research disproved this outright before any code was written**: `analytics.dashboard`'s resolve loop only ever iterates `MetricRegistry.list()`, structurally incapable of producing a raw entity-list widget like "Recent Projects" (every entry is scalar/breakdown-shaped, never a row list). The honest reuse, narrowed and disclosed rather than forced to match the plan's own wording: the `DashboardLayout` persistence/versioning **data model and mutations are 100% shared**; the **frontend chrome is a fresh, independent primitive**.
- **`dashboardKey` generalization**: `dashboardLayout.save`/`.reset`/`.saveAsTemplate` (`apps/api/src/modules/analytics/dashboard-layout.mutations.ts`) each gained an additive `dashboardKey: z.string().default("analytics")` input, every previously-hardcoded `"analytics"` literal replaced by `input.dashboardKey` — 100% backward compatible by construction, since every existing Analytics call site never passes it. New `dashboardLayout.get` data source (`dashboard-layout.data-sources.ts`) extracts the exact personal→role-template→undefined 3-tier fallback `analytics.dashboard` already computed inline, now parameterized by `dashboardKey` — `analytics.dashboard` itself deliberately left calling its own inline copy rather than this new data source, a disclosed choice to avoid touching already-shipped Analytics code in this phase.
- **`DashboardGrid@1`** (`apps/web/src/sdui/primitives/DashboardGrid.tsx`, new) — a full drag/resize/edit-mode/Manage-Widgets/Save/Reset/Cancel reimplementation of `AnalyticsDashboard.tsx`'s own chrome (confirmed widget-shape-agnostic: it only ever reads `key`/`label`+grid geometry, never a metric-specific field), but operating on ordinary blueprint `childNodes` (each independently `bind`-fetched) instead of a server-computed widget catalog — the catalog is simply whatever children the blueprint authored. Deliberately a fresh, parallel implementation, not shared code with `AnalyticsDashboard.tsx`, to avoid any risk to that already-shipped component (its own visual-polish pass, Phase H, stays scoped to "no engine changes"). `autoLayout`/`mergeLayout` are exported pure functions for direct testability, matching the plan's own precedent.
- **Two originally-distinct requested sections collapsed into already-real data, disclosed not silently merged**: "Activity timeline" + "Notifications" → one `ActivityFeed` widget (no separate activity-log backend exists anywhere in this codebase — only `Document.approvalStatus` and a dead, zero-writer `AuditLog` table); "Upcoming meetings" + "Calendar overview" → one `List` widget bound to `calendar.list`, already a properly time-windowed, ascending-sorted aggregator across meetings/events/tasks/appointments (`meetings.list` itself has no upcoming-only mode and would have needed its own real backend change to deliver alone).
- **A second small, disclosed backend change**: `calendar.list`'s `from`/`to` params (`apps/api/src/modules/calendar/calendar.data-sources.ts`) — previously both required — are now optional, defaulting server-side to a rolling today→+14-day window when omitted. A static blueprint-authored widget has no way to compute "today" at seed-authoring time, so this was required to make the "Upcoming" widget work at all through the standard `bind` mechanism; every existing caller (`CalendarWorkspace`) always passes both explicitly, so this is additive with zero behavior change for it.
- **`List@1` primitive's first real blueprint adoption anywhere** (previously registered, confirmed zero live usage) — gained additive `title`/`limit` props (`apps/web/src/sdui/primitives/List.tsx`), now wraps its output in `Card`/`CardHeader`/`CardBody` (previously no card wrapper) for its two new appearances, "Recent Projects" (bound to `projects.list`) and "Upcoming" (bound to `calendar.list`).
- **A dedicated Dashboard-specific "Ask AI" button was scoped in the plan but dropped on inspection — a real finding, not a shortcut taken**: no blueprint-level conditional-visibility mechanism exists anywhere in this SDUI system to gate a node on the tenant-level `manifest.aiAvailable` flag the way every other AI entry point in this codebase does in real component code (`AnalyticsDashboard.tsx`, `PatientsWorkspace.tsx`, etc. all gate the *button's rendering* on it, not just the click handler) — an ungated blueprint button would open a broken panel for a tenant with no AI configured. The header's own "Ask AI" button (`workspace/layout.tsx`, already gated on `aiAvailable`, already visible on every page including Dashboard) already covers this ask without new, riskier surface area.
- **`page.dashboard` (IT blueprint only — Healthcare's own stays its deliberate Phase-C-era placeholder heading, unchanged) restructured**: the greeting `Heading` stays outside a new `DashboardGrid` (`dashboardKey: "dashboard"`) wrapping the 3 existing `KpiCard`s (un-nested from their old wrapping `Grid`), the existing `Chart`/`TaskList`/`ProjectBoard`/`QuickActions` (content, `bind`, and mutation actions copied verbatim, unchanged — "keep all existing functionality intact" honored literally, not curated away in favor of the new lighter widgets), plus the 3 new widgets above.
- **13 new backend tests** (`dashboardKey` defaulting/scoping across all 3 mutations via the exported `inputSchema`, `dashboardLayout.get`'s 3-tier fallback and `dashboardKey` scoping, `calendar.list`'s new rolling-default and still-honors-explicit-params cases), full backend suite **579/579 passing** (up from 566). **12 new frontend tests** (`DashboardGrid`'s exported `autoLayout`/`mergeLayout` pure functions — matching `AnalyticsDashboard.tsx`'s own long-established "test the pure logic, not the `react-grid-layout` drag interaction" precedent, since it has no dedicated test file either; `List`'s new `title`/`limit` rendering), full frontend suite **81/81 passing** (up from 69).
- **A real type-safety catch during typecheck, not shipped broken**: adding `.default("analytics")` to `dashboardKey` made the mutations' inferred input type (`z.infer`, the post-parse *output* shape) require the field non-optionally — correctly rejecting several pre-existing direct `.resolve(...)` test calls that omitted it (those calls bypass the framework's own `inputSchema.parse()` step that would normally supply the default). Fixed by passing `dashboardKey` explicitly in those calls (matching what real parsed input looks like) and moving the "does it actually default" assertions to the schema itself (`inputSchema.parse(...)`), the technically correct place to test Zod's own defaulting behavior.
- **One real new-file lint warning, caught and fixed before shipping**: `DashboardGrid.tsx`'s `children` array (`childNodes ?? []`) was recreated on every render while feeding a `useMemo` dependency — the exact same warning class 5 pre-existing composites (`AnalyticsDashboard.tsx`, `CalendarWorkspace.tsx`, `ChatWorkspace.tsx`, `OrgStructure.tsx`) already carry and were left alone (outside this phase's scope) — fixed here since the file is brand new, by wrapping `children` in its own `useMemo`.
- Full monorepo typecheck/lint/test/production-build clean on both sides.
- **Fully live-verified against a real, throwaway IT tenant — 12/12 checks passed**: had to re-run `pnpm prisma:seed` first to push the new `page.dashboard` content into the shared `Blueprint` table — the same "blueprint content lives in a DB row, not read live from `seed.ts`" footgun Analytics Phase G already documented, re-confirmed rather than re-learned the hard way; the live `page.dashboard` fetch then matched the new structure exactly. `dashboardLayout.save`/`.get` round-tripped correctly under `dashboardKey: "dashboard"` while the tenant's pre-existing `"analytics"`-keyed role-template layout stayed completely unaffected throughout, checked both before and after the dashboard-key save (proving no cross-key collision); `.reset` correctly fell through to empty (no role-level `"dashboard"` template exists for this tenant). `calendar.list` called with **no params at all** returned 200, not a validation error, proving the new optional-`from`/`to` rolling default is wired correctly end-to-end. `projects.list`/`notifications.list` both returned correctly-scoped (empty, fresh-tenant) data with no errors. Throwaway tenant and its Supabase Auth user fully deleted afterward (Postgres rows in dependency order, then the Auth user), dev server stopped cleanly.
- No `CONTEXT.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md` dedicated section of its own — same "Last updated" banner + this entry treatment every phase of this redesign (and the Healthcare Domain before it) has used, per the established recent convention.

#### Session 2 continued — Frontend Structural Redesign, Phase 0: Foundation (complete) — supersedes the Platform-Wide UI/UX Redesign roadmap (Phases A-F above stay shipped, their infrastructure now finally adopted)

- The user reviewed the shipped Phase A-F work and said it read as a color-palette pass over the existing page structure, not the rebuild they'd asked for — real, correct feedback. A new architecture proposal was written from scratch (research: three Explore passes across the whole frontend), targeting page **layout, information hierarchy, navigation model, and interaction depth** this time, not tokens. `AskUserQuestion` resolved the one genuinely consequential fork before writing it: single-record views get **dedicated full-page routes** (`/workspace/<module>/[id]`), not a slide-over panel — the bigger option, chosen deliberately, since no per-record routing exists anywhere in this codebase.
- **The structural problem, confirmed by direct reading, not assumed**: all 8 core workspace composites (`ProjectBoard`, `TaskList`, `CalendarWorkspace`, `MeetingsWorkspace`, `DocumentsPanel`, `ChatWorkspace`, `AnnouncementsWorkspace`, `AttendanceWorkspace`) shared the identical shape — one `Card`, an inline toggle-to-reveal create-form, a flat unpaginated list, "more info" always an in-place expand-toggle, never real navigation. A grep across `apps/web/src/modules/**` confirmed `Dialog`/`Table@3`/`AsyncListState`/`usePagination`/`useBulkSelection`/`useAsyncAction`/`useFormValidation` — all real, tested infrastructure the old roadmap's own Phases B-D built — had **zero adoption anywhere**; `Table@3`'s only consumer in the whole app was `AnalyticsDrillDown.tsx`.
- **The routing gap**: `/workspace/[pageId]/page.tsx` fetches a compiled, permission-pruned *blueprint page* tree — entity-agnostic, no concept of "show me project X." New per-record routes are a **sibling** route type (`apps/web/src/app/workspace/<module>/[id]/page.tsx`), plain hand-written Next.js pages (same category as `ChatWorkspace`/`AnalyticsDashboard`/`login`/`signup`), not a Configuration Engine or manifest-schema change. Next.js's own segment-depth resolution means zero collision with `[pageId]` (`/workspace/projects` still matches `[pageId]`; `/workspace/projects/<id>` matches the new route).
- **New backend surface, small and fully disclosed**: `project.detail`/`task.detail` (new, additive, read-only data sources), following `document.detail`'s own already-shipped precedent exactly — fold `id` into the entity's own existing `projectsWhere`/`tasksWhere` scope function, one `NotFoundException` for both "doesn't exist" and "out of scope," never leaking which. **A real gap found and closed, not glossed over**: a hand-written route page has no pruned `actions` array to read capability from the way a blueprint-driven composite does — both new sources gained computed `canUpdate`/`canDelete`/`canManageMembers`/document-capability boolean flags, mirroring `patients.list`'s own already-shipped `chartVisible` precedent (`ctx.effective.has(...)`, the same primitive every mutation's `requiredPermission` check already uses — no new authorization concept).
- **A real, load-bearing primitive gap found and fixed, not routed around**: `Table@3`'s existing row-click-to-navigate only ever resolves a *static* blueprint page id (`ActionSpec.to: string` dispatched via the SDUI `navigate(pageId)` function) — no way to express "go to *this row's* own dynamic href." Fixed with a small, additive `onRowClick?: (row) => void` prop (a plain-React escape hatch, invisible to the Zod schema since a function can't be blueprint-authored; defaults to today's exact behavior when omitted). A new `Table.test.tsx` confirms `onRowClick` takes precedence when both are given, and that the existing `navigateAction` dispatch still works unchanged when it's absent — the `AnalyticsDrillDown.tsx` consumer is unaffected.
- **New shared patterns** (`apps/web/src/ui/`): `PageHeader` (title/description/actions/search/filters/view-switcher, replacing every module's own ad hoc header row), `ViewSwitcher` (the List/Board/Table pill toggle), `Avatar` (initials chip, replacing plain assignee/owner text), `DetailPageShell` (back-link + title/status/actions + tab strip + two-column body with a metadata rail, collapsing to one column below `lg`) — plus `apps/web/src/sdui/use-entity-detail.ts`, a thin `useDataSourceQuery` wrapper with consistent not-found handling (recognizes a 404 `ApiError`, not a string-matched error message).
- **Projects is the first fully-redesigned module — proof the whole pattern works before it's copied everywhere else**: `ProjectBoard.tsx` rewritten with `PageHeader` + `ViewSwitcher` (Board/Table/List, all bound to the same `projects.list` data), a real drag-and-drop Board view finally adopting `KanbanBoard@1` (previously demoed only on the Analytics page) wired to the already-existing `project.update` mutation — needed one small, disclosed `seed.ts` addition (a `project.update` action entry on `page.projects`'s own node, reusing the mutation's own existing `project:update` permission, nothing new granted). `Dialog`-based "New Project" (via `useAsyncAction`/`useFormValidation`, their first real adoption anywhere) replaces the old inline toggle-form. Member management/Comments/Documents — previously cluttering every list row via per-row expand toggles — now live entirely on the new `/workspace/projects/[id]` detail page (Overview/Tasks/Documents/Comments tabs, a metadata rail with owner/department/task-and-document counts/members) — a genuinely better information hierarchy, not a relocation for its own sake. `TaskList.tsx` got a minimal, additive touch only (task titles now link to `/workspace/tasks/[id]`) — its own full redesign is explicitly Phase 1's job, deliberately not pulled forward into Phase 0's scope.
- **Dashboard gained a small, disclosed hero-band touch**: `Heading` gained an additive `subtitle` prop (a static, interpolated one-liner, not a live KPI summary — KPI values are independently bound to separate `KpiCard` nodes with no single place already holding all of them, a real simplification not worth a bigger refactor for one line of text). `KpiCard` gained a hover-elevation treatment reusing the existing `--shadow-*`/`--duration-*` tokens — the color palette itself is deliberately not being redone in this initiative.
- **13 new backend tests, 591/591 passing (up from 579)**: `project.detail`/`task.detail`'s not-found/scope-reuse/capability-flag behavior, mirroring `document.detail`'s own established spec shape. **13 new frontend tests, 90/90 passing (up from 81)**: `Table3`'s new `onRowClick` precedence behavior (4 tests), `Avatar`'s initials-computation logic (5 tests, covering two-word/single-word/three-word/empty-name cases), plus the pre-existing Phase F test count. Full monorepo typecheck/lint/production-build clean on both sides — the new routes correctly appear in the Next.js build's own route table.
- **Fully live-verified against a real, throwaway IT tenant**: `page.projects`'s `project.update` action confirmed present after a re-seed; `project.detail`/`task.detail` both confirmed returning full, correctly-shaped data (owner/department names resolved, capability flags computed correctly) for a real record, and a clean 404 for a nonexistent one; `project.update` (the Kanban drag mutation) confirmed to actually persist a status change, with `project.detail`'s own `taskCount` confirmed to update live after a real `task.create` against that project; both new frontend routes (`/workspace/projects/[id]`, `/workspace/tasks/[id]`) confirmed serving valid, correctly-chunked HTML with no server crash — deliberately checked for the documented stale-`.next`-cache-corruption footgun (a prior production build immediately preceding this `next dev` start), not just hoped against. Throwaway tenant and its Supabase Auth user fully deleted afterward, both dev servers stopped cleanly.
- **Explicitly disclosed, same no-browser-automation gap as every UI phase before this one**: the actual drag-and-drop feel, the Board/Table/List switcher's visual polish, and the new detail pages' real rendered layout all need the user's own browser review — every API-level round trip is proven, the pixel-level result is not.

#### Session 2 continued — Frontend Structural Redesign, Phase 1: Tasks + Documents (complete)

Same research (one Explore pass re-reading `TaskList.tsx`, `DocumentsPanel.tsx`, `documents.data-sources.ts`, `seed.ts`'s `page.tasks` node, `TaskDetail.tsx`, and `tasks.mutations.ts` in full) → file-level plan → approval → implementation → test → live-verify → document cycle as Phase 0.

Wired to the already-existing `task.updateStatus` mutation — **zero `seed.ts` change needed this time**: confirmed by direct research that `page.tasks`'s blueprint node already carried every action Phase 1 needed (`task.create`/`task.updateStatus`/`task.reassign`), unlike Phase 0's Projects work, which needed one small additive action-list entry. `Dialog`-based "New Task" (via `useAsyncAction`/`useFormValidation`) replaces the old inline five-field row.
- **The per-row Comments toggle, reassign `Dropdown`, and status `Select` were all removed from every Tasks list view** — `TaskDetail.tsx` (built read-only in Phase 0) now owns real edit capability instead: a status `Select` and a reassign `Dropdown`, both gated on the task's own existing `canUpdate` flag. **Zero new backend capability flag needed for Tasks** — confirmed both `task.updateStatus` and `task.reassign` already share the identical `task:update` permission, which `task.detail`'s `canUpdate` (built in Phase 0) already computes. Same "list rows show state, the detail page is where you change it" consistency Projects' own Phase 0 work established; Board-view dragging stays the one shared exception.
- **Documents gained its first-ever dedicated page**: `/workspace/documents/[id]` (new route, new `DocumentDetail.tsx` — Overview/Versions/Activity/Comments tabs), built entirely on `document.detail`, a data source that **already existed before this whole initiative began** (predates Phase 0). One small, disclosed backend extension: `document.detail` gained `canUpdate`/`canDelete` capability flags — the exact same `ctx.effective.has(...)` pattern `project.detail`/`task.detail` already established, since a hand-written route has no pruned `actions` array to read capability from.
- **`DocumentsPanel.tsx` (mounted inline inside `ProjectDetail`'s own Documents tab) had its per-row expand-toggle removed** — it revealed activity history (a local `DocumentActivityFeed` component) + comments inline, both now redundant with the new detail page's own tabs, and doubly so since `DocumentsPanel` was already nested one level inside a detail page. Replaced with a small "view details" `next/link` icon per row. **Everything else stays completely unchanged** — search, upload, click-to-open-file/download, replace, approval-status `Select`, delete, and the per-document AI summarize/Q&A buttons — confirmed as genuinely fast, primary actions worth keeping inline, not detail-page-only.
- **A real, correct follow-on cleanup, not scope creep**: removing the expand-toggle also retired `DocumentsPanel`'s now-fully-unused `mentionCandidates` prop (previously only feeding the removed inline `CommentThread`) — removed from its signature and both call sites (`ProjectDetail.tsx`, `PatientsWorkspace.tsx`), confirmed via typecheck that nothing else depended on it.
- **1 new backend test, 592/592 passing (up from 591)**: `document.detail`'s new capability-flag computation, mirroring the exact test shape Phase 0 established for `project.detail`/`task.detail`. **Zero new frontend tests** — `TaskDetail`'s new status/reassign controls and `DocumentDetail` are thin wrappers over already-tested mutations/already-existing data, matching this codebase's own "no dedicated test for thin UI wrappers over already-tested logic" precedent (`Checkbox`/`Radio`/`Switch` never got one either); frontend suite stays 90/90. Full monorepo typecheck/lint/production-build clean on both sides — both routes confirmed in the Next.js build's own route table.
- **Fully live-verified against a real, throwaway IT tenant, including a genuine end-to-end file upload** (not simulated): `document.createUploadUrl` → a real signed `PUT` to Supabase Storage → `document.create` → `document.detail` confirmed returning `canUpdate: true, canDelete: true` for the full-permission actor alongside the real uploaded file's metadata and a real "uploaded" activity-log entry; `document.detail` still 404s cleanly for a nonexistent id. `task.updateStatus`/`task.reassign` both confirmed still correct when called the way `TaskDetail`'s new controls call them. Both routes served valid, correctly-chunked HTML with no crash and no stale-`.next`-cache corruption (same footgun deliberately checked for after this phase's own production build, not just hoped against). Throwaway tenant, its Supabase Auth user, and the uploaded test Storage object all fully deleted afterward; both dev servers stopped cleanly.

#### Session 2 continued — Visual Polish & Consistency Pass (complete)

- The user asked to check widget/button alignment and revisit the color theme toward "multicolor and interactive." Rather than assume, I asked via `AskUserQuestion` — the actual answer was more restrained than any of the three options offered: a **premium enterprise feel** (Ramp/Linear/Notion/Asana/ClickUp) with neutral backgrounds, subtle shadows, generous spacing, and color reserved for status/priority/charts/important actions, explicitly **not** a colorful/flashy reskin.
- **A cross-cutting consistency pass, not a new Frontend Structural Redesign phase** — Phase 2 (Calendar + Meetings + Attendance) stays queued, unaffected. Scoped from a direct code audit (an Explore-agent pass), not assumption: the base warm-neutral token system, `Badge`'s 6-tone vocabulary, and chart-color usage were already correctly restrained — the real gaps were duplication/drift and partially-applied patterns, not a wrong direction.
- **Shared `chart-colors.ts`**: `Chart`/`Treemap`/`Funnel` each independently redeclared an identical 5-color series array — now one exported `CHART_SERIES_COLORS` constant.
- **Hover elevation extended to the 6 chart-family widgets that were missing it** (`Chart`, `Treemap`, `Funnel`, `Heatmap`, `CalendarHeatmap`, `Leaderboard`) — the exact `hover:shadow-md`/`transition-shadow` class already proven on `KpiCard`/`ProgressGoal`, not a new pattern.
- **Every stray hardcoded `duration-150` migrated to the real `duration-[var(--duration-fast)]` token.** The plan's own initial audit estimated ~5 files; a full-codebase grep during implementation found 46 occurrences across 20 files — disclosed to the user mid-implementation rather than silently expanding scope, and proceeded since the approved plan's own wording ("migrate every stray hardcoded duration-150") already covered it.
- **`Avatar`'s size classes exported as `AVATAR_SIZE_CLASSES`**, reused by `ProjectBoard`'s "+N more" member-overflow chip instead of a hand-copied pixel-value literal that could silently drift from `Avatar`'s own sizing.
- **`shrink-0` added to badge groups** in `ProjectBoard`'s and `TaskList`'s List-view rows, so a long title (already `truncate`d) is the only thing that absorbs space pressure at narrow widths — badges no longer compress/wrap inconsistently.
- **New shared `SectionLabel` component** (`apps/web/src/ui/SectionLabel.tsx`) replacing 5 identical duplicated literal occurrences of the same class string in `RolesPermissionsWorkspace.tsx` — pure de-duplication, byte-identical rendered output.
- **`Leaderboard`'s row padding bumped** (`px-2 py-1.5` → `px-2.5 py-2`) to match the more generous internal spacing every sibling widget (`KpiCard`, `ProgressGoal`) already uses — the one confirmed spacing outlier, not a blanket spacing rewrite.
- **`ViewSwitcher`'s toggle buttons gained an explicit `h-8`**, matching `Button`'s own `sm` size exactly, guaranteeing pixel-identical height if ever composed in the same row.
- **`KpiCard` gained an optional `tone` prop** (`neutral`/`success`/`warning`/`danger`/`info`/`accent`, reusing `Badge`'s exact same vocabulary and CSS variables) rendering a subtle `border-l-2` left-border accent when set; omitted, it renders byte-for-byte identical to before. Applied to Dashboard's 3 KPI nodes in `seed.ts`: `k1` Active Projects → `accent`, `k2` My Open Tasks → `info`, `k3` Overdue Tasks → `danger`. New `KpiCard.test.tsx` confirms the accent class only appears when `tone` is passed.
- **Deliberately out of scope, disclosed not silently dropped**: base color token values, a blanket spacing rewrite across the app, and a new formal typography-scale component — all confirmed already correct/consistent in practice by the audit, not overlooked.
- **Full monorepo typecheck/lint/test/build all green** (592 backend tests, 92 frontend tests including the 2 new `KpiCard` tests) — 0 lint errors, only pre-existing unrelated warnings. Compiled CSS bundle grepped directly to confirm every new/changed class (`border-l-danger`/`border-l-info`/`border-l-accent`, `border-l-2`, `duration-\[var\(--duration-fast\)\]`, `hover\:shadow-md`, `h-8`) actually compiled, and that `duration-150` is fully gone from the bundle.
- **Explicitly disclosed, same no-browser-automation gap as every visual phase**: whether the hover elevation feels right on charts and whether the KPI tone accent reads as intended in a real browser still need the user's own review. No data source, mutation, or RBAC touched — nothing needed a live-tenant round-trip this pass.

#### Session 2 continued — Education Domain, Phase A: Foundation (complete) — the platform's second non-IT industry blueprint

- The user directed replicating Healthcare Domain's success for three more industries (Education, Finance, Manufacturing), keeping only the modules each domain needs and adding only the new modules required to make it genuinely usable. Given the scale, confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`: build **one domain at a time, full cycle** (mirroring Healthcare's own Foundation → Calendar → Analytics → domain-UI/AI phasing), with **deeper functionality than Healthcare's original MVP bar** — real grading/gradebook for Education specifically (Finance/Manufacturing not started). Researched via 3 parallel Explore agents (backend module template, frontend Pattern A vs Pattern B, supporting engine mechanics) + 1 Plan agent, then a full research → architecture plan → `ExitPlanMode` approval cycle before any code, same discipline as every major initiative this project has used.
- **5 new Prisma models** (`Student`, `Course`, `Enrollment`, `Assignment`, `Grade` — vs. Healthcare's 2), confirming the engine's own claim of being 100% generic over industry still holds even for a materially richer domain. `Course`, not `Student`, plays Patient's "owns a chart Project" role (`materialsProjectId`, 1:1 auto-created on `course.create`) — a deliberate, disclosed asymmetry: shared course materials (syllabus/readings/handouts) are universally useful and non-sensitive, while a per-Student individual document file edges into student-record-privacy territory better deferred. `Enrollment` is the Student×Course join (`ProjectMember` only accepts real Users, and Student has none, same reasoning that makes `Appointment` its own model). `Assignment` deliberately does not reuse `Task` — no `maxScore`/gradebook fields, avoiding leaking Education-specific concerns into every other industry's Task model.
- **The one real design problem, and its resolution**: `Grade.gradedById` is null until first graded, so it can't be the sole scope-check field the way `Appointment.doctorId`-style fields are — mirroring `appointment.updateStatus` literally would make it impossible for a Teacher to ever enter a first grade. Resolved by splitting into `grade.record` (first entry — scope resolved *transitively* through a new exported `teacherOwnedCourseIds(tx, ctx)` helper in `courses.data-sources.ts`, walking Course → Assignment → Grade) and `grade.update` (correcting an already-graded row — now the literal `isRowInScope`-on-`gradedById` mirror of `appointment.updateStatus` is correct, since `gradedById` is guaranteed set by then). `teacherOwnedCourseIds` is a plain ownership query, deliberately never routed through `coursesWhere` (which answers "which courses can I *see*," not "which do I *teach*") — conflating the two would have silently widened every Teacher's `:own` floor to the whole tenant, defeating scope entirely. The frontend gradebook (`AssignmentsGradebookTab.tsx`) dispatches `grade.record` vs `grade.update` per cell based on `grades.list`'s own `gradeId: string | null` field.
- **4 flat roles** (School Administrator = `role.admin`, Teacher, Teaching Assistant, Registrar — no `extends` chains, mirroring Healthcare's exact precedent): Teacher gets `course:read:tenant` + `course:update:own` (own-scoped courses) and the real gradebook authority (`assignment:*:own`, `grade:*:own`, both resolved via the transitive chain); Teaching Assistant gets broader read (`assignment:read:tenant`, `grade:read:tenant`) with zero create/update — "sees more, originates nothing"; Registrar owns the Student/Enrollment lifecycle tenant-wide with zero assignment/grade access and is never a course's `ProjectMember` — the concrete "structurally excluded from the gradebook" proof, mirroring Receptionist's own precedent. Navigation drops Projects/Tasks/Announcements/Team-Management/HR-group/Workspace-Admin-group and keeps the same core-tooling tail Healthcare kept (Chat/Meetings/Calendar/Attendance/Analytics/Roles & Permissions/Settings/Account).
- **Frontend built on Pattern B** (the "Frontend Structural Redesign" direction — `PageHeader`+`ViewSwitcher` list pages, dedicated `DetailPageShell`-based `/workspace/<module>/[id]` detail routes, `*.detail` data sources), not the older Healthcare-era inline-workspace pattern — confirmed via research that this is the codebase's current direction and that only list-page composites need SDUI registration. `CourseDetail`'s Materials/Discussion tabs reuse `DocumentsPanel`/`CommentThread` with **zero backend/type changes** (`entityType: "project"`, `entityId: course.materialsProjectId` — the same mechanism `ProjectDetail` already uses). The gradebook UI (`AssignmentsGradebookTab.tsx`) is hand-rolled (a nested per-assignment student table doesn't fit `Table3`'s flat-rows contract), with a dedicated test confirming the `grade.record`/`grade.update` dispatch rule.
- **`apps/api/src/auth/signup.schema.ts`**: `industry` enum grew `["IT", "Healthcare"]` → `["IT", "Healthcare", "Education"]`. New migration `20260811140000_education_students_courses_enrollments_assignments_grades` (hand-assembled via `prisma migrate diff --from-config-datasource` against the live DB rather than `migrate dev`, which fails in this environment — documented pre-existing Supabase shadow-database limitation; a spurious unrelated `embeddings` column-drop artifact from the known Prisma+pgvector `Unsupported("vector")` diffing bug was manually excluded) — every new table ships its own `tenant_isolation` RLS policy in the same migration, same discipline every table in this codebase follows.
- **80 new backend tests** (672/672 total, up from 592) covering all 5 modules' data-sources/mutations, including a regression guard proving `teacherOwnedCourseIds` never widens via `coursesWhere`'s tenant-wide branch, and the `grade.record`/`grade.update` pair proving the split-mutation design end-to-end. **2 new frontend tests** (94/94 total) for the gradebook dispatch rule — everything else follows this codebase's own established "no dedicated test for thin UI wrappers" precedent (zero test files exist for `ProjectBoard`/`ProjectDetail`/`TaskList`/`TaskDetail` either).
- **Fully live-verified against a real signup** (`industry: "Education"`): Admin's full happy path (course → real `materialsProjectId`/`Project` creation → student register → enroll → assignment create → grade record/update); Teacher's `course:read:tenant` (sees both courses) vs `course:update:own` (403 on another teacher's course); the assignment/grade transitive chain (`assignment.create`/`grade.record` succeed on own course, 403 on another's); **the definitive proof of the split-mutation design** — Teacher1 corrects their own just-recorded grade successfully, a second Teacher account gets 403 attempting the same; Teaching Assistant's broad read (`grades.list` succeeds) with zero write capability (`grade.record`/`assignment.create` both 403, permission absent entirely); Registrar's full Student/Enrollment CRUD tenant-wide with `course.create`/`assignment.create`/`documents.list` on a course's materials all 403 — the "structurally excluded" proof. Regression-checked: fresh IT and Healthcare signups both still work end-to-end (correct nav, `project.create`/`patient.register` still succeed) — zero cross-blueprint interference. A real, disclosed mid-verification finding: `user.invite`'s `departmentId` field is required even for blueprints with no department taxonomy (`departmentTypes: []`) — worked around by creating one plain `Department` row first via the existing `department.create`, not a product bug, same category of pre-existing-behavior-surfaced-by-testing as Healthcare Phase C's own `mustChangePassword` finding.
- Also fully live-verified: production build compiles cleanly, both new detail routes (`/workspace/students/[id]`, `/workspace/courses/[id]`) serve valid HTML with correctly-loading CSS.

#### Next up
- **Education Domain Phase B (Calendar integration), Phase C (Analytics), and Phase D (UI polish + AI) are next** for this initiative, each its own approval-gated cycle mirroring Healthcare's own phasing — then **Finance Domain** and **Manufacturing Domain** (both scoped at a high level, neither started) follow as their own full multi-phase initiatives, one at a time, per the user's own explicit sequencing direction.
- **Frontend Structural Redesign, Phase 2 (Calendar + Meetings + Attendance) is next** — needs its own dedicated research → plan → approval cycle. The API dev server used for this session's live verification was stopped cleanly afterward.

- **The confirmed Realtime INSERT-delivery limitation (Chat, Submodule 2) applies to any future table that wants push-based updates** — Presence & Status (whenever it's picked up) will need its own fresh investigation into whether Presence (a different Realtime feature, not `postgres_changes`) is affected the same way.

- **The external-I/O-inside-a-transaction mechanism (`preResolve`/`rollbackPreResolve`) has a second real precedent to point to now** (Calendar's reminder poller does the equivalent outside any mutation transaction instead, same discipline, different trigger point) — still unused by any live mutation, real infrastructure for whenever one needs it.
- **A real, reproducible papercut flagged for whenever it's next hit**: `role.updateCustom` 400s on resubmitting a role's own unchanged `permissions` array if that array contains `task:delete:*` (excluded from `PERMISSION_CATALOG`) — see Delegation's own entry above for the full explanation.
- **⚠️ Standing rules to keep applying**: never `Promise.all` multiple queries against a shared `tx` inside one resolver; the documentation-sync practice — check it before calling any future stage complete; action-level permission pruning requires `requiredPermission` on the blueprint `ActionSpec` itself, not just on the mutation/data-source definition; before committing to any infrastructure requiring Docker (or similar), confirm it's actually installed before designing around it; when documentation makes a claim about live behavior, verify it empirically before designing around it; and now also — **a controller-level `.parse()` call needs its own `ZodError` → `BadRequestException` catch, per-controller — it is not handled globally anywhere in this app** (the exact gap Calendar's own verification found and fixed in `MutationsController`/`DataSourcesController`; any *new* controller added later needs the same treatment, it won't come for free).
